<meta>This page continues the philosophical defense of the "Five Ritual Elements," specifically addressing the fifth element—sexual union—by reframing it as a sacred reflection of cosmic creation. It transitions from the previous page's warning against ritual abuse to a theological explanation of gender and divinity.</meta>

<page-num>22</page-num>

<header>HYMN TO KÂLÎ</header>

great sinner. Shiva has also said in the *<term>Ocean of Kula Tradition<note>original: Kulârnava</note></term>*, ‘One reaches heaven by the very things which may lead to Hell.’ The fifth <term>letter M<note>original: Ma-kâra; referring to Maithuna or sexual union, the fifth of the five 'M' rituals</note></term>, that is, sexual intercourse, is the root-cause of the creation of the world of <term>living beings<note>original: Jîvas</note></term>. All living beings, be they <term>deities<note>original: Devatâs</note></term>, men, beasts, birds, fish, insects or flies, are produced by the sexual union of their respective parents. In this world every male is an <term>individualized<note>original: Vyashtîbhûta</note></term> aspect of Shiva, the <term>Original Person<note>original: Âdipurusha</note></term>, and the <term>Goddess Chandi<note>original: Chandî; the fierce form of the Goddess described in the Devi Mahatmya</note></term> says, ‘all females in all the worlds’ are part of the <term>Great Power<note>original: Mahâshakti</note></term>. The <term>Tortoise Chronicle<note>original: Kûrma Purâna; one of the eighteen major ancient Hindu texts</note></term> says, ‘The <term>Great Goddess<note>original: Mahâdevî</note></term> is Herself One, present in <term>many parts or divisions<note>original: Anekavibhâgasthâ</note></term>, beyond <term>illusion<note>original: Mâyâ</note></term>, absolutely pure, <term>Great Illusionist<note>original: Mahâmâyâ</note></term>, <term>Supreme Sovereign<note>original: Ishvarî</note></term>, eternal, <term>stainless<note>original: Niranjana</note></term>, ancient, <term>full of consciousness<note>original: Chinmayî</note></term>, the <term>First Person<note>original: Âdipurusha</note></term> of all persons.’ The <term>Celestial Musician Scripture<note>original: Gandharva Tantra</note></term> says, ‘The <term>male form<note>original: Pungsho rûpam</note></term>, the female form, and any other good form—all this is undoubtedly Her <term>supreme form<note>original: Paramam rûpam</note></term>.’ One <term>Absolute Reality<note>original: Brahman</note></term>, becoming dual, appears as Shiva and <term>Power<note>original: Shakti</note></term>, and that aspect in which there is union of Shiva and Power is the true aspect of the <term>Existence-Consciousness-Bliss<note>original: Sachchidânanda</note></term> Reality. It is from this aspect of <term>Blissful<note>original: Ânandamaya</note></term> union that the world is created, and for that reason men and all other creatures ever seek happiness. The Bliss of the reproductive power of males and females manifests in their bodies only at the time of sexual union. At this time ignorant men remain intent only on gratifying their passion, but <term>spiritual practitioners<note>original: Sâdhakas</note></term>, possessed of the knowledge of the <term>Kula tradition<note>original: Kula</note></term>, then meditate on the <term>Yoga-blissful<note>original: Yogânanda</note></term> <term>form<note>original: Mûrtti</note></term> of Shiva and Power present in the hearts of males and females and, calling to mind the <term>meaning<note>original: Artha</note></term> of the <term>sacred chant<note>original: Mantra</note></term> of their <term>chosen deity<note>original: Ishtadevatâ</note></term>, perform <term>meditative repetition<note>original: Japa</note></term> of it. In the <term>All-encompassing Essence of the Kali Tradition<note>original: Kâlikulasarvasva</note></term>, <term>Lord Eternal Shiva<note>original: Shrî Sadâshiva</note></term> says, ‘By doing meditative repetition of the sacred chant and by adoration of the <term>Divine Mother<note>original: Bhagavatî</note></term>, the consort of Shiva, at times of sexual union, a man becomes, like <term>Shuka<note>a legendary sage known for his total purity and detachment</note></term>, free from all sins.’ In another place He says, ‘The consort of Shiva should be worshipped by becoming Shiva.’ True <term>practice centered on the Goddess<note>original: Shakti-sâdhanâ</note></term> consists in considering all girls and women, old and young, and of all castes, as the visible forms

<summary>This page explains the Tantric view of sexuality as a sacred mirror of cosmic creation, where human union reflects the divine union of Shiva and Shakti. It argues that for the enlightened practitioner, these acts are not for mere pleasure but are a form of meditation that cleanses sin and recognizes the Goddess in all women.</summary>

<header>INTRODUCTION</header>

of one’s own <term>Chosen Deity<note>original: Ishtadevatâ; the specific form of the Divine an individual worshipper selects for devotion</note></term> and (according to one’s means) worshipping them with clothes, ornaments and so forth; or bowing to them as mothers with the <term>Personal Mantra<note>original: Ishtamantra; the sacred sound-syllable given by a guru for one's chosen deity</note></term> in mind and not treating them with neglect or contempt under any circumstance. In the *<term>Garland of the Kaula Sect<note>original: Kaulâvalî Tantra</note></term>*, the god <term>Shiva<note>The Auspicious One</note></term> says, ‘One should make obeisance on seeing a young woman of a <term>Kaula family<note>original: Kaula; an ancient lineage within Tantra focusing on the union of Shiva and Shakti</note></term>. One should bow to any female, be she a young girl, or flushed with youth, or be she old, be she beautiful or ugly, good, or wicked. One should never deceive, speak ill of, or do ill to, a woman and one should never strike her. All such acts prevent the attainment of <term>spiritual perfection<note>original: Siddhi; the success or power resulting from ritual practice</note></term>.’

At the present time a measured use of wine, flesh and so forth and a thorough respect for woman as for the <term>Deity<note>original: Devatâ</note></term> are particularly seen in the civilized society of the West. <note>The author here presents a historical perspective common in early 20th-century Indian scholarship, attributing the scientific and political success of Western nations to their supposed adherence to certain Tantric principles, specifically the respect for women.</note> Satisfied at this, the <term>Great Goddess<note>original: Mahâdevî</note></term>, who is the Queen of Queens, has granted to the people of the West the light of science and sovereignty over the whole world. The <term>Primordial Lord, Great Time<note>original: Shrîmat Âdinâtha Mahâkâla; Shiva in his aspect as the ultimate witness of time</note></term> has, in the ‘<term>Hymn to the Camphor-Clad Goddess<note>original: Karpûrâdi Stotra; a primary liturgical poem for the worship of Kali</note></term>’ called the <term>Hymn of the Essential Form<note>original: Svarûpa-Stotra</note></term>, briefly described the <term>Sacred Mantra, Ritual Geometry, Meditation, and Spiritual Practice<note>original: Mantra, Yantra, Dhyâna, Sâdhanâ</note></term> of <term>The Benevolent Kali of the South<note>original: Shrîmatî Dakshinâ-Kâlikâ</note></term> who is the <term>Supreme Reality<note>original: Parabrahman; the ultimate, formless ground of being</note></term> <insert>manifesting in the form of the Supreme Reality</insert>. This <term>Supreme Essence<note>original: Tattva</note></term> is hard to attain even by such <term>Divine Lords<note>original: Îshvaras</note></term> as <term>Brahma the Creator, Vishnu the Preserver, and Rudra the Destroyer<note>original: Brahmâ, Vishnu, and Rudra</note></term>. <term>Great Time<note>original: Mahâkâla</note></term> Himself says, ‘Neither the <term>Creator<note>original: Dhâtâ</note></term> nor the <term>Lord<note>original: Îsha</note></term> nor the <term>Preserver<note>original: Hari</note></term> knows Your Supreme Essence.’

However, in accordance with the teachings of my <term>Supreme Teacher<note>original: Paramaguru; the teacher of one's own teacher</note></term>, the <term>Greatest of Scholars<note>original: Mahâmahopâdhyâya</note></term> and most worshipful Ramananda Svami Siddhantapanchanana, the crest-gem of <term>Tantric practitioners<note>original: Tântrikas</note></term>, now gathered to the feet of Shiva, I write this commentary on the Essential Form under the name of ‘<term>She Who Bestows Pure Bliss<note>original: Vimalânandadâyinî</note></term>,’ of this *Hymn to the Camphor-Clad Goddess*, in consonance with the views of <term>Tantra<note>The body of esoteric scriptures</note></term> and other <term>Sacred Books<note>original: Shâstras</note></term>.

Whose Body is imagined to be blue of colour,
Because like the blue sky She pervades the World,
And because She is <term>Massive Pure Consciousness<note>original: Chidghanâ; from *Chit* (Consciousness) and *Ghana* (compact mass or cloud)</note></term> <term>composed of the quality of purity<note>original: Sattvaguṇamayî; consisting of *Sattva*, the quality of light, harmony, and goodness</note></term>

Because though Herself changeless She binds infinite numbers

hair and because She makes liberated (<term>Free<note>original: Mukta</note></term>) <term>the Creator<note>original: Brahmâ</note></term>,
<term>the Preserver<note>original: Vishnu</note></term> and <term>the Great Lord<note>original: Maheshvara</note></term> who are <term>Hair<note>original: Kesha; here used as a mystical code for the Trinity</note></term>,²
Who is imagined as having the Sun, Moon and Fire as Her
three eyes,
Because as the <term>Cosmic Manifestation<note>original: Virât</note></term>, the Witness of the world past, present and
future She sees everything,
Who is pictured as wearing the dead bodies of two boys as Her
ear-ornaments,

being the sole <term>Female Creator<note>original: Creatrix</note></term>, Preserver and Destructress of
infinite millions of Worlds, has on Her Body the mark of
the <term>Source<note>original: Yoni; the womb or vulva, symbolizing the origin of all existence</note></term> signifying creation, full and high breasts denoting 

---

¹ This is a play on the word <term>Mass<note>original: Ghana</note></term> which means mass and black or dark blue cloud. <term>Massive Consciousness<note>original: Chidghana</note></term> is massive, compact, unmixed, pure Consciousness (<term>Awareness<note>original: Chit</note></term>). Again She is <term>Beyond Qualities<note>original: Nirguna</note></term> and stainless but is also <term>Cloud-bodied<note>original: Meghânggî</note></term> because through <term>Superimposition<note>original: Adhyâsa</note></term> of the three <term>Qualities<note>original: Gunas</note></term> She appears varicoloured just as a cloud in itself colour-less appears white, blue, and so forth by contact with the sun's rays. So the *<term>Ancient Chronicles of the Goddess<note>original: Devî Purâna</note></term>* says, 'Just as the uniform cloud appears as of many colours, so does She too through the instrumentality of the <term>Qualities<note>original: Gunas</note></term>.'

² <term>Hair<note>original: Kesha</note></term> = *K* + *A* + <term>Lord<note>original: Îsha</note></term>. And *K* = Brahmâ, *A* = Vishnu, and <term>Lord<note>original: Îsha</note></term> = <term>The Roarer<note>original: Rudra; a fierce form of Shiva</note></term>. The *<term>Highest Tantra<note>original: Niruttara Tantra</note></term>* says, 'Kâli who is <term>Unobstructed Wisdom<note>original: Aniruddha-sarasvatî</note></term>, is the great desire-granting tree, the sole Cause of Enjoyment and Liberation for Brahmâ, Vishnu and the <term>Great Lord<note>original: Mahesha</note></term>.'

Who is said to have large teeth, and a lolling tongue and to hold in Her hand a cup made of human skull,

Because the <term>Great Goddess of Pure Consciousness<note>original: Chinmayî Mahâdevî</note></term> drinks the wine of delusion arising from the <term>Quality of Darkness and Inertia<note>original: Tamas Guna</note></term> of Her <term>Spiritual Practitioner<note>original: Sâdhaka</note></term> by means of the <term>Quality of Activity dominated by Purity<note>original: Sattva-pradhâna rajoguna</note></term>,¹

¹ White Teeth stand for the white <term>Quality of Purity<note>original: Sattva Guna</note></term>, the red Tongue stands for the red <term>Quality of Activity<note>original: Rajo Guna</note></term> and Delusion is the <term>Quality of Darkness<note>original: Tamo Guna</note></term>. The meaning is, the <term>Goddess of Great Wisdom<note>original: Mahâvidyâ</note></term> is represented with a lolling tongue because She first destroys the practitioner's darkness by increasing his activity, and large teeth because by increasing his purity and suppressing his activity She grants him the state of the <term>Attribute-less Absolute<note>original: Nirguna Brahman</note></term>. In the <term>Meditation on the Goddess Tara<note>original: Dhyâna of Târâ</note></term> it is said, '<term>The Formidable Savior<note>original: Ugratârâ</note></term> Herself destroys the <term>unconscious nature<note>original: Jâdya</note></term> of the three worlds by putting it in her skull-cup.'

² In the eighteenth century work of Kamalakanta called *Delight of the Practitioner* <note>original: Sâdhaka-ranjana</note> it is said: 'Of the <term>Formless Absolute<note>original: Nirâkâra Brahman</note></term>, understand <term>Illusion<note>original: Mâyâ</note></term> to be the <term>Form<note>original: Âkâra</note></term>' (*"Of the formless Brahman, see Maya as the form"*). The <term>Void<note>original: Shûnya</note></term> has no form until encircled by <term>Illusion<note>original: Mâyâ</note></term>.

³ <term>Transformation<note>original: Vikâra</note></term> is also 'change'. She is then in Her changeless aspect.

# HYMN TO KÂLI

Who is pictured as having a waist-chain of human hands
Because hands are the principal instrument of <term>action<note>original: Karma</note></term>
and at the close of a <term>cosmic age<note>original: Kalpa</note></term> all <term>individual souls<note>original: Jîvas</note></term> with their accumulated actions
are merged in the <term>Power of Ignorance<note>original: Avidyâ Shakti</note></term> of the <term>Great Illusionist<note>original: Mahâmâyâ</note></term>,

Who is seen standing on the breast of corpse-like Shiva,
Because the <term>Supreme State<note>original: Paramapada</note></term> and the <term>essential state of being<note>original: Svarûpâvasthâ</note></term>
of the <term>Great Goddess<note>original: Mahâdevî</note></term> (who is one with Shiva) is <term>without attributes<note>original: Nirguna</note></term> and <term>unchanging<note>original: Nirvikâra</note></term>,

Who is seen in <term>the reverse sexual position<note>original: Viparîta-maithuna</note></term>¹ with <term>Great Time<note>original: Mahâkâla</note></term>,
Because at the beginning of a cosmic age She who is <term>composed of eternal bliss<note>original: Nityânandamayî</note></term>, and being united with Shiva, feels
pleasure in the work of creation which She effects by
bringing the changeless <term>Supreme Shiva<note>original: Parashiva</note></term> under Her <term>sovereign control<note>original: Vashîbhûta</note></term>,

Who is again said to live in the cremation ground,
Because when at the end of a cosmic age all things in the universe
from the creator <term>Brahmâ</term> to a blade of grass are dissolved in <term>Great Time<note>original: Mahâkâla</note></term>,
She is in and one with that Great Time, who may be thus
compared to a cremation ground, and because at the death
of living beings She exists as the <term>individualized soul<note>original: Vyashti Jîvâtmâ</note></term> in
the burning ground,

Whose <term>sacred geometric diagram<note>original: Yantra</note></term> for worship is composed of a circle symbolizing

<term>material nature<note>original: Prakriti</note></term>, three Pentagons representing the fifteen <term>parts or limbs<note>original: Avayavas</note></term>
and a <term>central point<note>original: Bindu</note></term> denoting the union of Shiva and Shakti,

Because She is, as the <term>Supreme Soul<note>original: Paramâtmâ</note></term>, in the physical and subtle bodies
consisting of the three <term>fundamental qualities<note>original: Gunas</note></term> and twenty-four <term>principles of existence<note>original: Tattvas</note></term>,

Whose <term>seed-syllable<note>original: Bîja</note></term> ‘Krîm’,² the Queen of sacred chants is the <term>pure quality of light and goodness<note>original: Sattva Guna</note></term>, and <term>full of consciousness<note>original: Chaitanyamayî</note></term> and grants both
worldly enjoyment and spiritual liberation,

¹ Sexual union in which the woman assumes the dominant role. In this theological context, the Goddess (Shakti) is the active power and Shiva is the passive principle.

<meta>This page begins the translation of the 'Karpūrādistotra', a foundational tantric hymn to the goddess Kali. The text focuses on the symbolic construction of her 'seed-syllable' (mantra) and her various theological aspects.</meta>

# Hymn to Kali

## (Hymn Beginning with the Word "Camphor")

->1<-

O MOTHER¹ and Spouse of the Destroyer of the Three Cities,² they who thrice recite³ Thy **Seed-syllable**⁴ formed by omitting from "Camphor" (*Karpūra*), the middle and last consonants and the vowels, but— <note>The text describes a coded method for constructing the sacred mantra 'Krīm' by manipulating the sounds in the word for camphor.</note>

## NOTES

¹ The Divine Mother of the World in Her aspect as the Beneficent Kali of the South (*Dakshinakālikā*), who is the compassionate granter of Ultimate Liberation (*Nirvāna*).

The *Heart of Kali* (*Kalikahridaya*) says: ‘I worship Kali the Destructress of Time and Death (*Kāla*), the Shining One, who is the Seed-syllable *Krīm*, who is Desire and Will (*Kāma*), who is beyond the parts of Time (*Kala*), and who is the Beneficent Kali of the South (*Dakshinakālikā*).’ The *Gandharva Tantra* says: ‘*Hrīm* <note>A seed-syllable associated with the heart and the goddess</note>, I bow to the Great Goddess (*Mahādevī*) who is the Fourth State of Consciousness (*Turīya*) and the Absolute Reality (*Brahman*). He who remembers Her does not sink in the ocean of worldly existence.’ The *Chandi* <note>A famous text praising the Goddess</note> says: ‘Oh Thou whose Body is pure Wisdom (*Jnāna*), who hast three divine eyes, who weareth the crescent moon, to Thee I bow for the attainment of all good.’ (V)

² The Divine Power (*Shakti*) of the Great Lord (*Mahesha*), who destroyed the Demon (*Asura*) named "He of the Three Cities" (*Tripura*) along with his three fortresses in Heaven, Earth, and the Nether regions. (V). <note>The "Three Cities" symbolize the triple states of experience (waking, dreaming, deep sleep) or the three qualities of nature.</note>

³ Recite (*Japanti*); to utter repeatedly with the mind fixed on the meaning of the sacred sound (*Mantra*). (V). Literally, ‘to perform *Japa*.’ The word ‘recite’ is employed as the nearest English equivalent, but is not entirely accurate, in so far as in **mental repetition** (*mānasa Japa*) the action is purely internal, and in the next lower degree of **whispered repetition** (*Upāngshu*), there is movement of the lips only, but no audible sound.

⁴ The ‘seed’ syllable (*Bīja*). The seed is the cause or essence of the body of the Mantra. (V). According to the *Nitya Tantra*, sacred sounds are of four kinds based on the number of syllables: **The Monosyllable** (*Pinda*), **The Two-Syllabled** (*Kartarī*), **The Seed** (*Bīja*), and **The Garland** (*Mālā*). For more on the "Seed," see Arthur Avalon’s ‘Garland of Letters’. <note>Arthur Avalon was the pseudonym of Sir John Woodroffe, the primary translator and scholar of this text.</note>

<summary>This page introduces the 'Hymn to Kali' and explains the esoteric construction of her primary mantra, 'Krīm', while defining her role as the spouse of Shiva and the granter of liberation.</summary>

<keywords>Kali, Karpuradi Stotra, Seed-syllable, Mantra, Dakshinakālikā, Japa, Shiva, Shakti, Tantra, Arthur Avalon</keywords>

--- Page 56 ---
<meta>This page continues the translation of the first verse of the 'Hymn to Kali' (Karpūrādistotra), specifically completing the coded construction of the goddess's seed-syllable (mantra) and beginning the commentary by Vimalānanda.</meta>

<page-num>30</page-num>
# HYMN TO KĀLĪ

adding **the left eye** (*Vāmakshī*) and the **nasal point** (*Bindu*),¹ the speech of such worshippers, whether in poetry or prose, becomes like that of men who have attained all spiritual powers.² Their words issue with certainty and ease from the hollow of their mouths, O Thou who art beauteous with the beauty of a dark rain cloud.³

->I<-

## COMMENTARY

->(INNER SENSE)<-

*With respectful obeisance to the beautiful feet of the Master Rāmānanda, I write this "Exposition of the Essential Nature" (Svarūpa-vyākhyā) named "The Grantor of Pure Bliss" (Vimalānandadāyinī).* ⁴

<margin>‘Oh Mother’ (*Mātah*)</margin> This word is derived from the root *Mâ*, meaning "to measure," to which is added a suffix to form *Mâtri* (Mother). That is, She is the one who "measures out" or gives. She grants either worldly enjoyment or spiritual Liberation (*Moksha*) according to whether the seeker <note>Sādhaka: a practitioner of spiritual disciplines.</note> is filled with worldly desires or is free from them.

---

¹ This note explains the secret construction of the mantra *Krīm*. By taking the word for camphor (*Karpūram*) and removing specific letters, one is left with the sounds *K* and *r*. To this is added *Vāmakshī* (literally "the left eye," which is a code for the long vowel *ī*) and the *Nāda-bindu* (the dot and crescent moon symbol representing the resonance of the universe). This forms the syllable **Krīm**, which the *Essence of Tantra* (*Tantrasāra*) says fulfills all desires, and which the *Secret of the Dark Goddess* (*Shyāmārahasya Tantra*) calls the "King of Mantras." The *King of Tantras* (*Tantra-rāja*) also states that the letter *Ka* is the very form of the Goddess.

² *Siddhi*, or success. *Siddhi* is that which is sought (*Sādhya*) through spiritual training (*Sādhana*)—the development of higher psychical and spiritual faculties. It includes the eight great powers, such as *Animā* <note>The power to become as small as an atom.</note> and *Laghimā* <note>The power to become weightless.</note>, as well as the power to move or float in space mentioned in the *Skanda Purāna* and other ancient texts. The Goddess herself is called "Great Success" (*Mahāsiddhi*) in the *Litany of a Thousand Names of Lalitā* (v. 55).

³ *Dhvāntadhārādhararuchiruchire* <note>original: "O Thou who art as beautiful as a dark rain-bearing cloud."</note>. Just as dark clouds cool the earth parched by the sun by shedding nectar-like rain, so too dost Thou, by shedding the nectar of Thy Grace, give immortality to practitioners tormented by the three forms of pain: *Ādhyātmika* <note>Pain caused by the self, such as illness or mental distress.</note>, *Ādhibhautika* <note>Pain caused by other living beings.</note>, and *Ādhidaivika* <note>Pain caused by supernatural or natural forces, like storms or fate.</note>. The *Rudrayāmala Tantra* says: "The Goddess is the Supreme Power (*Shakti*) and delivers us from all difficulties. She is dark with the radiance of a million suns, yet cooling like a million moons." (V).

⁴ *Vimalānanda* ("Pure Bliss") is also the name of the author of this commentary.

<summary>This page concludes the first verse of the hymn, explaining how the sacred mantra 'Krīm' is constructed and its power to grant eloquence. It also introduces the commentary, defining the word 'Mother' as the one who dispenses either worldly pleasure or spiritual liberation.</summary>
<keywords>Krīm, mantra, Siddhi, Mother, Vimalānanda, spiritual powers, commentary, Tantra, Sanskrit roots</keywords>

--- Page 57 ---
<meta>This page continues the commentary on the first verse of the Hymn to Kali, transitioning from the technical construction of the seed-syllable (Krīm) to its deep philosophical and cosmological significance within the Tantric tradition.</meta>

<page-num>31</page-num>

### COMMENTARY 31

<margin>‘Spouse of the Destroyer of the three cities’</margin>
The "three cities" refer to the three types of bodies: the gross (physical), the subtle (mental/energetic), and the causal (karmic). She is the <term>Shakti<note>The divine feminine power or energy</note></term> of Him (Shiva) who grants Liberation from these bodies. As the Power-holder (<term>Shaktimān<note>The masculine principle who wields the power</note></term>) and His Power (Shakti) are one and the same, it is She who is the grantor of such Liberation. The *Kaivalya Upanishad* <note>An ancient Sanskrit text focused on the state of spiritual isolation and liberation</note> says, ‘From the <term>Self<note>Ātmā</note></term>—the root, the bliss, who looks upon all equally and dwells within the three cities—is born the multiple and various world, and into Him these three cities are eventually merged.’

<margin>‘They who recite’</margin>
This refers to meditating on the Goddess as being one with the <term>Self<note>Ātmā</note></term> of the <term>practitioner<note>Sādhaka</note></term>. The *Kālikā Shruti* <note>A sacred text dedicated to the goddess Kali</note> says, ‘One should always think of the Self as Kāli. Those who do so attain the fourfold <term>goals of life<note>Purushārtha: Dharma (duty), Artha (wealth), Kāma (pleasure), and Moksha (liberation)</note></term>, whether they are directly desired or not.’ The *Todala Tantra* (Chapter 6) says, ‘O Goddess, the letter **K** grants <term>Virtue<note>Dharma</note></term>, **R** grants <term>Desire<note>Kāma</note></term>, **I** grants <term>Wealth<note>Artha</note></term>, and **M** grants <term>Liberation<note>Moksha</note></term>. O Beloved, the recital of these combined gives the ultimate <term>Release<note>Nirvāna Moksha</note></term>.’

<margin>‘This’ (Etat)</margin>
Your aspect of <term>Pure Existence, Consciousness, and Bliss<note>Sattva sacchidānanda</note></term> denoted by the <term>seed-syllable<note>Bīja</note></term> ‘Krīm.’

<margin>‘Triple’ (Trih-kritang)</margin>
That is the triple aspect of nature: <term>Purity<note>Sāttvika</note></term>, <term>Passion<note>Rājasika</note></term>, and <term>Inertia<note>Tāmasika</note></term>.

<margin>‘Seed-Syllable’ (Bīja)</margin>
This denotes the aspect in which You are the Cause of the World. Although in Your true form as <term>Existence-Consciousness-Bliss<note>Sacchidānandarūpinī</note></term> You are <term>beyond all attributes<note>Nirgunā</note></term> when free of <term>Illusion<note>Māyā</note></term> (which is characterized by the <term>actions<note>Karma</note></term> of <term>individual souls<note>Jīvas</note></term> and the passage of <term>Time<note>Kāla</note></term>), You become the seed during the creation of the world, at the time when souls must enjoy the fruits of their past actions. In the *Devīgītā* <note>The 'Song of the Goddess'</note>, the Goddess says: ‘Then I, who am the Self, Consciousness, the <term>Supreme Reality<note>Parabrahman</note></term>, and called the "One," assume the seed aspect through union with My own Power. The causal body of which I have previously spoken is the <term>Unmanifest<note>Avyakta</note></term>, in which the world exists as a seed from which issues the subtle body.’

<margin>‘Camphor’ (Karpūram)</margin>
This refers to <term>God with Attributes<note>Saguna Brahman</note></term>, the <term>Fashioner<note>Kalpaka</note></term> or creator of the World.

<margin>‘Omitting therefrom’</margin>
Omitting from <term>Root-Nature<note>Mūlaprakriti</note></term> (which is composed of the qualities of Purity, Passion, and Inertia) the middle quality of Passion (the sound *Ū*) and the last quality of Inertia (the sound *M*). It is thus composed of the quality of Purity (*Sattva*) alone. The *Jnānasangkhalinī Tantra* says, ‘The sound *A* is Purity, *U* is Passion, and *M* is Inertia. <term>Nature<note>Prakriti</note></term> is these three.’

<summary>This page explains the philosophical symbolism of Kali's mantra 'Krīm,' linking its phonetic components to the three qualities of nature (Gunas) and the goddess's role as the grantor of liberation from the three bodies of man.</summary>
<keywords>Krīm, Shakti, Purushārtha, Gunas, Sattva, Rajas, Tamas, Sādhaka, Bīja, Moksha, Creation, Causal Body</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical and mystical breakdown of the seed-syllables (mantras) dedicated to the Goddess Kālī, shifting from the syllable Krīm to the syllable Hūm, while explaining her dark complexion as a symbol of infinite consciousness.</meta>

# HYMN TO KĀLĪ

<margin>‘Adding’</margin>
She is powerful to grant <term>Ultimate Liberation<note>Nirvâna Moksha</note></term> and, through her <term>Divine Creative Power<note>Mâyâ</note></term>, to grant the worldly desires of <term>spiritual practitioners<note>Sâdhakas</note></term>; she is the one in whom the <term>quality of purity and light<note>Sattvaguna</note></term> is most prominent. The *Tantra Kalpadruma* <note>A ritual manual whose name means "The Wish-Fulfilling Tree of Tantra"</note> states: "The letter **K**, because of its brilliance, represents the <term>Particle of Consciousness<note>Chitkalâ</note></term> or Wisdom (<term>Jnâna</term>). When associated with the fiery letter **R**, She is auspicious and full of all <term>radiant energy<note>Tejas</note></term>. As the vowel **Î**, She grants the desires of practitioners. As the nasal dot (<term>Bindu</term>), She grants <term>Absolute Liberation<note>Kaivalya</note></term>."

<margin>‘Beauty of dark clouds’</margin>
You should be meditated upon as having a dark blue or black (<term>Nîla</term>) color, because You are the <term>Infinite Space of Consciousness<note>Chidâkâsha</note></term> and possess the dense radiance of <term>Purest Harmony<note>Shuddhasattvaguna</note></term>. In the "Section on Liberation" (<term>Nirvâna Prakarana</term>) of the *Yogavāshishtha* <note>A famous philosophical text</note>, it is said: "Because the Goddess (<term>Shivâ</term>) is the <term>Ether<note>Vyoma; the subtlest element</note></term>, she is perceived as black." The *Tripurâsârasamuchchaya* <note>A compendium of the essence of the Goddess Tripura</note> says: "Being Liberation itself, She who is reached through <term>devotion<note>Bhakti</note></term> should be meditated upon as being like the sky itself, free from all clouds."

->2<-

O <term>Great Goddess<note>Maheshi</note></term>,¹ even if a person of dull mind² should at any time recite but once another doubled <term>Seed-Syllable<note>Bîja</note></term> of Yours—composed of the letter **H** (<term>Îshâna</term>),³ the long vowel **ū** (<term>Vâmashravana</term>),⁴ and the nasal sound (<term>Bindu</term>)⁵—then, O You who wear great and formidable earrings shaped like arrows,⁶ and who bear the crescent moon upon Your head, such a person becomes all-

### NOTES

1 The <term>Feminine Power<note>Shakti</note></term> of the Great Lord (<term>Mahesha</term>), who is the Lord even over the Creator (<term>Brahmâ</term>), the Preserver (<term>Vishnu</term>), and the Destroyer (<term>Rudra</term>) (according to the commentator Vimalânanda). The Goddess is the <term>Sovereign Ruler<note>Devî Îshvarî</note></term> of the Universe and the Spouse of the <term>Supreme Lord beyond all attributes<note>Nirguna Maheshvara</note></term>. According to the *Limga Purâna*, the Supreme Lord (<term>Îshvara</term>), when associated with the quality of <term>darkness/inertia<note>Tamas</note></term>, is the Destroyer (<term>Rudra</term>); with the quality of <term>passion/activity<note>Rajas</note></term>, He is the Creator born from the golden egg (<term>Brahmâ</term>); and with the quality of <term>purity/harmony<note>Sattva</note></term>, He is the Preserver (<term>Vishnu</term>).

2 The Sanskrit term is <term>Mandachetâh</term>, referring to one who is not yet capable of full devotion to your lotus feet, according to the commentator Kâshînâtha Tarkapañchânana (K.B.); for, as the *Brahmânda Purâna* says, all sin is wiped away simply by remembering the feet of the Supreme Power (<term>Shakti</term>).

3 That is, the letter **Ha**.

4 Literally the "left ear," which in the technical code of Tantra refers to the long vowel **ū**.

5 The union of the "vibration and the point" (<term>Nâda-bindu</term>)—that is, combining the sounds **H + ū + m** to form the syllable **Hūm**. One who performs the <term>repetitive chanting<note>Japa</note></term> of the syllable **Hūm** is more praiseworthy than a god (<term>Deva</term>) or a titan (<term>Asura</term>), according to the *Vishvasâra Tantra* (K.B.).

6 Attributes worn by the Goddess Kālī. There is a technical note regarding the reading of the word for "arrow" (<term>Bâna</term>) instead of the word for "child" (<term>Bâla</term>), which will be discussed <note>original: "post"</note> later in the text.

COMMENTARY 33
5

powerful, <note>According to the *Vishvasāra Tantra*.</note> having conquered even the <term>Lord of Speech<note>Brihaspati, the preceptor and guru of the celestial gods</note></term> and the <term>Wealth-Giver<note>Kuvera (or Kubera), the King of the Yakshas and god of riches. He is variously described as the son or grandson of the sage Pulastya.</note></term>, and charmed countless youthful women with eyes as beautiful as lotuses. <note>This indicates that the practitioner is granted the gifts of supreme eloquence, immense wealth, and physical beauty or magnetism.</note>

## II

### COMMENTARY

<margin>‘Great Power’ <note>Mashehi</note></margin>
She is the possessor of the great Power responsible for creating, preserving, and withdrawing the entire universe.

<margin>‘At any time’ <note>Kadāchit</note></margin>
The scholar Durgārāma Siddhāntavāgīsha is of the opinion that by using the word "at any time," it is meant that—unlike other religious <term>ritual actions<note>Karma</note></term> which can only be performed in a state of ritual <term>purity<note>Shuchi</note></term>—the <term>meditative recitation<note>Japa</note></term> of the <term>sacred phrase<note>Mantra</note></term> of Kālī can be done at any time, regardless of whether one is in a state of ritual purity or impurity <note>Shauchāshaucha-kāla</note>. Here he states that one should not abandon the worship even if there is a birth or death in the house <note>Events that traditionally cause "ritual pollution" in Hindu custom</note>. The <term>Tantric Scriptures<note>Tantra Shāstra</note></term> state that one should perform the recitation of the mantra whether one is pure or impure, and whether walking, standing, or even sleeping.

<margin>‘Recite’ <note>Japati</note></margin>
To meditate upon deeply.

<margin>‘Of dual aspect’ <note>Dvandvam</note></margin>
Having the dual nature of <term>Shiva-Shakti<note>The inseparable union of the Masculine (Consciousness) and Feminine (Energy) principles</note></term>. The Tantric scriptures speak of the "King of Mantras" being generated by the union of these two divine forces.

<margin>‘Another Seed-Syllable’ <note>Bījamanyat</note></margin>
This refers to Your <term>causal<note>Kārana</note></term> aspect, which is the <term>seed-syllable<note>Bīja</note></term> **Hūṁ**. In the *Yāmala* <note>A category of ancient Tantric texts</note> it is said: "It is with the double <term>Seed of Sound<note>Shabdabīja</note></term> (which is **Hūṁ**) that She awakens the mass of Sound."

<margin>‘The Ruler’ <note>Īshāna</note></margin>
This refers to the Supreme Lord (<term>Īshvara</term>). The *Kathopanishad* <note>An ancient philosophical Vedic text</note> says: "The <term>Cosmic Person<note>Purusha</note></term> is the size of only a thumb. He is like smokeless fire, the Ruler (<term>Īshāna</term>) of what has been and what is to be."

<summary>This page explains that Kālī's worship is unique because it transcends traditional rules of ritual purity, allowing the practitioner to meditate at any time. It also identifies the seed-syllable 'Hūm' as a representation of the union of Shiva and Shakti, the fundamental forces of the universe.</summary>
<keywords>Kālī, Mantra, Japa, Purity, Hūm, Shiva-Shakti, Kuvera, Brihaspati, Tantra Shāstra, Kathopanishad</keywords>

has been and will be. He is today and He is tomorrow. This is That.’ <term>The Moon<note>Sanskrit: Indu. Symbolically associated with the nectar of immortality.</note></term> is immortality. <term>The Left-Ear Sound<note>Sanskrit: Vāmashravana. A technical term in Tantric phonetics referring to the long vowel 'Ū'.</note></term> is the power of granting speech and of attracting forms. The *Dictionary of Tantra* <note>Tantrābhidhāna</note> says, ‘The vowel "Ū" is <term>Bhairava<note>The fierce form of Shiva</note></term>, subtle, the Goddess of Wisdom <note>Sarasvatī</note> . . . the attractor of forms.’

‘Who wears the crescent moon’ <note>Chandrārdha-chūde</note>
</margin>
Who grants the liberation of <term>Nirvana<note>The final release from the cycle of suffering and rebirth.</note></term>. The *Great Liberation Tantra* <note>Mahānirvāna Tantra</note> says, ‘The forehead of Her who is Eternal <note>Nityā</note>, the Form of Time <note>Kālarūpā</note>, the Formless <note>Arūpā</note>, and Shiva Himself, is marked with the moon as a sign of Her immortality.’

<margin>
‘Earrings’ <note>Mahāghora-bālāvatangse</note>
</margin>
Whose earrings (things very dear to her) are formed of two <term>practitioners<note>Sādhakas</note></term> who are like the Great Lord <note>Maheshvara</note> and as simple as boys; that is, practitioners who possess child-like simplicity and true knowledge are dear to Her. In the *Crest-Jewel of Discrimination* <note>Vivekachūḍāmaṇi, a famous philosophical text attributed to Shankara</note> it is said, ‘Just as a boy plays with toys, heedless of hunger and other pain, so the wise man plays—happy, unattached, and selfless.’ Such a practitioner attains all forms of knowledge and riches and can charm the whole world.

There is, however, another reading of the text given by the scholar Durgārāma Siddhāntavāgīsha, namely *Mahāghora-vānāvatangse*, which means ‘whose earrings are formed of frightful arrows.’ <note>Vāna</note>

O <term>Kālikā<note>A name for the Goddess Kālī</note></term>, O auspicious Kālī^1 with dishevelled hair,^2 from the corners of whose mouth two streams of blood trickle,^3 they—

## NOTES

^1 The Goddess <note>Devī</note>. See *Great Liberation Tantra*, chapter 13 and chapter 4, verse 31: ‘At the dissolution of all things, it is Time <note>Kāla</note> who will devour all, and because of this, He is called the Great Time <note>Mahākāla</note>; and since You devour the Great Time Himself, it is You who are the supreme, primordial Kālī.’

^2 ‘With hair flowing loose’ <note>Vigalitachikurā</note>, just as the worshipped <term>divine heroine<note>Nāyikā</note></term> is depicted. See further on in the text.

^3 ‘From whose mouth hang two streams of blood from the corners of her lips’ <note>Srikkadvandvāsradhārādvayadharavadane</note>. Kālī is represented in this way because she has devoured the flesh of demons. The *Great Liberation Tantra*, chapter 13, verse 9, says: ‘As She devours all existence, as She chews all existing things with Her fierce teeth, a mass of blood is imagined to be the clothing of the Queen of the Gods <note>Devas</note>.’ Esoterically, blood represents the <term>Quality of Passion and Activity<note>Rajas Guṇa</note></term>.

...who recite another doubled <term>seed-syllable<note>Sanskrit: Bīja. A single-syllable mantra that contains the essence of a deity.</note></term> of Yours composed of the <term>Lord<note>Sanskrit: Īsha. In Tantric phonetic code, this refers to the sound "Ha".</note></term>,¹ the <term>Lord of Fire<note>Sanskrit: Vaishvānara. This refers to the sound "Ra".</note></term>,² the <term>Left Eye<note>Sanskrit: Vāmanetra. This refers to the long vowel sound "Ī".</note></term>,³ and the lustrous <term>nasal drop<note>Sanskrit: Bindu. The dot above a letter signifying a nasalized humming sound.</note></term>,⁴ destroy all their enemies, and bring under their subjection the <term>three worlds<note>Sanskrit: Tribhuvana. Usually referring to the Earth, the atmosphere/heavens, and the netherworld.</note></term>.⁵

### III

### COMMENTARY

<margin>Kālikā</margin>
"Ka" is the Creator <note>Brahmā</note>, "A" is the Infinite <note>Ananta</note>, "La" is the Soul <note>Ātmā</note> of the universe, "I" is the subtle, "Ka" is the Creator, "A" is the Infinite. (According to the *Dictionary of Tantra* <note>Tantrābhidhāna</note>). Thus it is said that the Great Goddess <note>Mahādevī</note> is the subtle, beginningless and endless Soul of the universe. ‘You who are the Ultimate Reality <note>Brahman</note> without beginning or end.’ In the *Hymn of the Dark One* <note>Asitāstotra</note> found in the *Miraculous Ramayana* <note>Adbhutarāmāyaṇa</note>, the holy Rama says: ‘I bow to that aspect of Yours which is the Eternal Person <note>Purusha</note> without beginning and end, the unmanifest, <term>Unchanging One<note>Sanskrit: Kūṭastha. A term for the highest consciousness that remains stable while all else changes.</note></term> who is superior to Your aspect as Nature <note>Prakriti</note>, the Soul of the universe appearing in multiple and differing forms.’

[The scholar Durgārāma Siddhāntavāgīsha derives the word Kālikā as follows:—He who dissolves <note>Sanskrit: Kalayati</note> the world is Time/Death <note>Kāla</note>, or the god Shiva. And She who shines <note>Sanskrit: Divyati</note>—that is, who plays <note>Sanskrit: Krīḍati</note>—with Him is Kālikā. The formula is: Kāla + the suffix *ikan* + the feminine ending *ā* = Kālikā.]

<margin>‘With dishevelled hair’ <note>Vigalitachikure</note></margin>
This refers to one who is free from all <term>mental modifications<note>Sanskrit: Vikāras. Changes or distortions in the mind, often used here to mean vanity or worldly preoccupations.</note></term>, such as the passion for arranging the hair and so forth.

<margin>‘Streams of blood’ <note>Asradhārā</note></margin>
This blood indicates the <term>quality of passion<note>Sanskrit: Rajas Guna. The energy of movement, desire, and action, symbolized by the color red.</note></term>. The Great Goddess is without that, for She is of the <term>quality of pure essence<note>Sanskrit: Shuddhasattvaguna. The state of absolute purity, light, and harmony.</note></term>.

<margin>Recite <note>Japati</note></margin>
To meditate upon.

---

¹ This refers to the sound *Ha*, as described in the *Tantra of the Cow of Plenty* <note>Kāmadhenu Tantra</note>, chapter 2; and the *Satisfaction of the Life-Breath* <note>Prāṇatoṣiṇī</note>, page 53 and following.
² The Lord of Fire, whose seed-syllable is *Ra*.
³ The ‘Left eye,’ or the fourth vowel, the long *ī*.
⁴ The combined nasal sound and dot. The seed-syllable is thus formed as H + r + i + m = *Hrīṁ*. This is repeated: *Hrīṁ Hrīṁ*. In the *Independent Tantra* <note>Svatantra Tantra</note>, the sound *Ha* <note>representing Space or Vyoma</note> is said to denote the manifestation of the universe; *Ra* <note>Fire or Vahni</note> is its dissolution; and *Ī* is the maintenance of the worlds.
⁵ The earth, the upper world, and the nether world (see the *Tantra of the Essence of the Universe* <note>Vishvasāra Tantra</note> and the *Tantra of the Sound of the Pheut bird* <note>Pheṭkāriṇī Tantra</note>). The "three worlds" <note>Tribhuvanam</note> refers to the gods <note>Devas</note>, humans, and serpent-beings <note>Nāgas</note> who inhabit Heaven <note>Svarga</note>, Earth <note>Martya</note>, and the Netherworld <note>Pātāla</note>.

<summary>This page explains the construction of the mantra "Hrīṁ" as a combination of Shiva, Fire, and Maintenance, while also providing an etymological breakdown of the name Kālikā. It highlights the Goddess's nature as the "Pure Essence" who is beyond worldly passions and vanity.</summary>
<keywords>Hrīṁ, seed-syllable, Kālikā, Shiva, Rajas, Sattva, Tantra, Sanskrit etymology, three worlds, meditation</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of the goddess Kālī’s mantras, specifically moving from the general effects of the "Hrīm" syllable to a breakdown of its phonetic components and an explanation of her title "Dakshinā."</meta>

# HYMN TO KÂLÎ

<margin>
Of dual aspect
<note>Sanskrit: Dvandvam</note>
</margin>
The <term>seed-syllable<note>Sanskrit: Bīja; a foundational sound-form of a deity.</note></term> *Hrīm* is both the masculine principle <note>Shiva</note> and the feminine principle <note>Shakti</note>. In the *Song of the Goddess* <note>Devīgītā</note>, the Great Goddess says: "H is the physical body, R is the subtle body, and I is the causal body. I am *Hrīm*, the fourth state of pure consciousness <note>Turīya</note>."

<margin>The Lord <note>Īsha</note></margin>
Who is the aspect of the subtle <term>seed-syllable</term>.

<margin>The Lord of Fire <note>Vaishvānara</note></margin>
Which is full of burning radiance <note>Tejas</note>.

<margin>The Left Eye <note>Vāmanetra</note></margin>
That is, the power of illusion <note>Māyā</note> consisting of the quality of purity and light <note>Sattva Guna</note>.

<margin>The Moon <note>Indu</note></margin>
This is the feminine energy <note>Shakti</note> which grants immortality.

<margin>
"The
three-syllabled
Dakshinā"
</margin>
The word *Dakshine* is the goddess’s name, *Dakshinā*, addressed in the vocative. The latter refers to the aspect of "Existence-Consciousness-Bliss" <note>Sachchidānanda</note> which grants final liberation <note>Kaivalya</note> and is signified by the three-syllabled prayer <note>Mantra</note>. The *Tantra of Liberation* <note>Nirvāna Tantra</note> says: "The son of the Sun (Death) resides in the south <note>Dakshina</note>. The name of Kālī makes him flee in all directions with fear. Therefore, She is called the 'Southern One' <note>Dakshinā</note> throughout the three worlds." The *Tantra of the Goddess of Desire* <note>Kāmākhyā Tantra</note> says: "Just as a ritual fee <note>Dakshinā</note> given at the end of a rite makes it fruitful and leads to liberation, so this Goddess grants the fruit of all actions <note>Karma</note>, and is thus called Dakshinā Kālī." The same scripture also says: "The Male Principle <note>Purusha</note> is on the right <note>Dakshinā</note> and the Female Principle <note>Shakti</note> is on the left. The left conquers the right and becomes the giver of great liberation. Thus, She is called <term>Dakshinākālī<note>The 'Southern Kālī' or the 'Auspicious Kālī'.</note></term> in the three worlds."

[The commentator Durgārāma interprets these words as follows: *Dakshine tryakshare ati* (joined by the rules of <term>grammar<note>Sanskrit: Sandhi</note></term> as *tryakshare’ti*), which means "Oh Dakshinā, who art beyond the three-lettered one." Since <term>prefixes<note>Sanskrit: Upasargas</note></term> can change their position in a sentence, the word "ati" (beyond/above) has been placed after "Tryakshare" in the verse. *Atitryakshare* is a title for the Goddess meaning "She who has placed the Three-Lettered One (Shiva) beneath Her." The whole phrase then means: "Oh Dakshinā, you who stand upon the body of Shiva." The term <term>Three-Lettered One<note>Sanskrit: Tryakshara</note></term> literally refers to the sacred syllable *OM*, which is used as a name for Shiva. The *Hymn to the Greatness of Shiva* <note>Mahimnastotra</note> calls Shiva "OM," and another hymn describes Him as being composed of those three letters.

The same commentator then notes a different version of the text: instead of "Dakshine tryakshareti," the reading is "Dakshine Kāliketi," which he explains in two ways: (*a*) "Oh Dakshinā, who art Kālikā"...]

<summary>This page provides a mystical phonetic breakdown of the seed-syllable "Hrīm" as a union of Shiva and Shakti, followed by several etymological explanations for the name "Dakshinā Kālī." It explores themes of liberation, the triumph of the feminine principle over the masculine, and the Goddess's power over death.</summary>

<keywords>Hrīm, Dakshinā Kālī, seed-syllable, Shiva, Shakti, liberation, Tantra, Sanskrit grammar, Durgārāma, Arthur Avalon</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the grammatical and esoteric analysis of the goddess Kālī’s name, specifically explaining the title "Dakshinā Kālī" and its phonetic components, before moving into the translation and commentary of the fourth verse of the hymn, which describes her physical form and iconic hand gestures.</meta>

# COMMENTARY 37

"O Kālī" <note>original: *Kālike*</note> refers to "O Southern One who surpasses the darkness" <note>original: *Dakshine atikālike*</note>. The last word is the form of address for "She who surpasses the darkness" <note>original: *Atikālikā*</note>, which means "She by whom the bank of clouds" <note>original: *Meghajālam*</note> "is surpassed" <note>original: *Atikrāntā*</note>—that is, "made to look like Her" <note>original: *Sadrishīkritā*</note>. She—that is, She who looks like a bank of clouds; the whole phrase then means: "O Southern One <note>original: *Dakshinā*</note> who has the appearance of a bank of clouds." (*b*) "O Southern Kālī" <note>original: *Dakshine Kâliketi*</note> equals "O Southern Kālī, thus" <note>original: *Dakshine Kâlike iti*</note>. The word "thus" <note>original: *iti*</note> indicates the nature of the address <note>original: *Svarûpârthaka*</note>; it simply shows that She is addressed as "Southern Kālī" <note>original: *Dakshinā Kālikā*</note>. Examples of the blending of vowel sounds <note>original: *Sandhi*; the elision of 'I' after 'E'</note> are "Shakuntalā, thus" <note>original: *Shakuntaleti*</note> and "As in a bank of clouds, Kālī, thus" <note>original: *Meghajâle'pi Kâliketi*</note>.

## 4

> O Destructress of the sins of the three worlds, auspicious¹
> **Kālikā**, who in Your upper lotus-like left hand hold a sword,²
> and in the lower left hand a severed head;³ who with Your upper
> right hand make the gesture which dispels fear,⁴ and with Your
> lower right hand that which grants boons; they, O Mother
> with gaping mouth,⁵ who reciting Your name, meditate in this—

### NOTES

¹ **The Southern One** <note>original: *Dakshinā*</note>, the beneficent grantor of **final liberation** <note>original: *Nirvâna*</note>. (Commentary by Vimalānanda)

² **Sword** <note>original: *Khadga*</note>, the peculiar heavy sword with the blade curved at the tip so named, used to behead the sacrificial animals.

³ The Goddess <note>original: *Devî*</note> is the destroyer of the wicked.

⁴ The Goddess <note>original: *Devî*</note> is the dispeller of all fear, and makes with Her hand the **sacred gesture** <note>original: *mudrâ*</note>. The right upper hand makes the **gesture of dispelling fear** <note>original: *Abhayamudrâ*</note>, or the gesture of assurance of safety, and the right lower hand makes the **gesture of granting boons** <note>original: *Varamudrâ*</note>. (Vimalānanda) The practitioner <note>original: *Sâdhaka*</note> seeks fearlessness, which is the great gift of the Goddess, who is "She who removes fear" <note>original: *Bhayâpahâ*</note>. "If you are remembered in times of difficulty, You take away all fear" (*Mārkandeya Purāna*). At the same time, it is she who fills the ignorant with terror <note>original: *Pashuloka-bhayangkari*</note>—that is, those devoid of the knowledge of non-duality, for "fear comes when there is duality" (*Brihadaranyaka Upanishad* 1-4-2, *Lalitâ*, verse 99).

⁵ **With gaping mouth** <note>original: *Prakatita-vadane*</note> (see "The Secret of Worship of the Ten Great Wisdom-Goddesses" <note>original: *Dasha-Mahâvidyâ Upâsanârahasya*</note>, by Prasanna Kumâra Shâstrî). Vimalānanda reads "With protruding teeth" <note>original: *Prakatitaradane*</note>. The *Yoginî Tantra* says, "Supreme eternal, large-toothed, smeared with blood." The *Târâkalpa* speaks of "The Dark One <note>original: *Shyâmâ*</note> of the color of a new (freshly formed) cloud, with large breasts, terrible with protruding teeth." (Vimalānanda)

<summary>This page explains the linguistic roots of the name Dakshinā Kālī, comparing her dark complexion to rain clouds, and describes her four-armed form holding a sword and severed head while offering protection and blessings.</summary>

way¹ upon the greatness of Thy mantra, possess the eight great powers² of the Three-Eyed One³ in the palm of their hands.⁴

## IV

### COMMENTARY

<margin>Sword <note>original: *Kripânam*</note></margin>
The sword represents spiritual knowledge <note>term: *Jnâna*; the wisdom that distinguishes reality from illusion</note> by which the bonds of ignorance holding the desire-free practitioner <note>term: *Sâdhaka*; one who follows a spiritual path</note> are severed. See the *Shiva-dharmottara* <note>A classic text on the duties and rituals associated with the god Shiva</note>.

<margin>Severed head <note>original: *Chinnamundam*</note></margin>
The human head is considered the seat of the knowledge of ultimate truth <note>term: *Tattvajnâna*</note> which is free from worldly attachment.

<margin>Terrible countenance <note>original: *Prakratitaradane*; literally, 'revealed teeth'</note></margin>
Her white teeth indicate the white, self-manifesting quality of purity and harmony <note>term: *Sattva Guna*</note>. These teeth bite the red lolling tongue, which indicates the quality of passion and activity <note>term: *Rajas Guna*</note>. This symbolizes the suppression of both passion <note>Rajas</note> and inertia <note>term: *Tamas Guna*; the quality of darkness and ignorance</note> by the power of purity.

<margin>Precious Mantras <note>original: *Manu-vibhavam*</note></margin>
The three *Krîm* seed-syllables <note>term: *Bîja*; a sound-syllable containing the essence of a deity</note> represent the goddess in her aspect as pure, concentrated consciousness <note>term: *Chidghana*</note>. The two *Hūm* syllables represent the quality of purity <note>Sattva</note>, and the two *Hrîm* syllables represent the quality of passion-dominated purity <note>term: *Rajahpradhânasattva*</note>.

---

1. As stated—that is, the sequence *Krîm Krîm Krîm Hūm Hūm Hrîm Hrîm* which, when combined with the name *Dakshine* ("O Southern One"), creates a mantra of ten syllables.

2. *Siddhi*—these are the eight supernatural attainments: *Animâ* (the power to become tiny), *Laghimâ* (to become light), *Garimâ* (to become heavy), *Prâpti* (to reach anywhere), *Prâkâmya* (to realize any desire), *Îshitva* (lordship over creation), *Vashitva* (control over all), and *Kâmâvasâyitâ* (absolute satisfaction of all wishes). These powers are inherent in the Supreme Lord <note>term: *Îshvara*</note> and are achievable by practitioners of Yoga who realize their oneness with the Divine and gain Divine sovereignty <note>term: *Âishvarya*</note>. By realizing the true Self, that Divine state which is the universal Self is revealed, along with these eight manifestations of Divine power.

3. *Tryambaka* is a name for the god Shiva. According to Tarkâlangkâra’s commentary on the *Mahânirvâna Tantra* <note>a primary scripture of the Tantric tradition</note>, *Tryambaka* means the father of the three deities: Brahmâ (the Creator), Vishnu (the Preserver), and Rudra (the Destroyer). The ancient text *Rigvidhâna* uses it as a synonym for the "Great God" <note>term: *Mahâdeva*</note>. The *Mahânirvâna Tantra* explains: "As She surveys the entire universe, which is the product of time, with Her three eyes—the Moon, Sun, and Fire—therefore She is endowed with three eyes" (Chapter 13, verse 8). The Moon, Sun, and Fire symbolize the Divine powers of Will, Action, and Knowledge <note>term: *Ichchhâ, Kriyâ, and Jnâna Shaktis*</note>. (See also the *Shatchakranirûpana* by Pûrnânanda Svâmî and *The Serpent Power* by Arthur Avalon).

4. "Just as one who carries a flower enjoys its fragrance without seeking it, so too does one who views themselves as the universal Self enjoy the powers of the gods like Brahmâ." (From the commentary of Sureshwarâchârya on the 11th verse of the *Dakshinâmûrti Stotra*).

<summary>This page concludes the fourth verse of the hymn, explaining that the devotee who meditates on Kālī's mantra gains the eight supernatural powers (Siddhis) of Shiva. The commentary provides an esoteric interpretation of her iconography, linking her sword and severed head to spiritual knowledge and the three qualities (Gunas) of nature.</summary>
<keywords>Kālī, Mantra, Siddhi, Sattva Guna, Rajas Guna, Tamas Guna, Bija, Shiva, Mahânirvâna Tantra, Jnâna, Iconography</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on Verse 4, specifically interpreting the phrase "the wealth of mantras," before transitioning into the fifth verse of the hymn. The previous page detailed the symbolic meaning of the goddess's physical attributes.</meta>

### COMMENTARY

[Durgârâma Siddhântavâgîsha <note>A renowned 19th-century Sanskrit scholar and commentator</note> explains the phrase "Wealth of Mantras" <note>original: *Manuvibhava*</note> in the following different ways:—(*a*) *Manuvibhava* refers to the Power or Precious Possession <note>original: *Sampatti*</note> of the Sacred Syllables <note>term: *Mantras*</note>. This precious possession is the name "Oh Kālī of the South" <note>original: *Dakshine Kâlike*</note> used in the vocative case. The meaning of the passage is that those who recite Thy name, which is the very wealth of the Mantras, and meditate on Your appearance, will possess supernatural powers and other blessings. (*b*) *Manuvibhava* is the power of the twenty-two syllabled prayer <note>term: *Manu*; here referring to a specific mantra</note> of the goddess Kālī. This power is the specific name "Dakshinâ Kâlikâ." (*c*) *Manu-vibhava* means that the goddess’s body is generated by the Mantra itself. The forms of the Deities <note>term: *Devatâs*</note> are produced by their respective seed-sounds. Thus, the passage means that those who recite Thy name and meditate on this form of Yours—which is brought into being by the Mantra—possess great power.]

See last Verse.

<margin>
Kâlikâ (Oh Kâlikâ)

"Three-eyed one" <note>original: *Tryambaka*</note>
</margin>
[The same commentator (Durgârâma) offers three explanations of the term "Three-eyed One" <note>original: *Tryambaka*</note> used as a title for the god Shiva: (*a*) He who has three eyes <note>original: *Ambakas*</note> is the Three-eyed One. (*b*) He who has three Mothers <note>original: *Ambâs*</note> is the Three-eyed One. The *Kâlikâ-purâna* <note>An ancient scripture dedicated to the goddess Kālī</note> says, "As the god Hara (Shiva) is born of three Mothers, He is known, even amongst the Gods, by the title Tryambaka." (*c*) The *Todala Tantra* <note>A foundational text of the Tantric tradition</note> says "the Wisdom-Goddess <note>term: *Vidyâ*</note> Bhubaneshvarî <note>The Mistress of the Universe</note> exists in Heaven, Earth, and the Netherworld <note>original: *Pâtâla*</note>. He who delights in the Goddess as appearing threefold in these three places is called the Three-eyed One. He is united with Divine Power <note>term: *Shakti*</note> and is worshipped in all Tantric scriptures."]

->5<-

O Mother, they who recite Thy charming Seed-Syllable <note>term: *Bîja*; a primary sound-symbol of a deity</note>, composed of the first of the group of letters,¹ followed by Fire,² Passion,³ and beautified by the Moon,⁴ three times; followed by the Seed-Syllable of the Spiritual Teacher ⁵ twice, and there—

---

¹ That is, the letter *Ka*.
² The God of Fire, representing the letter *Ra*.
³ *Rati*, the consort of the God of Love, representing the long vowel *Î*.
⁴ The moon, representing the nasal sound <note>original: *Nâda-bindu*</note>. The combined Seed-Syllable is, therefore, *Krîṁ*.
⁵ That is, the sound *Hûṁ*.

<meta>This page transitions from the general commentary on the "Wealth of Mantras" into the specific interpretation of Verse V. It focuses on the phonetic structure of the goddess's mantra—specifically the "Seed-sound of Modesty"—and its power to transform the practitioner into a figure of divine beauty and attraction. It continues the scholarly tradition of citing Durgârâma Siddhântavâgîsha.</meta>

-># HYMN TO KÂLÎ<-

...after, O Smiling Face, the Seed-sound of Modesty <note>original: *Lajjā Bīja*; the sacred syllable *Hrīṃ*</note> twice, followed by the two *Tha* sounds <note>original: *Thas*, which combined form the word *Svāhā*</note>, they, O Spouse of the Destroyer of the God of Desire <note>original: *Smarahara*; a title for the god Shiva, who destroyed the god of love with his third eye</note> who contemplate Your true form, become themselves like the God of Love <note>original: *Deva* of Desire; refers to Manmatha or Kama</note>. Their eyes become as beautiful as the petals of the lotus which the goddess Lakshmi <note>The goddess of prosperity and beauty</note> holds in Her playful dance.

### V

### COMMENTARY

<margin>Whoever (Original: *Ye, ye*)</margin> Even the most sinful. *The Secret of the Kali Lineage* <note>original: *Kâlikularahasya*</note> says, ‘Whoever he be who remembers the goddess Durga with or without reverence is delivered from evil and attains the supreme end.’

<margin>Recite (Original: *Japanti*)</margin> Meditate upon.

<margin>Thy Seed-sound (Original: *Thy Bîja*)</margin> [Durgârâma Siddhântavâgîsha <note>A prominent 19th-century commentator</note> calls it the nine-syllabled Seed-sound.]

<margin>First letter (Original: *Vargâdyam*)</margin> The aspect of Pure Consciousness <note>original: *Chinmayarūpa*</note> which is the beginning of creation.

<margin>Placed on Fire (Original: *Vahnisamstham*)</margin> Full of radiance <note>term: *Tejas*; the vital spiritual heat or fiery energy</note>.

<margin>Associated (Original: *Vidhuratilalitam*)</margin> That which is cooling and beautiful <note>Likening the goddess to the cooling rays of the moon</note>.

---

1. *Hrīṃ*; the literal meaning of *Lajjā* is modesty.

2. Or *Svāhā*, the feminine power <note>term: *Shakti*</note> of the fire-god Agni. The sacred chant <note>term: *Mantra*</note> is, then: *Krīṃ, Krīṃ, Krīṃ, Hūṃ, Hūṃ, Hrīṃ, Hrīṃ Svāhā*. This is the nine-lettered Wisdom-chant <note>term: *Vidyā*; a feminine form of mantra</note> which ends with *Svāhā*. (See *The All-Pervading Essence Tantra* <note>original: *Vishvasâra Tantra*</note>).

3. "Destroyer of Memory/Desire" <note>original: *Smarahara*</note> or Shiva, who destroyed the god of love <note>original: *Manmatha*</note> with fire from his central eye of wisdom when the latter sought to distract him by passion from his meditation <note>term: *Yoga*</note>. The Goddess <note>term: *Devī*</note>, according to the *Brahmavaivarta Scripture*, restored the god of love to life. (Regarding this, see Bhâskararâya's Commentary on the *Lalitâ*, verse 34).

4. "True form" <note>original: *Svarūpam*</note>, that is the essential nature of the goddess as described in the first and subsequent verses.

5. Lakshmi is associated with, holds, and stands on the lotus, hence Her titles—The Lotus-One <note>original: *Kamalâ*</note>, The Lotus <note>original: *Padmâ*</note>, Dweller in the Lotus <note>original: *Padmâlayâ*</note>, and Holder of the Lotus <note>original: *Padmadhârinî*</note>. (See "Hymn to Lakshmi" in the *Essence of Tantra* <note>original: *Tantrasâra*</note>).

<summary>This page details Verse V of the hymn, explaining how the recitation of specific seed-sounds (Mantras) related to Kali can transform a practitioner into a person of immense beauty and spiritual power. The commentary emphasizes that even the sinful can reach the supreme end through devotion to the goddess Durga/Kali.</summary>

<keywords>Kali, seed-sound, Bija, Hrīṃ, Shiva, Manmatha, Lakshmi, Mantra, Durga, Tejas, Tantra, Durgarama Siddhantavagisha</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the specific commentary on Verse V, moving from the identity of the practitioner to the esoteric breakdown of the goddess’s mantra. It defines the syllables of the mantra and establishes the philosophical foundation of non-duality (advaita) between the worshiper and the deity, citing various Tantric authorities and the commentator Durgârâma Siddhântavâgîsha.</meta>

# COMMENTARY

<margin>‘Thrice’ <note>original: *Trayam*</note></margin>
That is the three aspects of Existence, Passion, and Inertia <note>original: *Sattva*, *Rajas*, *Tamas*; the three qualities or "strings" that make up the physical and mental world</note>.

<margin>Seed-sound of Power <note>original: *Kūrca*; the syllable *Hūṃ*</note></margin>
Is the Supreme Reality in the form of Sound <note>term: *Shabdabrahman*; the causal vibration from which the universe is manifested</note>.

<margin>‘Modesty’ <note>original: *Lajjā*; the syllable *Hrīṃ*</note></margin>
Is the Supreme Reality <note>original: *Brahman*</note> associated with the Power of Illusion <note>term: *Māyā*; the divine creative power that projects the world</note>.

<margin>‘Two Thas’</margin>
The word *Svāhā* <note>formed by the suffix *tha-tha*</note>, which is the revealing Power <note>term: *Shakti*</note> of Fire.

<margin>‘Smiling face’ <note>original: *Smitamukhi*</note></margin>
Because She is always blissful.

<margin>‘Spouse of the Destroyer’ <note>original: *Smaraharamahile*</note></margin>
The Power of Shiva who is the Destroyer of passionate Desire; that is, She destroys the lust, anger, and so forth of Her spiritual practitioners <note>original: *Sādhakas*</note>.

<margin>‘Thy true form’ <note>original: *Svarūpam*</note></margin>
That which is not different in essence from the individual soul <note>original: *Jīvātmā*</note>. ‘True Form’ <note>original: *Svarūpa*</note> is explained here as the form of the Self <note>original: *Sva*, meaning *Ātmā*</note>, meaning the Oneness of the Supreme Soul <note>original: *Paramātmā*</note> and the individual soul. The *Sacred Text of Kâlikâ* <note>original: *Kālikā Śruti*</note> says, ‘One should always think of the Self as Kâli.’ *The Essence of the Kâli Lineage* <note>original: *Kālikulasarvasva*</note> says, ‘He who worships the spouse of Shiva thinking that his own soul is Kâlikâ’s soul and meditating on the Shiva-like Teacher <note>original: *Guru*</note> is the Ever-Auspicious Lord <note>original: *Sadāshiva*</note> Himself.’ The *Union of Yoga Treatise* <note>original: *Yoginî Tantra*</note> says, ‘He who thinks, even if it were for a moment, “I am the Supreme Reality” <note>original: *Brahman*</note>, to him the Goddess <note>original: *Devî*</note> gives unending fruit. One’s own body should always be thought of as the body of one’s chosen deity <note>term: *Ishtadevatā*; the specific form of the divine a practitioner focuses upon</note>. And so the whole world should be considered as Her body.’

[Durgârâma explains ‘True Form’ <note>original: *Svarūpa*</note> in the following ways: (*a*) The true form is that indicated in the previous or following verses. (*b*) It is that of the nine-syllabled sacred chant <note>term: *Mantra*</note>. (*c*) It is that indicated by the letters composing the chant. For instance, the *Tantra of the Giver of Boons* <note>original: *Baradā Tantra*</note> says that in the syllable ‘Krîm’, **K** is Kâlî, **R** is the creator Brahmâ, **Î** is the Great Illusion <note>original: *Mahāmāyā*</note>, the nasal vibration <note>original: *Nāda*</note> is the Womb of the universe, and the point <note>original: *Bindu*</note> is the Dispeller of Sorrow. In ‘Hûm’, **H** is Shiva, **Û** is the fierce Bhairava, the nasal vibration means the Supreme, and the point is the Dispeller of Sorrow. In ‘Hrîm’, **H** is Shiva, **R** is Nature <note>original: *Prakriti*</note>, **Î** is the Great Illusion, the nasal vibration is the Mother of the Universe, and the point is the dispeller of pain. Contemplation on these sacred chants constituted of these letters reveals their living consciousness <note>term: *Chaitanya*; the divine energy inherent in sacred sound</note>. Ritual repetition <note>term: *Japa*</note> of a chant without knowing its living consciousness is useless.]

<sig>6</sig>

<summary>This page provides an esoteric commentary on the components of Kâli's mantra, defining the symbolic meaning of specific syllables and emphasizing the non-dual realization that the worshiper's soul is identical to the Goddess.</summary>
<keywords>Mantra, Seed-sounds, Gunas, Svarupa, Brahman, Atma, Shiva-Shakti, Chaitanya, Japa, Durgarama, Esoteric Phonetics</keywords>

<meta>This page transitions from the spiritual fruits of the fifth verse—the power of transformation and charm—into the sixth verse, which details the specific recitation of the Goddess's secret seed-syllables and provides extensive commentary on her maternal iconography.</meta>

They acquire the power of assuming whatever form they desire and of charming the whole world with their beauty.

### 6

<margin>
‘They take any form they desire’
<note>original: *Kāmarūpā bhavanti*</note>
</margin>
O Goddess <note>original: *Devī*</note>¹ of full breasts,² whose throat is adorned with a garland of heads, those who, while meditating,³ recite any one or two or three of Thy very secret and excellent seed-syllables <note>original: *Bījas*</note> or all of them⁴ together with Thy name,⁵ in the moonlike face of all such the—

### NOTES

¹ **Goddess** <note>original: *Devī*</note>, which comes from the root *Div* (to shine), is the Shining One. (V)

² **She of the full breasts** <note>original: *Pinastanādhye*</note> (see also the *Hymn to the Terrific One* <note>original: *Bhairavīstotra*</note> in the *Essence of Tantra* <note>original: *Tantra-sāra*</note>, p. 596). The physical characteristics of the Goddess in swelling breasts and hips are emblematic of Her great Motherhood, for She is the **Sacred Mother** <note>original: *Shrīmātā*</note>. See also as to the former, the **Visualization of Durgā** <note>original: *Durgā Dhyāna*</note> in the *Ancient Chronicle of the Goddess* <note>original: *Devī Purāna*</note>, which speaks of her "large and rising breasts" <note>original: *Pinonnatapayodharām*</note>; the **Hymn to the Provider of Food** <note>original: *Annapūrnā Stava*</note> ("whose breasts are like jars" <note>original: *Bakshojakumbhântari*</note>); the **Hymn to the Lady of the Worlds** <note>original: *Bhubanesvarī Stotra*</note> ("with high and heavy breasts" <note>original: *Âpîvarastana-tatîm*</note>); and the **Visualization of the Goddess of Wisdom** <note>original: *Saraswatî Dhyâna*</note> ("whose body is bent by the weight of her breasts" <note>original: *Kuchabharanamitâmgim*</note>). The **Visualization of the Provider of Food** <note>original: *Annapurnâ Dhyâna*</note> ("intent on giving food, bent by the weight of her breasts" <note>original: *Annapradânaniratâmg stanabhâranamrâm*</note>) speaks of Her limbs as weighted by Her breasts. The *Great Chronicle of the Goddess* <note>original: *Mahâbhâgavata*</note> describes Her as naked, terrific, with fiery eyes, full and erect breasts, and dishevelled hair; and the **Hymn to the Playful One** <note>original: *Lalitâ*</note> (verse 15) says: "Her golden girdle supports Her waist, which bends under the burden of Her breasts, thrice folding the skin below Her bosom" <note>original: *Stanabhâradalanmadhyapattabandhavalitrayâ*</note>.

³ **Meditating** <note>original: *Bhâvayanah*</note>, that is, meditating on the naked, full-breasted, black form with dishevelled hair as stated in Her meditative visualization <note>original: *Dhyâna*</note>, and which is the **meaning** <note>original: *Artha*</note> of the particular sacred chant <note>original: *Mantra*</note>. The *Tantra of the Purification of the Elements* <note>original: *Bhûtashuddhi Tantra*</note> says, "A sacred chant should be recited mentally while meditating on the form of the Goddess denoted by it." (V)

⁴ Any one of the aforesaid **seed-syllables** <note>original: *Bījas*</note> or the whole—that is, the whole nine-lettered **sacred knowledge** <note>original: *Vidyâ*</note> in full. Which according to the *Tantra of the Maiden* <note>original: *Kumârî Tantra*</note> cited in the *Jewel of Tantra* <note>original: *Tantraratna*</note> is *Krīm, Krīm, Krīm, Hūm, Hūm, Hūm, Hrīm, Hrīm, Hrīm*. The *Secret of the Dark One* <note>original: *Shyâmârahasya*</note> quoting the *Revelation of Kali* <note>original: *Kâlikâ Shruti*</note>, says that the whole sacred knowledge should be recited once, twice, or thrice, or the whole mantra with "To the South-facing Kali" <note>original: *Dakshine Kâlike*</note> between the seed-syllables. (K.B.) Thus: *Krīm, Krīm, Krīm, Hūm, Hūm, Hrīm, Hrīm, To the South-facing Kali, Krīm, Krīm, Krīm, Hūm, Hūm, Hrīm, Hrīm*.

⁵ **The South-facing Kali** <note>original: *Dakshinâ Kâlikâ*</note>.

<summary>This page introduces the sixth verse of the hymn, focusing on the practitioner's meditation upon Kali's seed-syllables and her physical form. The commentary explains her iconography—specifically her "full breasts"—as a symbol of divine motherhood and abundance, supported by numerous scriptural references.</summary>
<keywords>Kali, seed-syllables, Bija, motherhood, iconography, meditation, Dhyana, Mantra, South-facing Kali, Tantra</keywords>

COMMENTARY

The Goddess of Speech¹ ever wanders, and in their lotus-like eyes
the Lotus Goddess² ever plays.³

## VI

### COMMENTARY

<margin>'The Goddess' <note>original: *Devī*</note></margin>
The self-manifest one.

<margin>'Full breasts' <note>original: *Pīnastanā-dhyā*</note></margin>
The milk of these is the food with which She nourishes the world and the drink of immortality with which She liberates Her <term>spiritual practitioners <note>original: *Sādhakas*</note></term>.

<margin>'Whose neck' <note>original: *Mundasra-gatishaya-lasatkanthi*</note></margin>
She who is <term>Sound-Brahman <note>original: *Shabdabrahman*</note></term> <note>The Divine Absolute manifested as sound</note> consisting of the fifty letters of the Sanskrit alphabet. The *Response Beyond Tantra* <note>original: *Niruttara Tantra*</note> says, 'She is adorned with a garland of heads representing the fifty letters.' The *Cow of Plenty Tantra* <note>original: *Kāmadhenu Tantra*</note> says, 'In My throat is the wonderful <term>seed-syllable <note>original: *Bīja*</note></term> of fifty letters.' Again, 'I worship the Mother, the source of the universe, Sound-Brahman itself, blissful.' The *Essence of the Universe* <note>original: *Vishvasāra*</note> says, 'Blissful Brahman is adorned with Sound-Brahman and within the body is represented by all <term>sacred mantras <note>original: *Mantras*</note></term>.'

<margin>'Seed-Syllable' <note>original: *Bīja*</note></margin>
The <term>divine form <note>original: *Mūrti*</note></term> in the individual aspect as the <term>Wise <note>original: *Prājña*</note></term> <note>the state of deep sleep</note>, the <term>Luminous <note>original: *Taijasa*</note></term> <note>the state of dreaming</note>, and the <term>Universal <note>original: *Vishva*</note></term> <note>the waking state</note>; and in the collective aspect as the <term>Lord <note>original: *Īsha*</note></term>, the <term>Thread-Soul <note>original: *Sūtra*</note></term>, and the <term>Radiant One <note>original: *Virāt*</note></term>. The *Song of the Goddess* <note>original: *Devīgītā*</note> says 'the causal self is the Wise, the subtle-bodied one is the Luminous, and the gross-bodied one is the Universal.'

<margin>Eyes <note>original: *Netra*</note></margin>
Similarly, the Lord <note>original: *Īsha*</note> is spoken of as the Lord, the Thread, and the Radiant One. The first is the individual <note>original: *Vyashti*</note> aspect and the second the aggregate or collective <note>original: *Samashti*</note> aspect.

Not to speak of themselves being wealthy, the sight of them gives wealth to others. The *Tantra of the Terrible One* <note>original: *Bhairava Tantra*</note> says that the Lotus Goddess and the Goddess of Speech never forsake them for three generations downwards.

---

¹ The Goddess of Eloquence <note>original: *Sarasvatî*</note>. The *Tradition of Bharadvaja* <note>original: *Bhâradvâja Smriti*</note> says She is the one who ever resides in the tongue of all beings, and who causes speech.
² The Goddess of Fortune <note>original: *Lakshmî*</note>: for them is all learning, wealth, and prosperity (see *Tantra of the Great Liberation* <note>original: *Mahânirvâna Tantra*</note>, *Chapter* vii. verse 50).
³ In other words, they become both rich and learned.

<summary>This page provides a deep theological commentary on the Goddess as the source of sound and speech, identifying her with the fifty letters of the alphabet and the various states of consciousness. It promises that the successful practitioner will be blessed with wisdom and wealth that extends to their descendants.</summary>
<keywords>Sarasvati, Lakshmi, sound-Brahman, fifty letters, states of consciousness, seed-syllables, Tantric commentary, spiritual liberation</keywords>
