<meta>This page transitions from the commentary on the sixth verse to the translation and detailed theological analysis of the seventh verse. It continues the exploration of the Goddess's iconography, specifically her nakedness, her three eyes, and her position atop the body of Shiva.</meta>

HYMN TO KÂLÎ

->7<-

O Mother, even a person of slow wit becomes a great poet when he meditates upon You—You who are clothed only in space,¹ the three-eyed ² Creatrix ³ of the three worlds, whose waist ⁴ is beautiful with a girdle made of many severed arms of dead men, and who rests upon the breast of a corpse,⁵ as Your throne.

### NOTES

¹ The <term>Goddess <note>original: *Devī*</note></term> is naked, as is the god Shiva, for, like Him, She is "clothed with space" <note>This refers to the term *Digambari*, meaning one whose only garment is the cardinal directions/space itself</note>, and is the <term>Great Void <note>original: *Mahâshûnya*</note></term> itself.

² <note>original: *Trinayanâm*</note>. Her three eyes represent the Sun, the Moon, and Fire. The *Tantra of the Great Liberation* <note>original: *Mahânirvâna Tantra*</note> says, ‘Three eyes are attributed to the <term>Dark Goddess <note>original: *Kâlikâ*</note></term> because She observes the whole world with eyes that are the Sun, the Moon, and the like’. Regarding the deeper meaning of these terms—which do not merely refer to physical celestial bodies or elements—see Arthur Avalon’s *The Serpent Power* and *Studies in the Mantra Scripture*.

³ <term>The Provider <note>original: *Vidhâtrim*</note></term>. She who provides both worldly enjoyment and ultimate liberation for all <term>living souls <note>original: *Jivas*</note></term>.

⁴ <note>original: *Nitamba*</note>, literally meaning the buttocks or hips, but the girdle spans the entire waist. Kālī is traditionally depicted wearing this specific girdle of human arms.

⁵ The corpse <note>original: *Shava*</note> represents Shiva because He is inactive, while it is His <term>Divine Power <note>original: *Shakti*</note></term> who performs all actions. "On the breast of a corpse" <note>original: *Shavahridi*</note> refers to Her being upon the breast of Shiva in the <term>inverted union <note>original: *Viparitarati*</note></term>. The Goddess is given the dominant position in Her union with Her consort because She is the <term>Doer <note>original: *Kartri*</note></term> (the active principle), and He is the <term>Enjoyer <note>original: *Bhoktâ*</note></term> (the inactive witness). According to the <term>Sankhya <note>One of the six orthodox schools of Hindu philosophy</note></term> system, the <term>Pure Consciousness <note>original: *Purusha*</note></term> is neither a producer nor produced, but is passive—a mere observer of the actions of <term>Nature <note>original: *Prakriti*</note></term>. It is not the masculine Consciousness that is active in the creation of the world, but rather the feminine Nature who, under His gaze, performs the dance of creation. Thus, the *Tantra of the Hunchback Goddess* <note>original: *Kubjikâ Tantra*</note> says: ‘It is not the Creator-god, but His Power, who creates; it is the Power of the Preserver, not the Preserver Himself, who protects; it is the Power of the Destroyer, not the Destroyer Himself, who withdraws all things. Their husbands are like dead bodies.’ For in terms of manifest power, they are entirely dependent on their feminine Power. Regarding the spiritual practice <note>original: *Sadhana*</note>, see the *Satisfaction of Life* <note>original: *Prânatoshinî*</note> page 622: ‘By meditating on the inverted union, one attains the state of ultimate liberation.’ Sometimes two corpses are depicted: the lower one being the eternally still Shiva, and the upper one being Shiva joined with Power in the act of creation. Similarly, the Goddess is often shown reclining on a couch made of five corpses, known as the <term>Great Dead <note>original: *Mahâpreta*</note></term>. These "Great Dead," whose <term>seed-syllable <note>original: *Bija*</note></term> is *Hsau*, are the five great deities: Sadashiva, Ishana, Rudra, Vishnu, and Brahma.

<summary>This page translates the seventh verse of the hymn, focusing on the iconography of Kālī as the active feminine principle (Shakti) who dominates the passive masculine principle (Shiva). The commentary explains the philosophical foundations of this imagery through the lens of Sāmkhya and Tantric theology.</summary>

<keywords>Kālī, Shakti, Shiva, Sāmkhya, Purusha, Prakriti, Viparitarati, Mahāshūnya, Mahāpreta, Tantra, creation, liberation</keywords>

It explains the transformation of a "dullard" into a "poet" through meditation and provides a deep theological justification for the Goddess's girdle of severed arms and her nakedness.</meta>

couch in the cremation ground,¹ you enjoy the company of <term>the Great Dissolver <note>original: *Mahâkâla*, an epithet of Shiva as the Lord of Time and Death</note></term>.²

### VII

### COMMENTARY

<margin>‘Dullard’ (<term>dull-minded <note>original: *Jadachetâh*</note></term>)</margin>
One whose mind is overwhelmed by a passion for worldly things.

<margin>‘Poet’ (<term>seer <note>original: *Kavîh*</note></term>)</margin>
A great <term>possessor of spiritual wisdom <note>original: *Jnânî*</note></term>.

<margin>‘Meditates’ (<term>contemplating <note>original: *Dhyâyan*</note></term>)</margin>
One who, in their mental vision, perceives You as <term>the embodiment of Truth, Consciousness, and Bliss <note>original: *Sachchidânan-darûpinî*</note></term>.

<margin>‘Whose loins’ (<term>adorned with a girdle of severed hands <note>original: *Bâhuprakara-krita-kânchî-parilasanni-tambâm*</note></term>)</margin>
At the end of each <term>aeon <note>original: *Kalpa*</note></term>, all <term>individual souls <note>original: *Jîvas*</note></term> abandon their physical bodies. They continue to exist in their "subtle bodies"—in which their respective <term>actions and consequences <note>original: *Karmas*</note></term> reside—and form part of the <term>spiritual ignorance <note>original: *Avidyâ*</note></term> contained within the causal body of the <term>Goddess as the Absolute <note>original: *Brahmarûpinî*</note></term>. There, they are associated with Her own <term>fundamental attributes <note>original: *Gunas*</note></term> until they are liberated at some future time after the start of the next cycle of creation. 

Because of this, the girdle decorating the loins, lower belly, and reproductive organs of the <term>Great Goddess in Her universal form <note>original: *Mahâdevî virâtrûpinî*</note></term>—who is capable of bringing forth all life—is fashioned from the arms and hands of dead souls. These arms and hands were their primary instruments for performing <term>work <note>original: *Karma*</note></term>. *The Wave of Bliss of the Shakti* <note>original: *Shâktânandataramginî*</note> says, "A soul is born with its actions, dies with its actions, and in the next body, those same actions remain attached to it." *The Song of the Goddess* <note>original: *Devîgîtâ*</note> says, "In Her, at the time of dissolution, all souls and their actions are merged into an undifferentiated mass, just as all <term>worldly activity <note>original: *Vyavahâra*</note></term> merges into <term>dreamless sleep <note>original: *Sushupti*</note></term>." Again, the Goddess says, "It is I who create the whole world and enter into it with <term>life-force <note>original: *Prâna*</note></term>, <term>creative illusion <note>original: *Mâyâ*</note></term>, <term>action <note>original: *Karma*</note></term>, and so forth."

<margin>Raimented with space (<term>naked <note>original: *Digvastrâm*</note></term>)</margin>
Clothing represents the covering of <term>illusion <note>original: *Mâyâ*</note></term>. She is without that covering and stands above all illusion.

---

¹ The site for certain forms of <term>spiritual practice <note>original: *Tantrik Sâdhana*</note></term>, such as the <term>corpse-posture <note>original: *Shavâsana*</note></term> or <term>skull-posture <note>original: *Mundâsana*</note></term>. Regarding these, the *Fetkârinî Tantra* says that the cremation ground is an excellent place for such discipline. "He who performs <term>mantra-repetition <note>original: *japa*</note></term> a specific number of times while seated on a corpse in a cremation ground attains all manner of <term>spiritual success <note>original: *Siddhi*</note></term>."
² <term>The Supreme Shiva <note>original: *Parama Shiva*</note></term>.

<summary>This page explains the esoteric symbolism of Kali’s appearance, specifically how her girdle of severed arms represents the accumulated karma of all souls at the end of a cosmic cycle. It also defines her nakedness as a symbol of being beyond the veil of worldly illusion (Maya).</summary>
<keywords>Karma, Kali, Maya, Individual Souls, Aeon, Cremation Ground, Spiritual Practice, Action, Dissolution, Girdle of Arms</keywords>

<meta>This page concludes the specific philosophical definitions of the Goddess's titles from the previous section—explaining her relationship to time, creation, and the Absolute—and begins the translation and commentary for Verse 8, which describes the setting of the cremation ground.</meta>

# HYMN TO KÂLÎ

<margin><term>Three-eyed <note>original: *Trinayanâm*</note></term></margin>
Having knowledge of the three divisions of Time: past, present, and future.

<margin><term>Creatrix <note>original: *Vidhâtrî*</note></term></margin>
She who, at the beginning of the next <term>aeon <note>original: *Kalpa*</note></term>, grants life and the enjoyment of experiences to <term>individual souls <note>original: *Jîvas*</note></term> according to their respective <term>accumulated past actions <note>original: *Sanchita Karma*</note></term>.

<margin>‘On the breast of a corpse’ (<term>Shavahridi</term>)</margin>
The corpse represents <term>the Absolute without attributes <note>original: *Nirguna Brahman*</note></term>. The couch is the <term>support or foundation <note>original: *Âdhâra*</note></term>. On the Absolute as Your foundation, You are established in Your own <term>state <note>original: *Pada*</note></term> as the Absolute. The *Gâyatrî Tantra* says, "By the word 'corpse' is indicated the Absolute as the <term>dead body <note>original: *Preta*</note></term>." The *Gandharva Tantra* says <term>the Eternal Shiva <note>original: *Sadâshiva*</note></term> is the couch on which lies the subtle form of the <term>Goddess of the Three Cities <note>original: *Tripurasundarî*</note></term>.

<margin>‘In the cremation ground’ (<term>Shmashânastha</term>)</margin>
The cremation ground (<term>Shmashâna</term>) is the <term>great ether <note>original: *Mahâkâsha*</note></term> in which all creatures are merged like corpses during the <term>Great Dissolution <note>original: *Mahâpralaya*</note></term>. In dissolution, even the greatest of creatures are but corpses, and hence the entire universe is a cremation ground.

<margin>‘Dost enjoy the Great Time’ (<term>Mahâkâlasurataprayuktâm</term>)</margin>
At the end of a cosmic age, there being no active creation and She being inactive, and there being nothing but the supreme Absolute, She is inseparable from the <term>Supreme Shiva <note>original: *Parashiva*</note></term> and experiences Herself as <term>unbroken <note>original: *Akhanda*</note></term> Bliss.

->8<-

Those who truly¹ meditate on Thee, the Spouse of <term>the Destroyer <note>original: *Hara*</note></term>,² who art seated in³ the cremation-ground strewn with funeral pyres, corpses, skulls, and bones, and haunted by female jackals howling fearfully; who art very youthful,⁴ and art in full

->NOTES<-

¹ The commentator K. B. notes that "truly" (<term>param</term>) means "rightly," or refers to meditation alone without the need for <term>meditative recitation <note>original: *japa*</note></term>.

² Shiva.

³ *Pravishtâm*, literally "entered."

⁴ <term>Very youthful <note>original: *Atiyuvatîm*</note></term>. She is without childhood or old age. The *Shâradâtilaka* <note>an important 11th-century Tantric text</note> says, "Although Thou art primordial, Thy youth is ever fresh."

<summary>This page provides a metaphysical interpretation of Kâlî's attributes, defining her as the mistress of time and the Absolute who rests upon the foundation of a motionless Shiva. It marks the transition to Verse 8, which describes the devotee's meditation on the Goddess within the symbolic and literal landscape of the cremation ground.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Goddess's presence in the cremation ground, finishing the notes on the previous verse before moving to Verse VIII. It focuses on the symbolic meaning of the "fearful" imagery surrounding Kālī, interpreting her appearance and surroundings as philosophical principles rather than literal horrors.</meta>

enjoyment upon¹ Thy Spouse, are revered by all and in all places.²

## VIII

### COMMENTARY

<margin>‘Meditate on’ (<term>Dhyâyanti</term>)</margin>
That is, to see with a mind that is steady and undisturbed.

<margin>‘Spouse of Hara’ (<term>Hara-badhûm</term>)</margin>
<term>Hara <note>original: *Hara*</note></term> is He who removes (<term>Harati</term>) the <term>threefold pains <note>original: *Âdhyâtmika, Âdhibhautika, Âdhidaivika*; these refer to sufferings caused by one's own body/mind, by other living beings, and by natural or supernatural disasters</note></term> of <term>individual souls <note>original: *Jîvas*</note></term>. His spouse is <term>Energy <note>original: *Shakti*</note></term>, she who grants liberation to souls and is the very embodiment of <term>Existence, Consciousness, and Bliss <note>original: *Sachchidânandarûpinî*</note></term>.

<margin>‘Hast entered’ (<term>Pravishtâm</term>)</margin>
Meaning, You are firmly established.

<margin>‘Flaming pyre’ (<term>Prakatita-chitâyâm</term>)</margin>
This refers to the <term>Power of Consciousness <note>original: *Chit-shakti*</note></term>, so named because She is self-manifesting, like a fire. The sacred text *Chandî* <note>The *Devī Māhātmya*, a central scripture of Goddess worship</note> speaks of "Her who pervades the whole universe as consciousness (<term>Chit</term>)."

<margin>‘Fearful’ (<term>Ghorâbhih</term>)</margin>
That is to say, those who are extremely powerful.

<margin>‘Jackals’ (<term>Shivâbhih</term>)</margin>
This refers to the <term>Great Elements <note>original: *Mahâbhûtas*</note></term> which are <term>auspicious <note>original: *Shiva*</note></term> before they have undergone the process of <term>quintuplication <note>original: *Panchîkrita*; the philosophical process by which subtle elements combine to form the physical world</note></term>.

<margin>‘Skulls & bones’ (<term>Mundâsthin-karaih</term>)</margin>
The white color of the skulls and bones represents the <term>Quality of Purity and Light <note>original: *Sattvaguna*</note></term>. Therefore, they are associated with the Purity and other qualities of the individual souls who have been dissolved back into the Divine during the <term>Great Dissolution <note>original: *Mahâpralaya*</note></term>.

<margin>‘Ever youthful’ (<term>Atiyuvatîm</term>)</margin>
This signifies that She is always the same: fresh, unchanging, and never decaying.

<margin>‘Satisfied with enjoyment’ (<term>Santushtâm-uparisuratena</term>)</margin>
She, having brought the <term>Supreme Shiva <note>original: *Parama Shiva*</note></term> under Her will, joyfully engages in the work of creating, preserving, and dissolving the universe. The *Nirvâna Tantra* says, "The <term>Left-Hand Goddess <note>original: *Vâmâ*</note></term> is the..."

---

¹ <note>original: *Santushtâm uparisuratena*</note> *This refers to the "reversed" sexual position where the Goddess is on top, symbolizing her active role as the creative power acting upon the passive Absolute. This is also called "reversed enjoyment." See the note on the previous verse for more detail.*

² *The commentator K. B. explains this literally: "They never suffer defeat or humiliation anywhere, in this world or the next."*

# HYMN TO KÂLÎ

She who grants Great Liberation after conquering the <term>Southern One <note>original: *Dakshina*; refers to Shiva in his auspicious aspect or as the one who sits on the right</note></term>. The *Gandharva Tantra* states: "She who is the Sun, Moon, and Fire, and the half of <term>Shiva <note>original: *Ha*</note></term>, places the <term>Universal Person <note>original: *Purusha*</note></term> beneath Her and enjoys him from above." The *Niruttara Tantra* says: "When <term>Kālī without attributes <note>original: *Nirgunâ*</note></term> becomes <term>Kālī with attributes <note>original: *Sagunâ*</note></term>, She engages in <term>reversed union <note>original: *Viparîtarati*; a symbolic posture where the Goddess is active atop the passive Shiva, representing the activation of consciousness by divine power</note></term>." The *Yogavâshishtha* in the section on <term>Final Liberation <note>original: *Nirvâna Prakarana*</note></term> says: "Natural unity is <term>Shiva <note>the Auspicious One</note></term>. Creation, by comparison, is unnatural." This means the <term>Great Goddess <note>original: *Mahâdevî*</note></term> is <term>Godhead without attributes <note>original: *Nirguna Brahman*</note></term> in Her <term>true essential nature <note>original: *Svarûpa*</note></term>, and the overturning of this essential nature is what causes creation.

<margin>
‘Nowhere’
<note>original: *Kvachidapi na*</note>

Humiliated
<note>original: *Paribhavah*</note>
</margin>

->In no birth.<-

That is, they are no longer subject to the cycle of birth, death, and rebirth, and instead attain <term>Final Liberation <note>original: *Nirvâna*</note></term>.

### 9

> What, indeed, O <term>Mother <note>original: *Jananî*</note></term>,¹ can we, with such dull minds, say of
> Thee, whose <term>Supreme Reality <note>original: *Paramam*</note></term>² not even the <term>Creator <note>original: *Dhâtâ*</note></term>,³ the <term>Lord <note>original: *Îsha*</note></term>,⁴ or the <term>Preserver <note>original: *Hari*</note></term>⁵
> know? Yet, despite our dullness and ignorance, our devotion
> towards Thee compels us to speak of Thee.⁶ Therefore, O <term>Dark Goddess <note>original: *Asite*</note></term>,⁷

## NOTES

1 <term>Mother <note>original: *Jananî*</note></term>; the origin of the three worlds.

2 <term>Supreme <note>original: *Paramam*</note></term>, or "reality" (according to the commentator K. B.).

3 <term>The Ordainer <note>original: *Dhâtâ*</note></term> is <term>Brahmā <note>the creator god</note></term> who distributes the results of <term>actions <note>original: *Karma*</note></term>. (Commentator V.)

4 <term>The Lord <note>original: *Îsha*</note></term> is <term>Shiva <note>the Auspicious One</note></term>, or <term>Rudra <note>the Howler</note></term>, who wields the power of divine sovereignty. (V)

5 <term>The Remover <note>original: *Hari*</note></term> is <term>Vishnu <note>the All-Pervasive One</note></term>, who takes away the threefold sufferings of <term>individual souls <note>original: *Jīvas*</note></term>. (V)

6 "Still, Your devotion makes us speak" <note>original: *Tathâpi tvadbhaktir mukharayati*</note>. "Still" <note>original: *Tathâpi*</note>: despite our ignorance (V). "Devotion to Thee" <note>original: *Tvadbhaktih*</note>: the urge to sing Your praises (V). "Makes us speak" <note>original: *Mukharayati*</note>: impels us to utter words in Your praise (V).

7 <term>Dark One <note>original: *Asite*</note></term>. While literal, the commentator V. notes this means the "Unlimited One." The *Mahâkâla-samhitâ* scripture says: "Unthinkable, unlimited, the <term>Divine Power <note>original: *Shakti*</note></term> itself, upon which everything manifested rests; beyond the three qualities of nature, free from the <term>pairs of opposites <note>original: *Dvandva*</note></term>, and understood only through the <term>higher intellect <note>original: *Buddhi*</note></term>: You alone are the <term>Supreme Reality <note>original: *Brahman*</note></term>." (V)

# IX

## COMMENTARY

<margin>‘Mother’</margin>
Of us all, including <term>the Creator <note>original: *Brahmâ*</note></term>, <term>the Preserver <note>original: *Vishnu*</note></term>, and <term>the Destroyer <note>original: *Rudra*</note></term>. In the <term>Hymn to the Goddess <note>original: *Devî Sûkta*</note></term>, the Preserver says, ‘One, subtle, and unchanged, and yet many, Thou dost give birth to millions of worlds. Who am I, the Preserver, and who is the other, <term>the Auspicious One <note>original: *Shiva*</note></term>, and who are the <term>Heavenly Beings <note>original: *Devas*</note></term> that we and they should be able to (fully) sing Thy praises?’ In the <term>Scripture of Markandeya <note>original: *Mârkandeya Purâna*</note></term>, the Creator says, ‘When the Preserver, <term>the Lord <note>original: *Îshvara*</note></term>, and myself owe our appearance to Thee, who has the power to (fitly) praise Thee?’ In the <term>Union of Vishnu <note>original: *Vishnuyâmala*</note></term>, the Preserver says to the Goddess, ‘Oh Mother, none know Thy supreme aspect. The heavenly ones therefore worship that <term>physical <note>original: *Sthûla*; literally "gross" or "material" as opposed to subtle</note></term> aspect of Thine in the form of <term>the Dark One <note>original: *Kâlî*</note></term> and the rest.’ The <term>Collection of the Great Lord of Time <note>original: *Mahâkâla-samhitâ*</note></term> says, ‘When <term>the Ordainer <note>original: *Dhâtâ*</note></term> was not, nor the Preserver, nor <term>Time <note>original: *Kâla*</note></term>, when the five <term>Elements <note>original: *Bhûtas*</note></term> were not, then Thou, the Cause, wast alone as the <term>Supreme Absolute <note>original: *Supreme Brahman*</note></term>, the Being of all that is.’

<margin>(Asite) ‘Unlimited’</margin>
She is not limited by the <term>Qualities of Nature <note>original: *Gunas*; the three fundamental threads of existence: harmony, passion, and inertia</note></term> and is <term>Beyond Attributes <note>original: *Nirgunâ*</note></term>.

# 10

> If by night,² Thy <term>devotee <note>original: *Bhaktah*</note></term>³ unclothed, with dishevelled hair,

## NOTES

¹ As one does not become angry with animals (<term>the uninitiated <note>original: *Pashu*; literally "animal" or "beast," referring here to those in a state of ignorance or limited consciousness</note></term> or ignorant men also called *Pashu*) because they do wrong, so do not be angry with us. It is, moreover, the part of the great to overlook the faults of their inferiors (Commentator K. B.).

² *Naktam*. At dead of night. The <term>Scripture of the Shrieking One <note>original: *Fetkârinî Tantra*</note></term> says, ‘By night, naked with dishevelled hair in union with <term>Divine Power <note>original: *Shakti*</note></term>, by him is all <term>spiritual attainment <note>original: *Siddhi*</note></term> gained’. The <term>Path of Kali <note>original: *Kâlikrama*</note></term> says, ‘The <term>uninitiated practitioner <note>original: *Pashu*</note></term> devoted to his own <term>mode of conduct <note>original: *Âchâra*</note></term> should recite his <term>sacred chant <note>original: *Mantra*</note></term> a hundred thousand times by day. The <term>heroic <note>original: *Vira*</note></term> or <term>divine <note>original: *Divya*</note></term> practitioner should recite it a hundred thousand times by night.’ The <term>Scripture of the Hunchback Goddess <note>original: *Kubjikâ Tantra*</note></term> says, ‘Such as are in the <term>state of the uninitiated <note>original: *Pashubhâva*</note></term> are but animals. They should not touch a rosary nor recite a sacred chant by night.’ (V)

³ *Bhaktah*. Here a <term>Heroic Practitioner <note>original: *Vira Sâdhaka*</note></term>. The <term>Highest Tantra <note>original: *Niruttara Tantra*</note></term> says, ‘The

# X

## COMMENTARY

<margin><term>Yoga of Dissolution <note>original: *Laya Yoga*</note></term></margin> is here described in this and following verses. <term>The Compendium of Gheranda <note>original: *Gheranda Samhitâ*</note></term> says, ‘One should become filled with the Goddess’s power by performing the <term>Seal of the Womb <note>original: *Yoni Mudrâ*</note></term>. One should be in the <term>Supreme Soul <note>original: *Paramâtmâ*</note></term> with the sweet <term>sentiment of love <note>original: *Shrimgârarasa*</note></term> and, being <term>full of bliss <note>original: *Ânandamaya*</note></term>, should unite with the <term>Absolute <note>original: *Brahman*</note></term>.’ <term>The Compendium of Goraksha <note>original: *Goraksha Samhitâ*</note></term> says, ‘Raising the <term>inner power <note>original: *Shakti*</note></term> with the <term>individual soul <note>original: *Jîva*</note></term> to the <term>thousand-petalled lotus <note>original: *Sahasrâra*</note></term> in the head, one should become filled with that power; and uniting with <term>the Auspicious One <note>original: *Shiva*</note></term>, should think of all forms of happiness and enjoyment.’ <term>The Wishing-Tree of Tantra <note>original: *Tantra Kalpadruma*</note></term> says, ‘One should meditate on the <term>Goddess Serpent-Power <note>original: *Devî Kundalinî*</note></term> as one's <term>chosen deity <note>original: *Ishtadevatâ*</note></term>, ever youthful, of the age of sixteen, full-breasted, dark, subtle, appearing as creation and <term>existing as the very soul of creation, maintenance, and dissolution <note>original: *Srishtisthitilayâtmikâ*</note></term>.’

---

‘The <term>practitioner <note>original: *Mantrin*</note></term> who has received <term>ritual consecration <note>original: *Abhisheka*</note></term> should perform the <term>worship of the lineage <note>original: *Kulapûjâ*</note></term>. Oh Devi, the <term>sacred formula <note>original: *Mantra*</note></term> of Kālī does not become <term>effective <note>original: *Siddha*</note></term> without the <term>way of the Kula tradition <note>original: *Kulâchâra*</note></term>.’ (V) <note>V likely refers to the commentator Vimalānanda.</note>

$^1$ <term>Meditating on Thee <note>original: *Tvâng dhyâyan*</note></term>. Mentally seeing Thee in his heart as ever in the enjoyment of union with <term>the Great Lord of Time <note>original: *Mahâkâla*</note></term>. (V)

$^2$ Thy <term>sacred formula <note>original: *Mantra*</note></term> is the aforementioned great formula <note>referring to the specific syllables of the Kālī-mantra discussed in earlier verses</note>. (V)

$^3$ He is <term>attached to the ritual union <note>original: *Ratâsakta*</note></term>, the meaning of which is as follows: <term>He recites the mantra when he is engaged in sexual union with a beautiful woman who acts as the embodiment of the Goddess's power <note>original: *Sa mantram japati yadâ sa shobhanâmgapratyamgashâlinyâ manohârinyâ yuvatyâ shaktyâ saha maithunâsakto bhavati*</note></term>. Whilst in union, the mind must be concentrated on <term>the Goddess Kālī <note>original: *Devî Kâlî*</note></term> and <term>silent recitation <note>original: *japa*</note></term> must be done of Her <term>Great Formula <note>original: *Mahâmantra*</note></term>. The devotee should not think of anything else.

$^4$ So also <term>the Tantra of the Screeching Goddess <note>original: *Fetkârinî Tantra*</note></term> (chapter 10) says: 
> <term>At night, lying naked and engaged in ritual union, or else with disheveled hair, through this all spiritual attainments are reached: the power of paralyzing, the power of deluding, and the power of subjugating <note>original: *Râtrau nagnah shayânash cha maithune cha vyavasthitah, Athavâ muktakeshash cha tena syuh sarvvasiddhayah Stambhanam mohanang chaiva vashikaranam eva cha.*</note></term>.

Here “or else” means if the <term>practitioner <note>original: *Sâdhaka*</note></term> is without a <term>ritual partner <note>original: *Shakti*</note></term>; then recitation of the formula with disheveled hair gives the same <term>attainment <note>original: *siddhi*</note></term>.

$^5$ <term>Seer <note>original: *Kavi*</note></term>, which does not here have the limited sense of a “Poet,” <note>implying one with divine vision or spiritual insight</note>.

It cites various Tantric texts to explain the internal process of raising Kundalinī and the external ritual conditions required for spiritual success.</summary>

## COMMENTARY

<margin><term>Thy devotee <note>original: *Bhaktah*</note></term></margin>
Here it refers to the <term>Divine Practitioner <note>original: *Divya Sâdhaka*</note></term> who is a <term>Yogi <note>original: *Yogin*</note></term>.

<margin><term>By night <note>original: *Naktam*</note></term></margin>
That is, awaking in the <term>Knowledge of the Absolute <note>original: *Brahmavidyâ*</note></term> which (though it is pure Light) is seen as darkness by all ordinary creatures. The <term>Song of the Lord <note>original: *Bhagavadgîtâ*</note></term> says, ‘The self-controlled man awakes in what is night to all creatures.’

<margin><term>Naked <note>original: *Vivâsâh*</note></term></margin>
That is, stripped of the covering of <term>Illusion <note>original: *Mâyâ*</note></term>; meaning one who is spiritually awakened.

<margin><term>Dishevelled hair <note>original: *Galita-chikurah*</note></term></margin>
That is, with a mind free from all restlessness. The word <term>Chikura <note>original: *Chikura*</note></term> means both "hair" and "restless."

<margin><term>Meditating <note>original: *Dhyâyan*</note></term></margin>
Meditating on Thee as being in the enjoyment of the <term>Bliss of Equal Essence <note>original: *Sâmarasya*</note></term> with the <term>Supreme Shiva <note>original: *Paramashiva*</note></term>.

<margin><term>Attached to delight <note>original: *Ratâsaktâm*</note></term></margin>
By performing the <term>Dissolution <note>original: *Laya*</note></term> (merging) of the <term>individual soul <note>original: *Jîvâtmâ*</note></term> into the <term>Coiled Power <note>original: *Kundalinî Shakti*</note></term>, who is the ever-youthful, all-pervading Mother and Preserver of all living beings. The creative and nourishing function of this Power is symbolized by the descriptions ‘heavy-hipped’ and ‘full-breasted.’

->11<-

*O Spouse of <term>the Destroyer <note>original: *Hara*</note></term>,¹ should a practitioner daily ² recite Thy <term>sacred sound-formula <note>original: *mantra*</note></term> for the space of a year, meditating the while ³ with knowledge of its meaning ⁴ upon Thee, intent ⁵ upon Thy union ⁶ with—*

->NOTES<-

¹ <term>Shiva <note>the Auspicious One</note></term>.
² <term>Always <note>original: *Sadâ*</note></term>: This means ‘always,’ but in this context signifies ‘daily’ (according to the commentary by K.B.).
³ <term>Having meditated <note>original: *Vichintya*</note></term>: This refers to one who has mentally contemplated the letters of the <term>Seed-syllable <note>original: *Bija*</note></term> and their inner meaning. This is known as <term>mental repetition <note>original: *Mânasa japa*</note></term>, which is defined in the <term>Ancient Tale of the Man-Lion <note>original: *Narasimha Purâna*</note></term> (cited in Raghunandana’s <term>Rites of Daily Conduct <note>original: *Âhnikâchâratattva*</note></term>) as the silent repetition in the mind, letter by letter and syllable by syllable, of the formula while meditating on its significance.
⁴ This refers to the <term>arrangement of letters <note>original: *Varna-samsthâna*</note></term>, their placement, their meaning, and so forth.
⁵ <term>Having become steady <note>original: *Susthibhûya*</note></term>: This describes one whose senses are no longer distracted by any external objects (according to the commentary by K.B.).
⁶ <term>Intense union with the Great Lord of Time <note>original: *Atishayamahâkâlasuratâm*</note></term>.

It also defines the practice of mental mantra repetition (mânasa japa) as a disciplined focus on the inner meaning of sacred sounds.</summary>

<header>HYMN TO KĀLĪ</header>

the <term>Great Lord of Time <note>original: Mahâkâla</note></term>, above whom Thou art,$^1$ then such a <term>Sage <note>original: Vidvân</note></term>$^2$ has every pleasure that he wills upon the earth,$^3$ and holds all <term>Great Spiritual Powers <note>original: Siddhi</note></term>$^4$ in the grasp of his lotus-like hands.

## XI

### COMMENTARY

<margin><term>Spouse of Hara <note>original: Harabadhû</note></term></margin> The Charmer of the <term>Great Lord of Time <note>original: Mahâkâla</note></term>.

<margin><term>Mentally recite <note>original: Vichintya japati</note></term></margin> The <term>Kaulâvalî Tantra</term> says that <term>Mental Recitation <note>original: Mânasa Japa</note></term> is a hundred times more efficacious than <term>Verbal Recitation <note>original: Vâchika Japa</note></term>.

According to the commentator <term>Durgârâma</term>, the words may also mean ‘recite’ while keeping in mind the <term>Meaning <note>original: Artha</note></term> and purpose of the <term>Sacred Sound <note>original: Mantra</note></term>. For it is said that he who does not know the <term>Meaning <note>original: Artha</note></term> of the <term>Sacred Sound</term>, the <term>Living Consciousness <note>original: Chaitanya</note></term> of the <term>Sacred Sound</term>, and the <term>Gesture of the Source <note>original: Yoni-mudrâ; a ritual hand gesture symbolizing the womb or origin</note></term> will find no <term>Success <note>original: Siddhi</note></term> even if he recites the <term>Sacred Sound</term> a million times.

<margin><term>Unperturbed mind <note>original: Susthî-bhûya</note></term></margin> The <term>Kulârnava Tantra</term> instructs thus:—‘Beloved, when performing the <term>Recitation <note>original: Japa</note></term> of a <term>Sacred Sound</term>, one should be calm, pure, moderate in food, reverent, self-controlled, unaffected by the <term>Pairs of Opposites <note>original: Dvandva; such as pleasure and pain</note></term>, steady of mind, silent, and self-disciplined.’

<margin><term>Meditating on Thee <note>original: Vichintya tvâm</note></term></margin> The <term>Kaulâvalî Tantra</term> says, ‘One should meditate upon the Spouse of <term>Shiva</term> before recitation, and after meditation should again recite.’ The <term>Practitioner <note>original: Sâdhaka</note></term> who performs recitation and meditation together soon attains success.

<margin><term>Upon Him <note>original: Vipârîtâm</note></term></margin> [The original word is ‘<term>Inverted <note>original: Vipârîtah</note></term>’ in the masculine case; <term>Durgârâma</term> therefore interprets it as an adjective describing the <term>Practitioner</term> who, he says, unites with his <term>Feminine Power <note>original: Shakti</note></term> in <term>Inverted Union <note>original: Viparita Maithuna</note></term>. <term>Vimalânanda</term>, however, reads it as ‘<term>Inverted <note>original: Vipârîtâm</note></term>’ in the feminine case, making it an adjective describing ‘Thee’ (the Goddess) who is the object of meditation.]

---

$^1$ <term>Inverted <note>original: Vipârîtâm</note></term> (referring to the Goddess's position atop Shiva; see verse 7, note 5.)
$^2$ <term>Sage <note>original: Vidvân</note></term> whose sole aim is <term>Liberation <note>original: Moksha</note></term>.
$^3$ Literally ‘wandering freely on Earth’ (Commentary, K.B.)
$^4$ <term>Spiritual Attainments <note>original: Siddhi</note></term> (see earlier, p. 38.)

It also explains the symbolic "inverted" relationship between the Goddess and Shiva during meditation and ritual.</summary>

<page-num>53</page-num>
<header>COMMENTARY 53</header>

<margin><term>Great Powers <note>original: Mahâsiddhi-nivahâ</note></term></margin>
Such as that by which is gained the states of <term>Living in the Divine Realm <note>original: Sâlokya</note></term>, <term>Taking the Divine Form <note>original: Sârûpya</note></term>, <term>Union with the Divine <note>original: Sâyujya</note></term> and <term>Final Liberation <note>original: Nirvâna; the ultimate release from the cycle of rebirth</note></term>.

### 12

O Mother, Thou givest birth to and protectest the world, and at the time of dissolution dost withdraw to Thyself¹ the earth and all things; therefore Thou art the <term>Creator <note>original: Brahmâ</note></term>, and the Lord of the three worlds, the <term>Spouse of the Goddess of Prosperity <note>original: Shrî; refers here to Vishnu</note></term>,² and the <term>Great Lord <note>original: Mahesha; refers to Shiva</note></term>,³ and all other beings and things.⁴ Ah Me! how, then, shall I praise Thy greatness?

### XII

# COMMENTARY

<margin><term>Dost withdraw <note>original: Samharati</note></term></margin>
That is, Thou dost make the world lose itself in Thy <term>Causal Body <note>original: Kârana; the subtle seed-state of the universe</note></term>.

<margin><term>The Creator <note>original: Dhâtâ</note></term></margin>
She is the creative <term>Power <note>original: Shakti</note></term> of the god Brahmâ.

<margin><term>Husband of the Goddess of Prosperity <note>original: Shripathih</note></term></margin>
She is the preservative <term>Power <note>original: Shakti</note></term> of the god <term>Vishnu</term>, whose spouse is <term>Prosperity <note>original: Shrî or Lakshmî</note></term>.

<margin><term>The Great Lord <note>original: Mahesha</note></term></margin>
She is the dissolving <term>Power <note>original: Shakti</note></term> of the god <term>Rudra <note>a fierce form of Shiva</note></term>.

<margin><term>All things <note>original: Samastam</note></term></margin>
Thou art both the material <note>the substance</note> and instrumental cause <note>the maker</note> of the world. The <term>Hymn of the Three Cities <note>original: Triputâ Stotra</note></term> says, "Thou art Earth, the Creator, and—"

# NOTES

¹ It is commonly said that She destroys, but it is not so. A <term>Deity <note>original: Devatâ</note></term> does not destroy <note>original: *Na devo nâshakah*</note>. Man does. She simply takes back what She has put forth.

² *The god Vishnu, husband of the goddess Lakshmi.*

³ *Shiva. The <term>Trinity <note>original: Trimûrti; the triad of Brahma, Vishnu, and Shiva</note></term> is, in fact, Her manifestation.*

⁴ <note>original: *Prâyah sakalam api*</note>, that is, all moving and unmoving things (Commentary by K.B.). For the <term>Goddess <note>original: Devî</note></term> is <term>She whose form is the Universe <note>original: Vishvarûpinî</note></term>. She is the objective world, <term>composed of matter <note>original: jadâtmikâ</note></term> (from the <term>Thousand Names of the Goddess <note>original: Lalitâ Sahasranama</note></term>, verse 90), as well as its Cause.

<summary>This page explains Verse XII of the hymn, identifying the Goddess as the supreme power (Shakti) behind the Hindu Trinity and the physical universe. It clarifies that "destruction" is actually a process of the world returning to Her causal form.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Hymn to Kālī. While the previous page established the Goddess as the source of the Hindu Trinity’s powers (Creation, Preservation, and Destruction), this page extends that identification to the elemental world (Water, Fire, Air) and introduces Verse 13, which emphasizes exclusive devotion to the Mother over all other celestial beings.</meta>

<header>HYMN TO KĀLĪ</header>

Creatrix of the world. Thou art also Water, <term>the Preserver <note>original: Vishnu</note></term>, and Preserver of the world. And thou art Fire, <term>the Destroyer <note>original: Rudra</note></term> and the Dissolver of the world. As the Air of the world thou art <term>Divine Sovereignty <note>original: Aishvarya</note></term>.' Another <term>Hymn of Praise <note>original: Stotra</note></term> says, 'She assumes three forms of body for the purpose of creation, maintenance and dissolution. The world being constituted of the <term>Three Qualities <note>original: Gunas; Sattva (purity), Rajas (activity), and Tamas (inertia)</note></term>, <term>the Creator <note>original: Brahmâ</note></term>, <term>the Preserver <note>original: Vishnu</note></term> and <term>the Destroyer <note>original: Rudra</note></term> are Her <term>Transformations <note>original: Vikritis; modifications of the original power</note></term>.'

->13<-

O Mother, people there are who worship many other <term>Gods <note>original: Devas</note></term> than Thyself.$^1$ They are greatly ignorant, and know nothing of the high <term>Truth <note>original: Tattvam</note></term>,$^2$ (but I) of my own uncontrollable$^3$ desire for Thee approach Thee, the <term>Primordial Power <note>original: Âdyâ</note></term>,$^4$ who dost deeply enjoy the great Bliss arising from union (with <term>the Auspicious One <note>original: Shiva</note></term>),$^5$ and who art worshipped by <term>the Preserver <note>original: Hari</note></term>, <term>the Destroyer <note>original: Hara</note></term>, <term>the Creator <note>original: Virinchi</note></term>, and all other <term>Gods <note>original: Devas</note></term>.$^6$

### NOTES

1 That is, thinking that other gods grant greater boons (Commentary by Krishnananda Bhadracharya). Compare also what the philosopher Shamkarâchârya says about the worship of other gods in the fourth <term>verse <note>original: shloka</note></term> of the *Hymn for Forgiveness of Sins against the Goddess*, and see the *Shrimad Devi Bhagavatam* (Book V, Chapter 19) (Hymn to <term>the Mother of the World <note>original: Jagadambikâ</note></term>).

2 *Paramam*, that is, the essential *Truth*.

3 For he is a <term>devotee <note>original: Bhakta</note></term> whose desire for Her is so great that he cannot control it, but is instead controlled by it. 

$^4$ *Âdyâ*. <note>The first or beginningless power.</note>

5 *Ratirasamahânandaniratâm*. The Goddess delights in creation, which is the fruit of Her union with the <term>Pure Consciousness <note>original: Purusha; the masculine principle represented by Shiva</note></term>. 'Great Bliss,' for, as on the physical plane <note>original: "yadrûpang paramânandam tan nâsti bhuvanatraye" (Mâtrikâbheda Tantra, chap. ii); translated: "that form which is supreme bliss exists nowhere else in the three worlds"</note>, it is the counterpart on that plane of the ecstatic union which produced the Universe itself. It is the reflection of the higher Bliss attainable even here by the union of <term>Shiva and Shakti <note>the male and female divine principles</note></term> (in the form of the <term>Serpent Power <note>original: Kundalinî</note></term>) in the <term>Crown Chakra <note>original: Sahasrâra; the energy center at the top of the head</note></term>. Some read *Rasikâm* <note>one who enjoys flavor/emotion</note> for *Niratâm* <note>one who is absorbed in</note>.

6 *Vishnu, Shiva, and Brahmâ*. What, then, is the use of praying to the Trinity when they themselves worship Her? (Commentary by Krishnananda Bhadracharya). Compare also the *Devîbhâgavata* scripture, in the same place mentioned before. The Goddess is the Mother of all, from <term>the Creator <note>original: Brahmâ</note></term> to the lowliest worm (<note>original: "Âbrahmâkhilajananî"; translated: "The Mother of everything beginning with Brahmā"</note>, *Lalitâ Sahasranāma* name 67).

<summary>This page concludes the identification of Kālī with the elemental forces and the Hindu Trinity, before presenting Verse 13. This verse asserts that true devotees seek the Goddess alone, as She is the source of the very gods others worship, and the source of the cosmic bliss arising from divine union.</summary>
<keywords>Kālī, Gunas, Hindu Trinity, Shiva-Shakti union, Kundalinî, Sahasrâra, exclusive devotion, Primordial Power, creation and dissolution</keywords>

## COMMENTARY

<margin>‘Deluded’ <note>original: Vimûdhâh</note> vs. ‘Enlightened’ <note>original: Vibudhaih</note></margin>That is, those who are devoid of the power of spiritual discrimination.

O Kālī, spouse of the <term>Lord of the Mountain <note>original: Girisha</note></term>,¹ Thou art Earth, Water, Fire, Air and Ether.² Thou art all. Thou art one and beneficent.³ What can be said in praise of Thee, O Mother? Of Thy mercy show Thy favour towards me, helpless as I am. By Thy grace may I never be reborn.⁴

### NOTES

1 The Lord who inhabits the mountain, whereas *Girîsha* would mean the Lord of the mountain itself.

2 The <term>Ancient History of the Emblem <note>original: Limgapurâna</note></term> says, ‘The Goddess becomes matter’ <note>original: Kshetra</note>. She is <term>of the nature of the field <note>original: Kshetrasvarûpâ</note></term>, that is, the field or matter which is known by the <term>soul <note>original: Kshetrajna</note></term>. See the <term>Thousand Names of the Playful Mother <note>original: Lalitâ Sahasranâma</note></term> (fourth hundred) for the <term>Ultimate Reality <note>original: Brahman</note></term> who creates the visible world and then enters into it <note>original: Tat srishtvâ tad evânuprâbishat</note>.

3 <term>Beneficent <note>original: Kalyânî</note></term>. According to the <term>Lotus History <note>original: Padma Purâna</note></term>, the Goddess is worshipped as the Beneficent One in the Malaya Mountains.

4 ‘May I never be reborn’ <note>original: Bhavam anu na bhûyân mama januh</note>, that is, the state of being liberated from the cycle of birth and death. The <term>Secrets of the Dark Goddess <note>original: Shyâmârahasya</note></term> reads this as ‘May he not experience the world,’ using <term>the world <note>original: bhavam</note></term> as meaning <term>pain <note>original: duhkham</note></term> arising from <term>worldly existence <note>original: bhava</note></term>.

<margin>‘Kālī’</margin>
Dispeller of the fear of <term>Time or Death <note>original: Kāla</note></term>.

<margin>‘Thou art Earth’ <note>original: Dharitrî kilâlangshuchirapi samîropi gagan am; translates to: "Indeed Earth, Fire, Air, and Ether too."</note></margin>
The <term>Tantra of the Secret Ocean <note>original: Guptârnava Tantra</note></term> says, ‘Thou art Earth, Thou art Water, Thou art Fire, Thou art the Air of the world, Thou art Ether, Thou art Mind as the <term>Sensory Mind <note>original: Manas</note></term>, <term>Ego <note>original: Ahaṅkāra</note></term>, <term>Universal Intellect <note>original: Mahat/Buddhi</note></term> and Thou art <term>Primal Nature <note>original: Prakriti</note></term>. Thou art also, Oh Mother, the <term>Self <note>original: Âtmâ</note></term>. Thou art the Supreme. Nothing is greater than Thee. Oh <term>Goddess <note>original: Devi</note></term> of terrible form showing Thy teeth may my sins be forgiven me.’ The <term>Hymn to the Triple Beauty <note>original: Triputâ Stotra</note></term> also says, ‘Thou art the <term>Supportive Power <note>original: Âdhâra Shakti</note></term> and the <term>Foundational Support <note>original: Âdhâra</note></term>. Without a second.

<margin>Beneficent <note>original: Kalyânî</note></margin>
Because She grants <term>Final Liberation <note>original: Nirvâna</note></term> to <term>Individual Souls <note>original: Jîvas</note></term>.

<margin>‘Spouse of the Lord of the Mountain’ <note>original: Girisharamanî</note></margin>

Though <term>Changeless <note>original: Nirvikârâ</note></term> Thou dost appear as the twenty-four <term>Principles of Existence <note>original: Tattvas</note></term>, namely, Earth and the rest, through Thy <term>Creative Illusion <note>original: Mâyâ</note></term>. The <term>Hymn to the Goddess <note>original: Devîsûkta</note></term> of the <term>Rig Veda</term> says, ‘Thou who art one and many, subtle and the <term>Gross Transformations <note>original: Vikâras</note></term>, and giveth birth to millions of universes.’

## XV

### COMMENTARY

<margin>‘And’ <note>original: Tu</note></margin>
For <term>Divine Seekers <note>original: Divya Sâdhakas; refers to the highest class of tantric practitioners characterized by pure, god-like qualities</note></term>.

<margin>‘Mahâkâlî’</margin>
or <term>She who is the form of the Supreme Brahman <note>original: Parabrahmarûpinî</note></term>.

<margin>‘Cremation-ground’ <note>original: Smashânasthah</note></margin>
The cremation-ground is the <term>Supreme Brahman <note>original: Parabrahman</note></term> into which in the <term>Great Dissolution <note>original: Mahâpralaya</note></term> all beings go as though corpses. ‘In the cremation-ground’ therefore, means devoted to the Supreme Brahman.

<margin>Naked <note>original: Dikpatadharah; literally "clothed in the directions"</note></margin>
That is, free from the covering of <term>Illusion <note>original: Mâyâ</note></term>; whose Consciousness is untainted.

<margin>‘Meditates on Thee’ <note>original: Dhyânaniratah</note></margin>
That is, upon Thy <term>Being-Consciousness-Bliss <note>original: Sachchidânanda</note></term> aspect. The <term>Rudrayâmala Tantra <note>original: Rudrayâmala</note></term> says, ‘He who follows the <term>Noble Path <note>original: Kula path</note></term> should do <term>mantra-repetition <note>original: Japa</note></term> seeking protection from the <term>Goddess <note>original: Devî</note></term> who is Consciousness.’

¹ <note>original: Arka = Sun flowers known as Âkanda (V) not the flower so called in English.</note> <note>These are the flowers of the Calotropis gigantea, a plant sacred to the sun.</note>

² <note>original: Nijagalitaviryena kusumam. Thus the offering is not only of the flowers of the Âkanda plant, yatah sâdhakah devyai svavîryamishritârkapushpâni samarpayati.</note> <note>Translation: "Flowers with his own fallen seed." Thus the offering is not only of the flowers of the Akanda plant, "because the practitioner offers sun-flowers mixed with his own seed to the Goddess."</note> The <term>Durgârâma Siddhântavâgîsha <note>a noted commentator</note></term> cites the <term>Great Compendium of Time <note>original: Mahâkâlasamhitâ</note></term> as saying that the <term>sun-flower <note>original: sûryapushpa</note></term> should be offered in the same way with <term>repetition <note>original: japa</note></term> of the <term>root-mantra <note>original: mûlamantra</note></term> (<term>sun-flowers mixed with one's own seed <note>original: svavîryamishrita-sûryapushpâni</note></term>). The <term>seed <note>original: vīrya</note></term> does not refer to the sap of the plant. <term>Self-source <note>original: Nija</note></term> refers to the practitioner. ‘Along with, that is dipped in or that is spread over with.’ The *Great Compendium of Time* says, ‘A <term>practitioner of the Kula path <note>original: Kaula Sâdhaka</note></term> in the cremation-ground, naked, dishevelled and with tranquil mind, should offer a thousand sun-flowers with seed reciting the while his Mantra. After meditating and worshipping with great devotion he should recite the Hymn’ (V).

<header>HYMN TO KÂLÎ</header>

Bliss and Source of knowledge, who is all <term>principles of reality <note>original: Tattvas; the fundamental building blocks of the universe in Tantric philosophy</note></term> whose refulgence is that of millions of flashes of lightning.’

<margin>‘Sunflowers’ (<note>original: Arkânâm</note>)</margin>
Flowers of feeling such as compassion, forgiveness and so forth which are functions of the Mind called the Sun in the <term>opening of Brahman <note>original: Brahmarandhra; the spiritual center or "crown chakra" at the top of the head</note></term>. The <term>Tantra of the Collection of Knowledge <note>original: Jnânasamkalinî Tantra</note></term> says, ‘Oh Beloved, the mind is seated on the surface of the sun and life on that of the moon.’ The <term>Collection of Yajnavalkya <note>original: Yâjnavalkya Samhitâ</note></term> says, ‘The Moon is known to be in the <term>left channel <note>original: Idâ</note></term> and the sun in the <term>right channel <note>original: Piṁgala</note></term> (<term>energy channel <note>original: Nâdî</note></term>).’

<margin>Self-produced Seed (<note>original: Nijagalita-vîryena</note>)</margin>
This <term>Seed <note>original: Bîja; here referring to vital essence or creative power</note></term> is here the nectar which naturally flows from the thousand-petalled Lotus. The <term>Tantra of the Great Liberation <note>original: Mahânirvâna Tantra</note></term> says, ‘The Heart-Lotus should be offered for seat, the nectar (<term>immortality-fluid <note>original: Amrita</note></term>) shed from the <term>thousand-petalled crown center <note>original: Sahasrâra</note></term> for water to wash the feet, the mind as the offering (<term>ritual water <note>original: Arghya</note></term>), Memory (<term>consciousness <note>original: Chitta</note></term>) is offered by way of flowers, and the vital airs (<term>life-force <note>original: Prâna</note></term>) as and by way of incense.’ The <term>Tantra of the Collection of Knowledge <note>original: Jnânasaṁkalinî Tantra</note></term> says, ‘Libation (<term>water-offering <note>original: Tarpana</note></term>) to the Supreme Liberatrix should be made from out the vessel of the Moon and <term>ritual offering <note>original: Arghya</note></term> should be given from out the vessel of the Sun. Compassion, wisdom, and forgiveness are flowers as is also control of the senses. So too are charity (<term>kindness <note>original: Dayâ</note></term>) and religious merit. Non-injury (<term>harmlessness <note>original: Ahimsâ</note></term>) to any being is an excellent flower. Bliss is a flower and so too is the worship of the <term>spiritual practitioner <note>original: Sâdhaka</note></term>. Whoever offers these ten flowers attains to the feet of the Liberatrix.’ In this verse <term>meditation with distinction <note>original: Savikalpasamâdhiyoga; a state of deep trance where the practitioner still maintains a sense of "I" and "the Divine"</note></term> is indicated.

<meta>This page continues the technical and esoteric commentary on the Hymn to Kālī. Having discussed internal yogic offerings on the previous page, it now addresses a specific ritual verse involving the offering of hair. It presents various layers of interpretation: a literal reading (a wife's hair), a tantric sexual interpretation (hair from ritual union), and a hint toward a deeper symbolic meaning.</meta>

# COMMENTARY

hair of his **female partner**,¹ <note>original: Shakti; here referring to a ritual partner or wife who embodies the Goddess</note> in the cremation-ground,² becomes a great

¹ The offering is stated in the words <note>original: "grihe sammārjanyā parigalitabījam hi chikurang samūlang madhyanhe vitarati chitāyāng kujadine"</note> "At midday on a Tuesday, he offers in the funeral pyre the hair, along with the root, whose seed has been dropped by the broom in the house." These words have received various interpretations, of which the two chief alternatives are given. **In the house** <note>original: Grihe</note> is by some translated as "at home," in distinction from the cremation-ground to which, according to this rendering, the **practitioner** <note>original: sādhaka</note> subsequently goes to make his offering. This, however, is said to be erroneous, as the **ritual practice** <note>original: sādhana</note> takes place not in the house but in the cremation-ground. Others (see Calcutta edition) translate it as the equivalent of **wife** <note>original: grihinī</note>. **Broom** <note>original: Sammārjanī</note> is by them read to mean "comb." **Dropped** <note>original: Parigalita</note> is translated "removed," in the sense that the curling of the hair of the wife is "removed" or straightened with the comb. **Seed** <note>original: Bījam</note> given either its primary meaning, or as the equivalent of **vital force** <note>original: vīrya</note> is said to mean **curliness** <note>original: kautilyam</note> of the hair. **Hair** <note>original: Chikuram</note> is "hair," and **with the root** <note>original: samūlam</note> qualifies it, meaning pulled out, taken off at the root. The meaning is, then, an offering is made of the wife’s hair, the curls of which have been straightened out with the comb, and some of which has come off at the root. 

The correct rendering, however, is according to the commentator Kaula-Brahmananda: "In the 'house' of the female partner and the practitioner (referring to the sexual center) at the time of sexual union, by the friction of the female and male organs, the hair fallen from the partner's organ, smeared with vital fluid, is offered to the Goddess." **In the house** <note>original: Grihe</note> thus does not mean "at home," but **the house of the god of love** <note>original: manmathagrihe</note>. The hair is from the same. **Broom** <note>original: Sammārjanī</note> is here a metaphor for the **male organ** <note>original: Shishna</note>. **With the root** qualifies **hair** in the sense of "come off at the root" under the circumstances stated. **Dropped** <note>original: Parigalita</note> is "dropped"—referring to the **vital fluid** <note>original: vīrya</note>.

According to Vimalānanda, **In the house, the vital fluid dropped** refers to that produced by union with the practitioner’s **own partner** <note>original: svashakti</note> or wife.

Of the words "In the house, the hair with the root, whose vital fluid has dropped by the broom," the commentator Durgārāma Siddhāntavāgīsha gives the two following alternative expressions:—(a) **Broom** means with a comb with which the hair is put in order. **Hair of which the vital force (crookedness) has been removed** means hair of which the curliness has been removed. **In the house** means in the wife: for it is said the wife is the home. The whole phrase then means: the wife’s hair, root and all, combed out straight with a comb. Or (b) **Vital fluid dropped by the broom** means the **seed** <note>original: Shukra</note> produced by the "broom," here meaning the **male organ** <note>original: Limga</note> of the practitioner; **in the house** means in the abode of **Desire** <note>original: Kāma</note>—that is, the **female organ** <note>original: Yoni</note> of the **female partner**—together with hair, root and all.

The English translation is somewhat abbreviated with the object of giving only so much as all renderings are agreed upon. But in practice **vital fluid** <note>original: Vīrya</note> is used by most in its literal sense; this is the gross meaning. The inner sense is given in the **Explanation of the True Form** <note>original: Svarūpa-vyākhyā</note> which follows.

² According to some, the offering is made on the built-up pyre, and,

## XVI

### COMMENTARY

<margin>**Kālī** <note>original: "Kâlî"</note>: The one who dispels the fear of **Time** or **Death** <note>original: "Kâla"</note></margin>

<margin>**Whoever** <note>original: "Whoever"</note>: Here referring to a **Divine Practitioner** <note>original: "Divya Sâdhaka"</note></margin>

<margin>**Midday** <note>original: "Madhyâhne"</note>: At noon.</margin>

<margin>**Devotion** <note>original: "Premnâ"</note>: That is, **Supreme Devotion** <note>original: "Parabhakti"</note></margin>

<margin>**Offers** <note>original: "Vitarati"</note>: Merges into Thee; that is, attains **Absorption without Distinctions** <note>original: "Nirvikalpa Samâdhi"</note>. The **Aphorisms of Patanjali** <note>original: "Pâtanjala Sûtra"</note> state that this state of absorption is attained by the suppression of the **modifications of the mind** <note>original: "Vritti"</note>.</margin>

<margin>**In the cremation-ground** <note>original: "Chitâyâm"</note>: Within Thee as **Pure Consciousness** <note>original: "Chit"</note></margin>

<margin>**Seed** <note>original: "Bija"</note>: This refers here to the nectar which flows during the enjoyment of the union of the **Serpent Power** <note>original: "Kulakundalinî"</note> and the **Supreme Shiva** <note>original: "Paramashiva"</note>. The **Gandharvamālikā Tantra** says, "O beloved One, the Queen of the **Gods** <note>original: "Devas"</note> unites with the **Supreme Shiva** <note>original: "Parashiva"</note> and in a moment, O **Supreme Goddess** <note>original: "Devi Parameshvari"</note>, nectar is forthwith produced. That nectar, O Goddess, is like the juice of red lac. With it, O Mistress of the Gods, a **libation** <note>original: "Tarpana"</note> should be offered to the supreme **Deity** <note>original: "Devatâ"</note>."</margin>

<margin>**At home** <note>original: "Grihe"</note>: In the **Thousand-petalled Lotus** <note>original: "Sahasrâra"</note> at the crown of the head.</margin>

<margin>**Hair with its root** <note>original: "Chikuram samûlam"</note>: The mind along with its various functions. It is such a practitioner who gains both worldly enjoyment and spiritual Liberation.</margin>

---

The **Niruttara Tantra** (Chapter 1) speaks of two kinds of **cremation ground**:

> "The cremation ground is said to be of two kinds, O Lord: the funeral pyre and the womb." <note>original: "Shmashanang dvividhang deva chita yonih prakirtitam."</note>

¹ That is, he becomes a **King** <note>original: "Râjâ"</note>, and has no longer to go on foot like common folk.

# COMMENTARY

## 17

The **devotee**¹ who, having placed before himself,² and **meditated and again meditated**³ upon, the **abode**,⁴ strewn with **flowers**,⁵ of the **God with the bow of flowers**,⁶ **recites**⁷ Thy **Mantra**, Ah!⁸ he becomes on earth the **Lord of the Celestial Musicians**,⁹ and

---

1 **Devotee** <note>original: "Bhaktah"</note>: here referring to the **Heroic Practitioner** <note>original: "Vīra Sādhaka"</note>. (V)

2 **Before** <note>original: "Purah"</note>: that is, with the **Sacred Diagram** <note>original: "Mandala"</note> of **Desire** <note>original: "Kāma"</note> placed before him. (V)

3 That is, with intensity. **Meditating and meditating** <note>original: "Dhyāyan dhyāyan"</note>, meaning repeatedly meditating. (V)

4 **The abode of the one with the flower-bow** <note>original: "Kusuma-dhanusho mandiram"</note>. The God with the bow of flowers is **Desire** <note>original: "Kāma"</note> whose abode is the **House of Passion** <note>original: "Madanāgāra"</note>. *The Wishing Tree of Tantra* <note>original: "Tantrakalpadruma"</note> says, "He who recites the Mantra ten thousand times meditating on the flower-covered **Source** <note>original: "Yoni"; the symbol of the Goddess's creative power</note> <note>original: "Svapushpairākirnam"</note> of **Power** <note>original: "Shakti"</note>, of a certainty charms all with his poetry." **Own-flower** <note>original: "Svapushpa"</note> is called the **Self-born flower** <note>original: "Svayambhukusuma"</note> in the **Tantric Scriptures** <note>original: "Tantra-shāstra"</note>. The *Tantra of the Different Mothers* <note>original: "Mātrikābheda Tantra"</note> says, "Oh Lady of the **Great Lord** <note>original: "Mahesha"</note>, **Own-flower**, which charms all, is the **Flow** <note>original: "Ritu"; referring to the menstrual cycle</note> which first appears in a married girl." (V)

5 **Strewn with "own-flowers"** <note>original: "Svapushpairākirnam"</note>. The word **own-flower** <note>original: "svapushpa"</note> is the same as the **self-born flower** <note>original: "svayambhupushpa"</note> mentioned in the Tantras. The word **flower** <note>original: "pushpa"</note> has here, and in ordinary speech, a figurative sense, as in English. For <note>original: "pushpashabdena atra ritur uchyate, mātrikābhedatantramānānusārena anūdhayā kanyāyāh prathama eva ritur uchyate"</note> **"by the word 'flower' here the menstrual flow is meant; according to the authority of the Matrikabheda Tantra, it refers to the very first flow of an unmarried girl."** <note>original: "Tantrāntare tu vivāhitāyā eva balāyā ritur atra vivakshitah"</note> **"However, in another Tantra, the flow of a young married woman is what is intended here."** The *Secret of the Dark Goddess* <note>original: "Shyāmārahasya"</note> reads "excellent flower" <note>original: "supushpa"</note>, which literally means a pleasing fragrant flower, but which is possibly a misprint for **own-flower** <note>original: "svapushpa"</note>. The meaning of the passage is as follows: <note>original: "Sādhakah svasya purobhāge shaktīng samsthāpya tasyā riturudhira-siktāng yonim avalokayan san devimantrang japati"</note> **"The practitioner, having placed the woman (representing the energy of the Goddess) before him, recites the mantra of the Goddess while gazing upon her womb/source which is moistened with the menstrual flow."**

6 That is, **Desire** <note>original: "Kāma"</note>, the God of Desire, whose bow and arrows are made of flowers.

7 **Recites** <note>original: "Japati"</note>: recites ten thousand times. (V)

8 **Ah** <note>original: "Aho"</note>. "Ah," an exclamation of wonder. (V)

9 **Celestial spirits** <note>original: "devayoni"</note>, who play and sing at the banquets of the **Gods** <note>original: "Devas"</note>. According to the *Ancient History of Vishnu* <note>original: "Vishnu Purāna"</note>, they are the sons of the Creator **Brahmā** "born imbibing melody." The **Practitioner** <note>original: "Sādhaka"</note> thus becomes a master of dance, music, and song. **Lord of the ranks of Celestial Musicians** <note>original: "Gandarvashrenipatih"</note>. He becomes a great singer and a master of melody. The *Sahasānka* says, "**Haha** <note>a specific name of a musician spirit</note> is called a Celestial Musician, and singing also makes one a Celestial Musician." (V)

<summary>This page explains the ritual meditation on the Goddess through the symbolism of the God of Desire (Kāma), equating "flowers" with sacred feminine creative fluids. It promises that the practitioner who masters this meditation will gain supernatural mastery over music, poetry, and the arts, becoming like the celestial Gandharvas.</summary>

## XVII
### COMMENTARY

<margin>**Devotee** <note>original: "Bhaktah"</note>: The **Practitioner** <note>original: "Sādhaka"</note> who follows the **divine path** <note>original: "Divya"</note> of a **Yogi** <note>one who practices internal union</note>.</margin>

<margin>**The Abode** <note>original: "Kusumadhanusho mandiram"</note>: The triangular **Sacred Circle of the Womb** <note>original: "Yoni Mandala"</note> located at the **Root Support** <note>original: "Mūlādhāra," the energy center at the base of the spine</note>. The *Nirvana Tantra* states: "Within the triangle, the dwelling of **Desire** <note>original: "Kāma"</note>, the **Phallic Symbol** <note>original: "Liṃga"</note> is the **Great Lord** <note>original: "Maheshvara"</note>."</margin>

<margin>**With its own flowers**: Adorned with the **Self-born Phallus** <note>original: "Svayambhu-liṃga"</note>, which is likened to a flower. The *Goraksha Samhita* says: "He is truly wise who recognizes the supreme **Radiance** <note>original: "Tejas"</note> within the **Source** <note>original: "Yoni"</note> known as the Self-born Phallus. Others are merely beasts of burden."</margin>

<margin>**Lord of the Celestial Musicians** <note>original: "Gandharva-shrenîpatih"</note>: A great singer. It is taught that "nothing is superior to a song."</margin>

<margin>**Poetry** <note>original: "Kavitvâmrita-nadinadinah"</note>: He becomes a master of verse like the legendary poet **Kalidasa**.</margin>

<margin>**Is great** <note>original: "Prabhavati"</note>: He attains **Liberation** <note>original: "Nirvâna"</note> by being united with You, who are the **Embodiment of Truth, Consciousness, and Bliss** <note>original: "Sachchidânandarûpâ"</note>. The *Kurma Purana* says: "The **Seekers of the Absolute** <note>original: "Brahmavâdîs"</note> have learned in all the **Sacred Teachings** <note>original: "Vedas and Vedântas"</note> of the one, omnipresent, subtle <note>original: "Kûtastha"</note>, immovable, absolute, endless, and undying **Universal Soul** <note>original: "Brahman"</note>—the sole supreme **Indivisible Reality** <note>original: "Nishkala Tattva"</note> which is higher than the highest, eternal, auspicious, and wondrous." The *Goddess’s Song* <note>original: "Devigîtâ"</note> says, "O Mountain, the one in whom **Supreme Devotion** <note>original: "Parabhakti"</note> is thus generated becomes merged in Pure Consciousness."</margin>

---

¹ He becomes a **Scholar** <note>original: "Pandita"</note> in all literature. The *Kali Tantra*, cited in the *Kali-Kalpalata* <note>The Wishing-Vine of Kali</note>, says that in strength he becomes like the wind, in wealth of gifts like **Indra** <note>King of the Gods</note>, and in the musical arts like **Tamburu**—a **Seer** <note>original: "Rishi"</note>, master of music and inventor of the **tambur** <note>a long-necked stringed instrument</note>.

² **Merged in the Supreme Abode** <note>original: "Paramapadalinah"</note>, that is, he reaches **Liberation** <note>original: "Nirvâna"</note>. The word **Is Great** <note>original: "Prabhavati"</note> suggests he becomes capable of creation and other divine acts upon being merged with Your Supreme Feet. The word literally means "Excels."

### 18

> He who at night, when in union with his **Female Energy** <note>original: "Shakti"</note>,¹ meditates with a concentrated mind ² on You, O Mother with the gently smiling face—perceiving You upon the breast of the corpse-like Shiva, who lies upon a fifteen-angled **mystical diagram** <note>original: "yantra"</note> ³ while You are deeply absorbed in the sweet play of love with the **Great Lord of Time** <note>original: "Mahâkâla"</note> ⁴—that man himself becomes the destroyer of the **God of Love**.⁵

### XVIII

### COMMENTARY

<margin>
**Mother**
<note>original: "Janani"</note>
The Progenitrix <note>the female ancestor or creator of all</note>.
</margin>

<margin>
**‘At night’**
<note>original: "Naktam"</note>
Midnight. The *Great Blue Tantra* <note>original: "Brihannîla Tantra"</note> says, ‘He who is intent on meditation at midnight or early dawn surely sees the supremely blissful aspect of the **Goddess** <note>original: "Devī"</note>.’
</margin>

<margin>
**‘Meditates’**
<note>original: "Dhyâyet"</note>
On You as being no different from the **Practitioner’s** <note>original: "Sâdhaka"</note> own **Inner Self** <note>original: "Âtmâ"</note>. You are the **Reflection of Consciousness** <note>original: "Chidâbhâsa"</note> within his body, which serves as a **Ritual Instrument** <note>original: "Yantra"</note>. *Gandharva [Tantra]*
</margin>

### NOTES.

1 **He who is himself absorbed in the bliss of union** <note>original: "Svayam api ratānandaniratah"</note>. The meaning is as follows: When the practitioner is engaged in the act of ritual union with his female partner, he meditates on the Goddess according to the methods described in these verses.

2 **Concentrated** <note>original: "Samāsaktah"</note> on You.

3 The *Vine of Kali’s Desires* <note>original: "Kâlikalpalatâ"</note> says it is a specific kind of **mystical diagram** <note>original: "yantra"</note>.

**On a fifteen-angled seat** <note>original: "Tripânchâre pîthe"</note>. This describes the Diagram. The *Kali Tantra* explains: ‘First draw a triangle. Outside it, place another. Next, draw three more triangles <note>totaling five superimposed triangles, creating fifteen corners</note>. In the center, draw the **Circle of the Point** <note>original: "Baindava Chakra"</note> adorned with the **Seed-Syllable of Illusion** <note>original: "Mâyâ Bîja," the sound HRĪM</note>. Draw a circle outside this six-cornered figure. Next, draw eight petals attached to the outer circle and the **Outer Square** <note>original: "Bhûpura"</note>. He who knows this great Diagram surely attains liberation.’ The **Outer Square** <note>original: "Bhûpura"</note> represents the gross physical body composed of the five **Elements** <note>original: "Bhûtas"</note>.

4 **Deeply absorbed in the beauty and fluid of the passion of the Great Lord of Time** <note>original: "Mahakālenochchairmadanarasalavanyaniratam"</note>. The Great Lord of Time is the **Supreme Shiva** <note>original: "Paramashiva"</note>. The description of being "absorbed in the essence of love" refers to the **Inverted Posture** <note>original: "Viparitratî," where the Goddess takes the active role above Shiva</note>.

5 **Destroyer of Desire** <note>original: "Smarahara"</note>. The destroyer of **Love/Desire** <note>original: "Kāma"</note> is Shiva Himself.

This means the practitioner becomes like Shiva, who destroyed the Deity of Love <note>Smara or Kāma</note> with the fire from His central eye. This occurred when Love attempted to distract Shiva from his deep meditation by inciting desire for the goddess **Pârvatî**. Alternatively, it can be interpreted that the practitioner "surpasses the God of Love in beauty."

<summary>This page explains a ritual meditation where the devotee identifies with Shiva while visualizing the Goddess Kali in a state of active union. It provides technical instructions for constructing a fifteen-angled yantra and explains the symbolism of overcoming worldly desire to achieve a god-like state.</summary>

<keywords>Shakti, Yantra, Mahakala, Kali Tantra, meditation, ritual union, Shiva, Kama, liberation, geometry, five elements</keywords>
