<meta>This page continues the commentary on Verse 18 of the Hymn to Kālī, shifting from the external ritual context to the internal, philosophical identification of the practitioner with the Goddess. It explains the "fifteen-cornered seat" not just as a physical diagram, but as a symbolic mapping of the human body and consciousness.</meta>

# HYMN TO KÂLÎ

The **Tantra** <note>a category of esoteric scriptures</note> says, ‘He who is in a **state of non-duality** <note>original: "Advaitabhâva"</note>, and thinks of himself as the **Deity** <note>original: "Devatâ"</note> in the three forms of the body, thinks of Her and his own **Soul** <note>original: "Âtmâ"</note> as one. He should worship the **Goddess** <note>original: "Devî"</note> as the **Soul** <note>original: "Âtmâ"</note> with the prescribed ritual items. The **mystical diagram** <note>original: "Yantra"</note> which is one's own body should be considered the best of all such diagrams.’ Again, ‘He who meditates on the **attributeless** <note>original: "Nirguna"</note>, unattached, pure **Soul** <note>original: "Âtmâ"</note> of the Goddess **Tripura** <note>a form of the Goddess representing the "Three Cities" or states of consciousness</note> as not being different from his own **Soul** <note>original: "Âtmâ"</note> becomes one with Her.’

<margin>
**‘Thee’**
<note>original: "Tvâm"</note>
That is, the Goddess who is **composed of the Absolute** <note>original: "Brahmamayî"</note>.
</margin>

<margin>
**‘Smiling face’**
<note>original: "Smera-vadanam"</note>
Because She is ever blissful, being Bliss itself.
</margin>

<margin>
**‘On the breast’**
<note>original: "Mahâkâlen-ochchhâih"</note>
On the breast of **Shiva** <note>the god representing pure consciousness</note> who is inactive like a corpse. She divides Herself into two parts like a grain of gram—namely, **Shiva** <note>Consciousness</note> and **Shakti** <note>Energy</note>—by means of **Illusion** <note>original: "Mâyâ"</note> associated with **Will** <note>original: "Ichchhâ"</note>, **Action** <note>original: "Kriyâ"</note>, and **Knowledge** <note>original: "Jnâna"</note>, while at the same time remaining established in Her **formless Absolute** <note>original: "Nirguna Brahman"</note> state.
</margin>

<margin>
**‘Fifteen-cornered seat’**
<note>original: "Tripanchâre pithe"</note>
This is the **practitioner’s** <note>original: "Sâdhaka"</note> own body conceived as the **mystical diagram** <note>original: "Yantra"</note>. In this internal map:
1. **Ignorance** <note>original: "Avidyâ"</note> is the encompassing outer circle.
2. The eight-fold **Nature** <note>original: "Prakriti"</note>—consisting of Earth, Water, Fire, Air, Ether, Mind, Intellect, and Ego—is the eight-petalled lotus.
3. The five **senses of knowledge** <note>original: "Jnânendriya"</note>, the five **organs of action** <note>original: "Karmendriya"</note>, and the five **life-breaths** <note>original: "Prâna"</note> are the five Triangles.
4. The **Point** <note>original: "Bindu"</note>, which is Consciousness reflected in the creative power of the Absolute composed of pure **quality of light and goodness** <note>original: "Sattvaguna"</note>, is the adorning **seed-syllable** <note>original: "Bîja"</note>. 

The *Gandharva Tantra* says, ‘The **diagram composed of mantras** <note>original: "Chakramantramaya"</note> is the **Deity’s** Supreme Body, which is the union of Shiva and Shakti.’ The *Bhagavadgîtâ* says, ‘Earth, Water, Fire, Air, Ether, Mind, Intellect, and **Ego** <note>original: "Ahangkâra"</note>—these **principles** <note>original: "Tattvas"</note> constitute my eightfold **Nature** <note>original: "Prakriti"</note>.’ The *Gandharva Tantra* says, ‘The subtle body composed of **uncompounded** <note>original: "Apanchîkrita"</note> **elements** <note>original: "Bhûta"</note> and equipped with the five life-breaths, Mind, Intellect, and ten senses is the vehicle for experience. Unbeginning and **undefinable** <note>original: "Anirvâchyâ"</note> Ignorance is the **causal limitation** <note>original: "Upâdhi"</note>. Know the **Soul** <note>original: "Âtmâ"</note> to be different from this three-fold limitation.’
</margin>

<summary>This page details the esoteric practice of identifying the practitioner's body with a mystical diagram (yantra), explaining how various physical and mental faculties correspond to geometric elements of the Goddess's seat. It emphasizes the non-dual philosophy where the individual soul and the universal Goddess are realized as one.</summary>

<keywords>Non-duality, Soul (Atma), Mystical Diagram (Yantra), Shiva, Shakti, Nature (Prakriti), Consciousness, Subtle Body, Tantra, Bhagavad Gita</keywords>

--- Page 91 ---
<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Goddess Kālī's nature, specifically focusing on her "reverse" union with Shiva and the internal spiritual success (siddhi) granted to her practitioners. It moves from the philosophical description of her union into the first lines of Verse 19 and its associated philological notes.</meta>

# COMMENTARY

<margin>
**‘Deeply immersed’**
<note>original: "Madanarasa-lâvanyaniratâm", literally: devoted to the beauty of the nectar of passion</note>
</margin>
Always united in the reverse <note>original: "Viparîta", referring to the tantric "reverse" posture where the Goddess is active atop the God</note> way with the **Supreme Shiva** <note>original: "Parama-shiva"</note>, the **Absolute with attributes** <note>original: "Sagunabrahman"</note>. The **Gandharva Tantra** <note>an authoritative esoteric text</note> says, ‘When that **Supreme Power** <note>original: "Shakti"</note> by putting that **Spirit** <note>original: "Purusha"</note> down, of Her own will appears as the universe, then She becomes passionate. And then becoming Herself active, the **Goddess** <note>original: "Devî"</note> rises upon the **Terrible One** <note>original: "Bhairava", a form of Shiva</note> and enhances Her own bliss with waves of natural pleasure.’

<margin>
**‘He himself also enjoying the bliss of union’**
<note>original: "Svayam api ratânanda-niratah"</note>
</margin>
Enjoying the bliss of union in **Absorption** <note>original: "Laya"</note> with the **Supreme Soul** <note>original: "Paramâtmâ"</note> by the **ritual gesture of the womb** <note>original: "Yoni-mudrâ", a physical or symbolic seal used in yoga</note> and becoming **composed of Power** <note>original: "Shaktimaya"</note> himself. The **Gheranda Samhitâ** <note>a classic manual of Hatha Yoga</note> says, ‘He should perform the **womb-gesture** <note>original: "Yoni-mudrâ"</note> and himself become **composed of Power** <note>original: "Shaktimaya"</note>. He should move within the **Supreme Soul** <note>original: "Paramâtmâ"</note> with the beautiful **sentiment of love** <note>original: "Shringârarasa"</note>. Becoming **composed of bliss** <note>original: "Ânandamaya"</note>, he should be one with the **Absolute** <note>original: "Brahman"</note>.’

<margin>
**Destroyer of Desire**
<note>original: "Smarahara", an epithet of Shiva who burnt Kama, the god of desire, to ashes</note>
</margin>
The **practitioner of Non-Duality** <note>original: "Advaita Sâdhaka"</note> attains **Liberation** <note>original: "Kaivalya", literally "aloneness" or absolute freedom</note> by being merged in Thee who art the **Supreme Soul** <note>original: "Paramâtmâ"</note>.

->19<-

O Dark One,¹ wondrous and excelling in every way,² becomes the accomplishment,³ of those worshippers ⁴ who living in this world ⁵ freely make offering to Thee in worship ⁶ of the greatly ⁷

### NOTES.

¹ **The Dark One** <note>original: "Asitâ"</note>. That is, the Goddess **Kālī** <note>original: "Kâlikâ", see the subsequent note</note>.

² **At every step** <note>original: "Pratipadam"</note>. The text *Summary of the Essence of the Secret of the Dark Goddess* <note>original: "Shyâmârahasyasârasamgraha"</note> reads "every day" <note>original: "pratidinam"</note> (according to the commentator K.B.), which seems preferable, for, as K.B. says, the worship <note>original: "pûjâ"</note> is the general daily ritual, upon which daily advancement in **success** <note>original: "siddhi"</note> would follow.

³ **Success** <note>original: "Siddhi"</note>; success in work or the accomplishment of all which is desired (according to the commentator V).

⁴ **Wise** <note>original: "Sat"</note>, that is, **good or pious** <note>original: "sâdhu"</note>. **Of the wise** <note>original: "Satâm"</note> = **Of the practitioners** <note>original: "Sâdhakânâm"</note> (V).

⁵ That is, among men.

⁶ **Even in worship** <note>original: "Pûjayâm api"</note> (see note 2, above).
**In worship** <note>original: "Pûjayâm"</note>: **Occasional** <note>original: "Naimittika"</note> or specific worship (V). The force of the particle **even** <note>original: "api"</note> is that the offering is not confined to special **spiritual practice** <note>original: "Sâdhanâ"</note> but is made in ordinary worship also. (K.B.)

⁷ **Greatly** or **Supreme** <note>original: "Param"</note> (K. B.).

<summary>This page discusses the Tantric concept of the Goddess's "reverse" union with Shiva, symbolizing the awakening of creative energy, and begins the commentary on Verse 19 regarding the daily success granted to her practitioners.</summary>

--- Page 92 ---
<meta>This page continues the specific ritual instructions for Verse 19, detailing the types of offerings—specifically various animal and symbolic sacrifices—that please the Goddess. It shifts from the physical description of the offerings into a philological and esoteric commentary on the terms used in the verse, explaining how these animals represent internal psychological vices to be overcome by the practitioner.</meta>

HYMN TO KĀLI

...satisfying [the Goddess with] flesh, together with hair and bone,¹ of cats, camels, sheep,² buffaloes, goats, and men.³

### XIX
### COMMENTARY

<margin>**‘The Black One’** <note>original: "Asitâ"</note></margin> **The Unbound One** <note>original: "Asitâ"</note> means one who is free from bondage. **Bound** <note>original: "Sitâ"</note> means one who is tied or restricted. Therefore, **The Unbound One** is "not bound," or eternally liberated. The linguistic root *So* means "to bind." The **Amarakosha** <note>a celebrated Sanskrit dictionary and thesaurus</note> gives the meaning of "Bound" as one who is tied.

<margin>**‘Wondrous’** <note>original: "Apûrvâ"</note></margin> This refers to that which is **Best** or unprecedented.

<margin>**‘At every step’** <note>original: "Pratipadam"</note></margin> Successively, or step by step.

<margin>**All Powers** <note>original: "Sarvasiddhi"</note></margin> This refers to the **five forms of Liberation** <note>original: "Siddhis"</note>. The **Shivagîtâ** <note>The Song of Shiva, a philosophical text</note> says: ‘**Residing in the divine realm** <note>original: "Sâlokya"</note>, **attaining the divine form** <note>original: "Sârûpya"</note>, **possessing divine power** <note>original: "Sârshti"</note>, **union with the divine** <note>original: "Sâyujya"</note>, and **absolute liberation** <note>original: "Kaivalya"</note>. Know these to be the five forms of liberation.’

<margin>**‘The flesh of’** <note>original: "Palalang"</note></margin> In an esoteric sense, these animals represent the **Six Enemies** <note>original: "Ripu", referring to internal vices</note> which are specifically characterized by the following animals:—
*   The **goat** represents **Lust** <note>original: "Kâma"</note>, as the saying goes, "as lustful as a goat."
*   The **buffalo** represents **Anger** <note>original: "Krodha"</note>, "as angry as a buffalo."
*   The **cat** represents **Greed** <note>original: "Lobha"</note>, "as greedy as a cat."
*   The **sheep** represents **Delusion** <note>original: "Moha"</note>, "as stupid as a sheep."
*   The **camel** represents **Envy** <note>original: "Mâtsarya"</note>, "as envious as a camel."
*   **Man** represents **Pride** <note>original: "Mada"</note>, specifically the "pride and arrogance of man."

---

¹ This refers to the offering of the whole animal, including flesh and all other parts.

² **Sheep** <note>original: "Maisham"</note>. The text **Shyâmârahasyasârasamgraha** <note>Compendium of the Secrets of the Dark Goddess</note> also mentions the use of **rat's flesh** <note>original: "mausham"</note>. The **Fetkârinî Tantra** <note>a specific ritual manual</note> lists both sheep and rat's flesh.

The **Shyâmârahasya** <note>Secrets of the Dark Goddess</note> states: ‘To the practitioner who offers the flesh of cats, sheep, camels, and buffaloes together with their bone, hair, and skin, the **Goddess Dakshinâ** <note>a form of Kali</note> is ever beneficial and nurturing like a Mother.’

³ Regarding the mention of human sacrifice, the commentator K. B. notes that **Kings alone**, and no others, are permitted to perform human sacrifice. He cites the **Yâmala** <note>a category of Tantric texts</note> as quoted in the **Kâlikalpalatâ**: ‘A King may offer human sacrifice, but no other person may do so, O Supreme Goddess.’ For the internal, spiritual meaning of this, see the section on **Essential Explanation** <note>original: "Svarûpa-vyâkhyâ"</note> later in the text.

<summary>This page concludes the list of ritual offerings for Verse 19 and provides a commentary explaining that the animals sacrificed (goat, buffalo, cat, etc.) symbolize the "Six Enemies" or internal vices like lust and pride. It also clarifies that references to human sacrifice are legally restricted to royalty and hints at a deeper, spiritual interpretation of these rituals.</summary>

<keywords>Kali, Asita, liberation, Siddhis, Six Enemies, animal symbolism, human sacrifice, Tantric ritual, Sanskrit etymology, Shivagita</keywords>

--- Page 93 ---
<meta>This page continues the commentary on the ritual requirements for Verse 19, specifically discussing the symbolic sacrifice of internal vices and moving into Verse 20, which details the practice of recitation (japa) and the distinction between the "animal" (Pashva) and "heroic" (Vira) paths of worship.</meta>

COMMENTARY 67

The **Annadâkalpa** <note>a ritual manual dedicated to the Goddess as the Giver of Food</note> says, ‘Worship should be performed by making an offering of lust in the form of a goat, buffalo, and so forth.’ Offering is made to You, who are the **form of pure consciousness** <note>original: "Chidrûpâ"</note>, by treating lust and other vices as the **articles of ritual offering** <note>original: "Upachâra"</note> in worship, with the goal of ridding oneself of them. The **Brihannîla Tantra** <note>The Great Blue Tantra, an important Shakta text</note> says, ‘In the fire of the **Soul** <note>original: "Âtmâ"</note> which flames with the **offering** <note>original: "Havih"</note> of **righteousness** <note>original: "Dharma"</note> and **unrighteousness** <note>original: "Adharma"</note>, I eternally make sacrifice through the **central energy path** <note>original: "Sushumnâ"</note>, with the mind as the ladle, of all the functions of the senses—**so be it** <note>original: "Svâhâ"</note>.’

<margin>‘**In worship**’ <note>original: "Pûjâyâm"</note></margin>
In mental worship according to the prescribed manner.

<margin>‘**With hair and bone**’ <note>original: "Loma, asthi"</note></margin>
That is, the whole offering without omitting any part. Such **practitioners** <note>original: "Sâdhakas"</note> attain **residency in the divine realm** <note>original: "Sâlokya"</note> and other forms of liberation.

->20<-

O Mother, he who, being a controller of his passions,¹ eats **sacrificial food** <note>original: "havishyânnam"</note>,² and, being proficient in meditation on Your feet, rightly recites³ Your **sacred sound** <note>original: "mantra"</note> a hundred thousand times by day, and he who afterwards⁴ naked at night, when united with his **divine partner** <note>original: "Shakti"</note>,⁵ rightly recites Your great **sacred sound** another such hundred thousand times, becomes on earth like the **Destroyer of the God of Love** <note>original: "Smara," referring to Shiva</note>.⁶

### NOTES.

1 **Self-controlled** <note>original: "Vashi"</note>. The first part of this verse refers to the **Elementary Path** <note>original: "Pashvâchâra," literally "the way of the animal"</note>.
2 That is, one who has undertaken the **Vow of Preliminary Recitation** <note>original: "Purashcharanavrata"</note>, and eats the pure form of food known as **sacrificial grains** <note>original: "Havishyânnam"</note>. **Devoted to eating sacrificial food** <note>original: "Havishyashanaratah"</note>: that is, after the recitation.
3 Performs **repetitive prayer** <note>original: "japa"</note>.
4 **Afterwards** <note>original: "Param"</note>: that is, when he has been **consecrated** <note>original: "Abhishikta"</note> into the **Heroic Path** <note>original: "Virachara"</note>.
5 **Naked at night, finding delight in union** <note>original: "Naktang nagno nidhuvanavinodena"</note>, the meaning of which is: **when the practitioner is engaged in the act of sexual union with his partner, he then recites the mantra**.
6 **The Destroyer of Love** <note>original: "Smarahara"</note> or **Shiva**. The **Tantrakalpadruma** <note>The Wishing-Tree of Tantra</note> says, ‘He who eats **sacrificial food**, who keeping the Goddess in mind recites the **sacred sound** a hundred thousand times by day and is at night united with his **divine partner**, becomes the Lord of the earth.’

<summary>This page explains how animal sacrifices are symbolic representations of sacrificing one's own vices and details the specific ritual requirements for Verse 20, which involves massive recitations of the mantra during both the day and night.</summary>
<keywords>Annadakalpa, symbolic sacrifice, Brihannila Tantra, Homa, Vira path, Pashva path, mantra recitation, Shiva, Shakti</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on Verse 20, specifically clarifying the spiritual state of the practitioner who achieves union with the Divine, and introduces Verse 21, which explains the origin of the Goddess's sacred mantra and the poetic powers granted to her devotees.</meta>

HYMN TO KÂLI

### XX

#### COMMENTARY

<margin>‘**Naked**’ <note>original: "Nagnah"</note></margin> That is, free from the covering of **illusion** <note>original: "Mâyâ"</note>; **changeless** <note>original: "Nirvikâra"</note>.

<margin>‘**Amorous play**’ <note>original: "Nidhuvana-vinodena"</note></margin> That is, enjoying the bliss of union between the **Soul** <note>original: "Âtmâ"</note> and the **Supreme Power** <note>original: "Parashakti"</note>. The **Kulârnava Tantra** <note>a foundational text of the Kaula tradition</note> says, ‘That is **union** <note>original: "Maithuna"</note> in which there is the bliss arising from the union of the Soul and the Supreme Power. Others are but enjoyers of women.’

<margin>‘**Becomes**’ <note>original: "Syât"</note></margin> That is, becomes **liberated while still living** <note>original: "Jîvanmukta"</note> like the **Great Lord Shiva**.

### 21

O Mother, this Hymn of Yours is the source from which Your **sacred sound** <note>original: "mantra"</note> originates.¹ It sings of Your **essential nature**, and contains instructions for the worship of Your two lotus Feet. He who recites it at midnight or at the time of **ritual worship** <note>original: "Pûjâ"</note>,² even his random talk³ becomes the nectar-juice of poetry.

### XXI

#### COMMENTARY

<margin>‘**Your real self**’ <note>original: "Svarûpâkhyam"</note></margin> Speaks of the **meditative visualization** <note>original: "Dhyâna"</note> of both Your gross and subtle aspects.

<margin>‘**Reads**’ <note>original: "Pathati"</note></margin> That is, recites aloud. The **Vishuddheshvara Tantra** <note>a Tantric scripture</note> says, ‘O **Goddess** <note>original: "Devi"</note>, the reading of a **hymn of praise** <note>original: "Stotra"</note> mentally, or the recitation of a **sacred sound** <note>original: "Mantra"</note> loudly is as ineffectual as water in a broken jar.’

#### NOTES.

1 **The source of the mantra's birth** <note>original: "Manusamuddharanajanuh"</note>—that is, the cause of the **extraction of the mantra** <note>original: "mantroddhâra"</note>: the formation of the sacred sound of the **Goddess** <note>original: "Devî"</note>. The sacred sound is made known and then impressed with the life and **consciousness** <note>original: "chaitanya"</note> of the **practitioner** <note>original: "sâdhaka"</note> (**the enlivening of the mantra** <note>original: "mantrachaitanya"</note>).

2 **Ritual worship** <note>original: "Pûjâ"</note>.

3 That is, even his meaningless, delirious talk, as in fever or madness, and so forth (K. B.).

<summary>This page concludes the commentary on Verse 20 by defining spiritual nakedness and divine union, then introduces Verse 21, which asserts that the hymn is the source of Kâli's mantra and grants the devotee the gift of eloquent poetry.</summary>
<keywords>Mâyâ, Soul, Supreme Power, Kulârnava Tantra, liberation while living, Shiva, Mantra, ritual worship, meditative visualization, poetic inspiration</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on Verse 21, which discusses the gift of eloquence and poetic power bestowed upon the devotee of the Goddess. It transitions into the final verse of the hymn (Verse 22), summarizing the worldly and spiritual benefits of the practice, before concluding with detailed scholarly notes on the state of the liberated soul.</meta>

# COMMENTARY

<margin>'**Nectar of Poesy**' <note>original: "Prasarati kavitvâmritarasah"</note></margin>
He becomes full of the sweetness of Poetry. The **All-in-All of the Kali Lineage** <term>Kâlikulasarvasva: a sacred Tantric text dedicated to the rituals of the Goddess Kali</term> says, 
> ‘All whose difficulties and dangers are destroyed by a single reading, as it were flies in a flame. His speech flows like the Ganges full of prose and poetry.’

->22<-

Numbers of women with large eyes, like those of the antelope,¹ impatient for his love, ever follow him. Even the King becomes subject to his control. He becomes like unto **Kuvera**² himself. An enemy fears him as if he were a prison. Living in continuous bliss³ the devotee is **liberated while yet living**, and is never again reborn.⁴

Here ends the Hymn by the **Great Lord of Time** <term>Shrî Mâhâkâla</term>, entitled the **Hymn Beginning with Camphor** <term>Karpûrâdistotra</term>.

->XXII<-

### COMMENTARY

<margin>'**Liberated**' <note>original: "Jivanmukta"</note></margin>
And on death gets **liberation after leaving the physical body** <term>Videhamukti</term>.

<margin>'**No rebirth**' <note>original: "Muktab pratijanuh"</note></margin>
He gets **extinction** <term>Nirvâna</term> in the **Absolute Reality** <term>Brahman</term>. The **Collection of the Great Lord of Time** <term>Mahâkâlasamhitâ</term> says, 
> ‘Whoever constantly and with devotion reads this Hymn originating from the **Great Lord of Time** <note>original: "Mâhâkâla"</note>, is free from danger, disease and death and in the end attains **absolute liberation** <term>Kaivalya</term>.’

### NOTES.

¹ ***Antelope*** <term>Kuramga</term>, which has beautiful large eyes.

² Lord of wealth.

³ ***Through the art of playful enjoyment*** <term>Kelikalayâ</term>, by the various entertaining acts <note>original: "parihâsâdinâ"</note> of which there are sixty-four <note>referring to the traditional 64 arts or Kalas in Indian tradition</note>. The meaning here is that there is continuous bliss.

⁴ ***Through the art of playful enjoyment he becomes liberated while living, and a devotee who is not reborn*** <note>original: "Kelikalayâ chiram jivanmuktah sa bhavati cha bhaktah pratijanuh"</note> The translation in the text reads **not reborn** <note>original: "pratijanuh"</note> to mean, as the commentator K. B. says, the **cessation of birth** <term>Janmanivritti</term>. But the term may also mean ‘birth after birth.’ According to this translation, **liberated while living** <note>original: "jivanmukta"</note> would not refer to the state immediately preceding **final liberation** <term>Kaivalya</term> but, as K. B. says, the **vision of the deity experienced while still in the living state** <term>Jivadasthânubhûtadevatâ-sakshâtkâra-mukha</term>; in which case the translation will be: *He, living in continuous bliss, obtains direct Experience of the Deity and is reborn life after life as Her devotee.* According to the translation adopted here, complete liberation follows; in the other case, some lower though happy state is reached.

<summary>This page concludes the Karpūrādi-stotra, detailing the rewards of its recitation—including poetic eloquence, worldly influence, and spiritual liberation—while providing a scholarly analysis of the final state of the devotee.</summary>
<keywords>Karpūrādi-stotra, Kali, Jivanmukta, Videhamukti, Nirvâna, Kaivalya, Mâhâkâla, rebirth, spiritual liberation, Tantric poetry</keywords>

<meta>This page concludes the "Hymn to Kali" (Karpūrādya Stotra) and its associated commentary, the "Giver of Stainless Joy" (Vimalānandadāyinī). It follows the previous page’s discussion on the spiritual and material rewards of the hymn, specifically the attainment of poetic eloquence and ultimate liberation from the cycle of rebirth.</meta>

# HYMN TO KĀLĪ

*Here ends the Hymn named the **Hymn of the True Form** <term>Svarūpastotra: a poem describing the essential nature of a deity</term> of the **Auspicious South-Facing Kali** <term>Shrīmatī Dakshinā Kālikā</term> by the **Great Lord of Time** <term>Shrīmān Mahākāla</term>.*

*Here also ends its annotation and **Explanation of the True Form** <term>Svarūpavyākhyā</term> entitled the **Giver of Stainless Joy** <term>Vimalānandadāyinī</term>.*

### OBEISANCE

to Kālī the spouse of the **Lord of Time** <note>original: "Kāla"</note>, who destroys all sin and is Time itself.¹

She who is **The Savior** <term>Tārā: a major Tantric goddess</term>, the Supreme **Knowledge of the Absolute** <term>Brahmavidyā</term>, who is adored by the **Lotus-born Deity** <note>original: "Deva"</note>.²

She who is the **Auspicious Knowledge** <term>Shrīvidyā</term>, desirous of the welfare of **spiritual practitioners** <term>Sādhakas</term> on the path of Liberation, to whom the **Lord of Preservation** <note>original: "Hari"</note> and the **Lord of Destruction** <note>original: "Hara"</note>³ make obeisance.

May that **Goddess** <term>Devī</term> the Mother, who appears in the form of all things, bring forth benefits for all such as sing Her praises.

### COLOPHON

Of this King of Hymns wherein the **Great Lord of Time** <term>Mahākāla</term> has described the true self of the **Goddess of Time** <term>Kālikā</term>, the **Hymn beginning with Camphor** <term>Karpūrādya</term>, untainted by worldly desire, which gives bliss to Devotees; the aforesaid Annotation containing its simple interpretation, as well as the **Explanation of the True Form** <term>Svarūpavyākhyā</term> which gives pure joy, was prepared by me, **Swami Vimalananda** <term>Vimalānanda Svāmī</term>, for the enlightenment of **spiritual practitioners** <term>Sādhakas</term> in the **Saka** <note>A historical Indian calendar era; the year 1837 corresponds to approximately 1915 CE</note> year 1837. Mayest Thou reside in the throat of him who reads it.

->**The Absolute is Truth** <note>original: "Om, Tat Sat, Om"</note><-

---

¹ The first **Lord of Time** <note>original: "Kāla"</note> is the **Great Lord of Time** <term>Mahākāla</term> and the second is the produced time of the material world.
² **The Creator** <term>Brahmā</term>. &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; ³ **Vishnu** and **Rudra** <note>Two of the primary deities of the Hindu trinity</note>.

<meta>This page serves as the opening of the manuscript, transitioning from the introductory front matter to the primary text of the "Hymn Beginning with Camphor" (Karpūrādi Stotra) and its commentary. It establishes the ritual framework and begins the technical decoding of the Goddess's secret mantra.</meta>

-> Salutations to the Holy Guru. Om, Salutations to the Supreme Goddess. <-

# THE AUSPICIOUS HYMN BEGINNING WITH THE WORD CAMPHOR

---

O Mother, spouse of the **Destroyer of the Three Cities** <note>original: "Tripuraharu-vadhu", referring to Shiva</note>, those who recite three times this **Seed-syllable** <term>Bīja: a primary vocal sound used in meditation</term>—formed by the word **Camphor** <note>original: "Karpūram"</note> when deprived of its middle and final vowels and joined with the **Moon** and the **Left Eye** <note>This is a tantric code: the word "Karpūram" modified results in the sound "K", "R", combined with the "I" (Left Eye) and "M" (the Moon/nasalization), creating the sacred syllable KRĪM</note>—from the hollows of their mouths flow streams of prose and poetry. Such seekers, having attained all spiritual perfections, wander at will, radiant as the beauty of a dense, dark rain cloud. ॥ 1 ॥

## COMMENTARY

> Om. Salutations to the one whose body is pure knowledge, whose three eyes are the **Vedas** <term>the most ancient Hindu scriptures</term>, and who is the cause of attaining the highest good—He who wears the half-moon. <note>original: "Somārddhadhāriṇe", a title for Shiva</note> — *The Holy Chandi*
>
> I bow to the Great Goddess, the **Fourth State** <term>Turīyā: the state of pure consciousness beyond waking, dreaming, and deep sleep</term>, who is the very form of the **Absolute** <term>Brahman</term>. By merely remembering Her, one does not sink into the ocean of worldly existence. — *Gandharva Tantra*
>
> I worship **South-facing Kalika** <term>Dakshina Kalika</term>, the goddess Kali who destroys Time, whose form is the seed-syllable **KRĪM** <note>original: "Krīṅkāra"</note>, who takes the form of desire and transcends all parts. — *The Heart of Kali*

Regarding the verse beginning with "Camphor": Through the first five verses of this text, the twenty-two-syllable **Mantra** <term>a sacred utterance or sound-formula</term> of the Great Goddess—known as the **Queen of Knowledge** <note>original: "Vidyārājñī"</note> in Her subtle form—is extracted and revealed. Furthermore, the methods of Her practice and Her great spiritual power are described in sequence. In the sixth verse, the extraction of various other mantras is discussed. 

As stated in the **Scripture of Kalika** <term>Kālikā-Shruti</term>: "One attains Her in the **Opening of Brahma** <term>Brahmarandhra: the energy center at the crown of the head</term>, She who is the form of the Absolute, the Fortunate One, the collective form of the energies of Desire, Fire, and Beauty. This is tripled at the beginning, followed by the two **Hūm** syllables, which are the union of the sixth vowel and the nasal point..." This describes the unfolding of the Goddess's primary sound-form...

<summary>This page introduces the Karpūrādi Stotra, a fundamental Tantric hymn to the goddess Kali, beginning with a coded verse that reveals her sacred seed-syllable (KRĪM). The accompanying commentary provides scriptural justifications for her worship and begins a technical explanation of her twenty-two-syllable mantra.</summary>

--- Page 98 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical commentary on the "Hymn Beginning with Camphor." Having established the first seed-syllable (Krīm) on the previous page, the text now introduces the complete fifteen-syllable mantra of the Goddess Dakshina Kalika and argues for the spiritual unity of the three Great Goddesses—Kali, Tara, and Tripurasundari.</meta>

"Having pronounced the sevenfold mantra, one should recite it after the sister of the knowledge of the Absolute. This is the highest and most excellent of all mantras." Thus it is said in the **Niruttara Tantra** <term>a sacred text of the Shakta tradition focusing on the highest non-dual realization</term>:

> "Now I shall speak, O Great Goddess, of the mantra of **South-facing Kalika** <note>original: "Dakshina Kalika"</note>, by the mere knowledge of which one becomes liberated even while living.
> 
> O Goddess, when combined with **Brahma** and **Fire** <note>The letters 'K' and 'R'</note>, joined with the **Left Eye** <note>The vowel 'Ī'</note>, and endowed with the **Nasal Sound and Point** <note>original: "Nada-Bindu," the 'M' sound of a mantra</note>, it is the very form of Consciousness, the highest of the high.
> 
> This single-syllable **Perfected Knowledge** <term>Siddha-vidyā</term> is the Queen of Mantras, O Goddess of the Lineage. Then, repeat it three times, followed by the pair of **Kūrca** seeds <note>the sound HŪṀ</note> and the pair of **Lajjā** seeds <note>the sound HRĪṀ</note>.
> 
> Add the words 'To South-facing Kalika' <note>original: "Dakṣiṇe Kālike"</note> and end with the **Wife of Fire** <note>the word "Svāhā," used to conclude offerings</note>. This is proclaimed as the Queen of Knowledge. This knowledge consists of all mantras and is the cause of the creation, maintenance, and dissolution of the universe."

Furthermore, in the first verse of this hymn, the single-syllable Queen of Mantras, the seed-syllable <term>Krīm</term> belonging to the auspicious **Kalika**, was extracted. In the second verse, the single-syllable mantra of the auspicious **Tara**, the seed-syllable <term>Hūm</term>, was extracted. In the third verse, the single-syllable mantra of the auspicious **Tripurasundari**, the seed-syllable <term>Hrīm</term>, was extracted. 

As the secret methods of realization for Kali, Tara, and Sundari are described here, it is the opinion of the most excellent practitioners initiated in this lineage that this "Hymn of the True Form" <note>Svarūpa-stotra</note> should be recited in relation to the **Three Powers** <term>Tri-Shakti</term>, much like the "Hymn of the K-cluster." This is also stated in the **Tara-rahasya Tantra**:

> "As is Kali, so is Tara, and so is **Blue Sarasvati** <note>Nīla-sarasvatī, a form of Tara</note>. So too is Tripurasundari, the giver of all desired fruits.
> 
> Whatever man practices his devotions while maintaining this view of non-difference, he shall be worthy of worship in the three worlds; he is indeed a son of the Savior Goddess.
> 
> But if a man practices these mantras while making a distinction between them, there is no salvation for him, O Goddess; he is cooked in the fires of hell."

It is also stated in the meditation and the "Armor" prayer of Tara: "Born of the seed-syllable **Hūm**," and "May the single-syllable form of the seed **Hūm** always protect my throat." Likewise, in the hymn to the auspicious Sundari:

> "O Mother, O Three-eyed One of the Three Cities, who dwells in the triangular hearth! For those who take Your name as the sound <term>Hrīm</term>, or those who daily chant Your name as <term>Hrīm Hrīm</term>—what name or goal in this world is difficult for them to attain?"

So too in the **Mahakala Samhita**: "One should recite this regarding the Three Powers: the Holy Knowledge, Kalika, and Tara." In reality, just as a single type of vine never grows from different kinds of seeds, so too from the various seed-mantras such as <term>Krīm</term>, <term>Hūm</term>, and <term>Hrīm</term>, a single form...

<summary>This page decodes the complex fifteen-syllable mantra of Dakshina Kalika and argues that the goddesses Kali, Tara, and Tripurasundari are essentially one. It cites multiple Tantric authorities to emphasize that spiritual success depends on perceiving the unity of these divine forms.</summary>

<keywords>Dakshina Kalika, Mantra, Niruttara Tantra, Bija, Krīm, Hūm, Hrīm, Tara, Tripurasundari, Tri-Shakti, Non-duality</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical and mystical commentary on the first verse of the "Hymn Beginning with Camphor" (Karpūrādi-stotra). Having previously identified the seed-syllable Krīm, the text now explains the esoteric meaning behind the Goddess's epithets and her universal presence across different religions.</meta>

**Commentary with the Text**
**Page 3**

Since a physical form is not possible for the absolute, the three previously mentioned seed-syllables indicate that the **Knowledge of the Absolute** <term>Brahma-vidya</term> is indeed **South-facing Kalika of the Infinite Space**, who embodies all forms.

O Mother, "beautiful with the luster of a dark rain-cloud," possessing a charm like the deep blue of a storm cloud. Just as a fresh rain-cloud cools the earth scorched by the sun with its rain of nectar, you, through the rain of your nectar-like mercy, grant immortality to the practitioner who is scorched by the **threefold miseries** <note>original: "tritāpa," referring to suffering caused by the self, by other living beings, and by natural forces</note>. O "Bride of the Destroyer of the Three Cities," you are the power <term>Shakti</term> of the Great Lord Shiva, who destroyed the demon named Tripura along with his strongholds in heaven, earth, and the underworld.

The word **Camphor** <note>original: "Karpūram"</note>—when stripped of its middle and final sounds (the 'r'-joined 'p' and the 'm'), and its vowels (the 'a' and 'u' sounds), leaving only the consonants—is reduced to the cluster "**KR**." When this is joined with the **Crescent Moon and Point** <note>the nasal 'M' sound</note> and the **Left Eye** <note>a traditional tantric code for the long vowel 'Ī'</note>, it becomes "**KRĪṀ**." This is your "Seed" <term>Bīja</term>, the causal source of your mantra-body. Those people who recite this three times, their minds absorbed in the meaning of the mantra, find that from the hollow of their mouths, speech—consisting of both prose and poetry—shines forth and flows effortlessly and without exertion. Furthermore, it is heard that practitioners of the **Islamic faith**, desiring the attainment of liberation, recite this same seed-syllable of **Kalika of the Space**, having adapted it into the Arabic language as the mantra "**Karom**." Likewise, practitioners of the **Christian faith** also constantly recite the mantra "**Christ**" <note>original: "Krāiṣṭa"</note> with the desire for salvation. || 1 ||

## Explanation of the Inherent Nature of the Verse || 1 ||

->Having bowed with devotion to the holy feet of the yogi **Ramananda**,<-
->this "Explanation of the Inherent Nature," which bestows pure bliss, is composed.<-

**O Mother** (The word "Mother" <note>Mātā</note> refers to one who measures or gives—derived from *mā* + *tṛc*), she who grants worldly enjoyment and liberation according to whether one's desires are selfish or selfless. **O You who are beautiful with the luster of a dark rain-cloud** <gloss>1</gloss>, she is to be meditated upon as blue-black because she is the condensed luminous energy of pure goodness <term>Sattva</term> and because she is the Space of Consciousness <term>Chid-akasha</term>. **O Bride of the Destroyer of the Three Cities**...

---

<margin>1. "Because both the Goddess and Shiva take the form of the infinite space, their bodies appear dark." — **Yoga-Vasistha**. (The Sanskrit text notes that the word "Shiva" here refers to both the Goddess and the God in union).</margin>
<margin>"In the state of liberation, one should meditate upon the Goddess, who resembles the cloudless sky and is attainable through devotion." — **Vidhurāsāra-samuccaya**.</margin>
<margin>2. "The individual soul who plays in the three cities (the states of waking, dreaming, and deep sleep)—from that soul, this entire manifold world is born. That ground of being, which is unbroken consciousness and bliss, is where the three cities eventually dissolve." — **Kaivalya Upanishad**.</margin>

It begins a deeper philosophical explanation of the Goddess's dark complexion as a symbol of the infinite space of consciousness.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the esoteric commentary on the "Hymn Beginning with Camphor," specifically focusing on the metaphysical breakdown of the Goddess's seed-syllable (mantra) and her role in granting liberation.</meta>

# Hymn Beginning with Camphor

He who destroys the "Three Cities"—the subtle, causal, and physical bodies—is the Giver of Disembodied Liberation. Your power <term>Shakti</term> is that very force; it is you alone who grants absolute liberation <term>Kaivalya</term>.

When the word "Camphor" <note>Karpūram</note> is stripped of its middle and final sounds, we find a deeper meaning. In the Root Nature <term>Mula-prakriti</term>, which consists of the three fundamental qualities of **Purity** <term>Sattva</term>, **Passion** <term>Rajas</term>, and **Inertia** <term>Tamas</term>, the middle sound (the letter "U") represents Passion, and the final sound (the letter "M") represents Inertia. Once these are removed, what remains is the state of Pure Purity.

This essence is joined with the "Moon" <note>the nasal 'm' sound or Bindu</note> and the "Left Eye" <note>the long 'ī' vowel</note>. This represents the Power that grants the liberation of Nirvana, as well as the **Power of Illusion** <term>Maya-shakti</term>—characterized by Pure Purity—which fulfills the desires of the practitioner.

"Camphor" signifies the manifested universe, which is the Absolute with qualities <term>Saguna Brahman</term>. Your "Seed" <term>Bīja</term> is the causal state of the universe. Though you are essentially without qualities <term>Nirguna</term>—embodying **Existence, Consciousness, and Bliss** <term>Sat-Cit-Ananda</term>—you become the "seed-form" for the creation of the world when you are joined with the power of Illusion, which is characterized by the karma and time of living beings. When the time comes for beings to experience the fruits of their past actions, you initiate creation. This "seed" is expressed as the sound **KRĪṀ**, representing your nature as Pure Purity and Existence-Consciousness-Bliss.

Those practitioners who recite this sound—which is multifaceted and represents the **Triple Power** <note>the powers of Will, Knowledge, and Action</note> through its divisions into pure, passionate, and inert qualities, as well as the physical, subtle, and causal levels—attain the "Fourfold Aims of Life": **Duty, Prosperity, Desire, and Liberation**, even if they do not consciously seek them. This is achieved by meditating on the mantra as being non-different from one's own self.

As stated in the *Vishvasara Tantra*, in the "Successive Hymn of the Auspicious Kali of the South":

> ->O Mother! The perfected ones chant this triple-seed of yours for the sake of liberation.<-
> ->Indeed, Mother, they become perfected at your lotus feet within just three days. (1)<-

***

1. The sound 'A' should be known as Purity <term>Sattva</term>; the sound 'U' is remembered as Passion <term>Rajas</term>; the sound 'M' is declared as Inertia <term>Tamas</term>. Through these three, Nature <term>Prakriti</term> is expressed. — *The Extraction of Knowledge* <note>Jnanasankalini Tantra</note>.
One should unite with the sound through meditation, then contemplate the supreme state beyond sound. — *The Upanishad on the Drop of the Absolute* <note>Brahmabindu Upanishad</note>.

2. Because the letter 'Ka' is supremely brilliant, it is the pure spark of Knowledge-Consciousness. Through its union with the blazing fire-syllable, it is auspicious and filled with all splendor. O Goddess, with the long 'Ī', it grants the desires of the practitioner. Because the point <term>Bindu</term> is unwavering, it grants the fruit of absolute liberation. — *The Wish-Fulfilling Tree of Tantra* <note>Tantrakalpadruma</note>.
And in the *Todala Tantra*: The letter 'K' grants Duty, O Goddess; 'Ī' grants Prosperity; 'R' grants Desire; and 'M' grants Liberation. By pronouncing each one, O Goddess, you grant the liberation of Nirvana.

3. My nature is Identity, the Consciousness-Awareness that is the one name of the Supreme Absolute. Through union with my own Power, I have reached the state of being the "Seed." The unmanifest causal body is the seed itself. In this, the world exists in seed-form, and from this, the "Emergence of the Sign" <note>Lingodbhava</note> occurs. — *The Song of the Goddess* <note>Devi Gita</note>.

4. One should always meditate on the self as being the form of Kali. — *The Kali Upanishad*.

<summary>This page provides a Tantric linguistic analysis of the seed-syllable "KRĪṀ," explaining how the word "Camphor" encodes the Goddess's power to grant liberation and fulfill worldly desires through the three qualities of nature.</summary>
<keywords>Krim, seed-syllable, Bija, Sattva, Rajas, Tamas, liberation, Kali, Tantra, Saguna Brahman, Nirguna, Kaivalya</keywords>

--- Page 101 ---
<meta>This page continues the esoteric commentary on the Goddess's hymns, moving from the first seed-syllable (Krīṁ) to the second—the Kurcca Bīja (Hūṁ)—and explaining its power to grant wisdom, wealth, and spiritual innocence.</meta>

->With the moon and the "left ear" vowel, if a dull-witted man even once recites the "Ishana" seed-syllable doubled, O Great Goddess, he conquers the Lord of Speech and the Lord of Wealth. O You who wear the crescent moon, O You adorned with the horrific earrings of children, he becomes capable of enchanting even the most beautiful of women. (2)<-

### Commentary

O **Great Goddess** <term>Maheshvari</term>—You who are the power of the Great Lord who rules even over Brahma, Vishnu, and Rudra. O **Crescent-Crowned One** <term>Chandrardhacuda</term>—You whose forehead is marked by the half-moon. O **Adorned with Horrific Children** <term>Mahaghorabalavatamsa</term>—You who wear earrings made of the corpses of children <note>In Tantric iconography, these "children" symbolize the pure, desireless state of the practitioner, as explained in the metaphysical section below</note>. 

Even a **dull-witted person**—one of slow intellect—if he chants Your **Seed-Syllable** <term>Bija-mantra</term> just once. This syllable is constructed as: **Ishana** (the letter 'Ha') joined with the **Left Ear** (the long 'U' vowel) and the **Moon** (the nasal 'M' or Bindu). From this, the single-syllable mantra **"HŪṀ"**—belonging to your second form, the Goddess **Tara**—is revealed. 

If one chants this **doubled** <note>original: "dvandvam"</note>—that is, "Hūṁ Hūṁ"—then for the rest of his life, he conquers and surpasses **Brihaspati** (the Lord of Eloquence) and even **Kubera** (the Lord of Wealth). He becomes capable of enchanting groups of **lotus-eyed women** <note>original: "ambujakshi-vridam"</note>, meaning he gains irresistible charm. The seeker becomes exceedingly learned, wealthy, and beautiful. 

As stated in the *Tara-Rahasya*: "Because she is the Savior, she is always called Tara; she is certainly the same as Kali." Furthermore, the *Krama-Stotra* says:

> By chanting Your double "Kurcca" syllable <note>The sound HŪṀ</note>, one becomes worthy of worship even by gods and demons. All attain wealth and scholarship—what else is there to say, O Goddess who is called both the Supreme and the Beyond? (2)

### Metaphysical Explanation (2)

O **Great Goddess**—You who possess the great majesty of creation, preservation, and destruction. O **Crescent-Crowned One** <gloss>1</gloss>—You who grant the final liberation of Nirvana. O **Adorned with Horrific Children** <gloss>2</gloss>—This address signifies that the seeker who is "child-like"—free from desire and as changeless as the Great Lord himself—is like a precious earring to You. The meaning is that a seeker who has attained the state of child-like innocence is extremely dear to the Goddess, who is the embodiment of the Absolute <term>Brahman</term>. The "Ishana" syllable with the moon and left ear...

---

<note>1. "The moon is described as being fixed on the forehead of the Eternal One, who is the form of Time, the Red One, and the soul of Shiva." — *Mahanirvana Tantra*</note>

<note>2. "Just as a child plays, having left behind the pains of the body, so the wise man delights in the Reality, free from 'mine-ness' and ego, and full of happiness." — *Viveka-Chudamani* (Crest-Jewel of Discrimination); Also from the *Brahmopanishad*: "As a young girl attains desireless bliss..."</note>

<summary>This page explains the second verse of the hymn, revealing the construction of the seed-syllable HŪṀ (the Kurcca Bīja) associated with the Goddess Tara. It interprets the Goddess's fearsome ornaments as symbols of the practitioner's spiritual innocence and detachment.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the esoteric commentary on the Goddess’s seed-syllables. Having previously discussed the first two syllables, it now concludes the explanation of the "Hūṁ" syllable and introduces the "Hrīṁ" syllable along with the full name of the Goddess, Dakshina Kalika.</meta>

# Hymn to Kali [Beginning with Camphor]

Surrounded by <gloss>1</gloss> the moon <note>representing the letter 'U'</note>, immortality, strength of speech, and the power of attraction. **Ishana** <gloss>2</gloss> refers to the Lord <note>the letter 'H'</note>. Thus, this is your **other seed-syllable** <gloss>3</gloss> <note>original: "hūṁ-bījātmaka"</note> which embodies the state of primal cause. The **double** <gloss>4</gloss> represents the unified form of Shiva and Shakti <note>the masculine and feminine principles of the divine</note>. If a practitioner ever chants this even once, or contemplates it through mental recitation, then that seeker—becoming possessed of both worldly and spiritual knowledge and great wealth—becomes capable of enchanting the entire world. This is the underlying meaning. (2)

### The Lord dwelling in fire, joined with the shining moon and the left eye,
### This other doubled seed-syllable of Yours, O Kali with disheveled hair, those who chant;
### They slay their haters and bring even the three worlds under their power—
### O Dakshina, whose mouth bears two streams of blood from the corners of Your lips! (3)

->Commentary<-

O **You with disheveled hair** <term>Vigalita-chikure</term>—You whose hair is loose and free. O **Kali**—You who are the very form of the Absolute <term>Brahman</term>. O **You whose mouth bears two streams of blood**—You whose face is beautified by two streaks of blood trickling from the corners of Your lips. 

**Dwelling in fire** <term>Vaishvanara-sthah</term> refers to the letter 'Ra'. **Joined with the shining moon** refers to the nasal dot <term>Bindu</term>. **Joined with the left eye** refers to the long vowel 'Ī'. **The Lord** <term>Isha</term> refers to the letter 'Ha'. Together, these form the other seed-syllable: **"HRĪṀ"**, which is the mantra of the Goddess Tripura Sundari. 

**Doubled** means the aforementioned seed-syllable is repeated <note>i.e., "Hrīṁ Hrīṁ"</note>. Those people who chant this, along with the three-syllable name **"Dakshine"** <note>forming the mantra "Hrīṁ Hrīṁ Dakshine Kalike"</note>, they **slay their haters**—meaning they destroy their enemies. They **bring the three worlds**—consisting of heaven, earth, and the underworld, including gods, humans, and serpent-beings—**under their power**, meaning they bring them into a state of subjection. This is the meaning. (3)

->Explanation of the Form (3)<-

O **You with disheveled hair**—meaning You who are free from the distortions of vanity or the arrangement of hair; You who are "without modification" <term>Nirvikara</term>. **Kali**: The letter **K** <gloss>5</gloss> is the Absolute; **A** <gloss>6</gloss> is the Infinite; **L** <gloss>7</gloss> is the World-Soul; **I** <gloss>8</gloss> is the Subtle Power.

---

1. The letter 'U' is the subtle Bhairava <note>a fierce form of Shiva</note>, the long-nosed Saraswati <note>Goddess of Speech</note>, the Playful One, the Creator of Obstacles, and the Power of Attraction.
2. "A person the size of a thumb, like a flame without smoke; he is the Lord <term>Ishana</term> of what was and what shall be. He is the first, he is the Sovereign." — *Katha Upanishad*
3. "By these two seed-syllables of sound, the entire treasury of sound is awakened." — *Yamala Tantra*
4. "From the union of Shiva and Shakti, the King of Mantras is born. She consists of that mantra, which is the embodiment of supreme bliss." — *Gandharva Tantra*
5. The letter 'K' represents the Lord of Anger, Mahakali, the God of Love, the Illuminator, the Auspicious City, the Beautiful Woman, and the Supreme Self.
6. The letter 'A' is the Infinite Victory.
7. The letter 'L' represents Indra <note>King of Gods</note>, the demoness Putana, the Earth, Vishnu, and the name of Shakra. It is the World-Soul, the powerful mantra, and the essence of the sacred mountain Meru.
8. The letter 'I' is the Subtle One, the Shalmali tree, Wisdom, the Moon, the Sun, and the Well-Hidden One. — *From the Dictionary of Tantric Syllables*.

It also provides a mystical breakdown of the letters in the name "Kali" using various Tantric authorities.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on the third verse of the hymn. The previous page ended with an analysis of the Goddess's disheveled hair and her status as the Absolute (Brahman). This page explains the symbolic meaning of the blood on her face and the esoteric composition of the seed-syllable "Hrīṁ," while exploring her different forms such as Dakshina Kali and Tripura Sundari.</meta>

# Annotated Commentary

The letter **Ka** represents Brahma <note>the Creator</note>, and **A** represents the Infinite <note>original: "Ananta"</note>. By this, the Great Goddess is indicated to be without beginning or end, infinite, subtle, and the very soul of the universe—meaning, "O You whose form is the Beginningless and Endless Absolute." It is said in the *Adbhuta Ramayana*, within the "Hymn to Sita" composed by Rama:

> I bow to that form called the Primal Spirit <note>original: "Purusha"</note>, which is without beginning or end, the soul of the universe, existing in various states yet beyond the reach of Nature, unchanging, unmanifest, and all-pervading.

**Whose mouth bears two streams of blood** <term>Srikva-dvandva-asra-dhara</term>—this refers to the face where streams of blood trickle from the two corners of the mouth. Here, the blood signifies the **Quality of Passion** <term>Rajas-guna</term>; because of this, the Great Goddess is described as being free from passion and composed of the **Quality of Purity** <term>Sattva-guna</term>, and is thus defined as **Viraja** <gloss>1</gloss> <note>The Stainless One</note>. However, from the perspective of the Goddess **Tripura Sundari** <note>the "Beautiful One of the Three Cities"</note>, the Quality of Passion indicates the Goddess’s red-colored form. 

**Dwelling in fire** <note>the letter 'Ra'</note>, **luminous**, joined with the **holder of the moon** <note>the nasal dot or 'Bindu'</note> which grants the power of nectar, and combined with the **left eye** <note>the long vowel 'Ī'</note>, she is joined with **Maya** <note>the syllable 'H'</note>, which is the essence of pure goodness. The **Lord** <term>Isha</term> refers to the subtle state of the seed-syllable of the Supreme Lord. This is your **other** <note>the second</note> **doubled seed-syllable** <note>original: "hrīṁ hrīṁ"</note>, which is the essence of the unified form of Shiva and Shakti <note>the masculine and feminine divine principles</note>. 

Furthermore, those practitioners who chant or meditate upon the name **Dakshina** <gloss>3</gloss>, which consists of three syllables and represents the form of Truth, Consciousness, and Bliss <term>Saccidananda</term> that grants liberation—they **slay** or destroy their "haters," meaning the obstacles to spiritual practice such as lust and anger. They also bring the **three worlds** <note>earth, atmosphere, and heaven</note> into a state of submission. 

From the perspective of the Goddess Sundari, the address "O Kali" refers to the Red Kali, who is the third Great Wisdom <term>Mahavidya</term>, the auspicious Tripura Sundari. As stated in the *Brihan-nila Tantra*:

> This Narayani is Kali; she is Tara, the one who carries the Void. Sundari is this Red Kali, and she is also the sounding Bhairavi.

And likewise, in another Tantra:

> Kali is said to be of two types, distinguished by the colors black and red. The black one is known as Dakshina, while the red one is considered Sundari.

**O Dakshina** means You who are worshipped by the "South-facing" form of Shiva <term>Dakshinamurti Bhairava</term>. **In three syllables** refers to the mantra "Aiṁ Klīṁ Hrīṁ."

---

->Footnotes<-

१. "In the supreme golden sheath is the stainless, partless Absolute. That is the pure Light of lights; it is what the knowers of the Self realize." — *Mundaka Upanishad*.

२. "The letter 'H' is the gross body; 'R' is the subtle body; 'Ī' is the causal soul; and the 'Hrim' sound itself is the Fourth state <term>Turiya</term> <note>pure consciousness</note>." — *Devi Gita*.

३. "Because the Son of the Sun <note>Yama, the God of Death</note>, who stays in the Southern quarter, flees in fear from all sides at the name of Kali, she is sung of in the three worlds as **Dakshina Kali**." — *Nirvana Tantra*. 
Also in the *Kamakhya Tantra*: "Just as the 'Dakshina' <note>the priest's fee</note> at the end of a ritual ensures the success of its fruits, so this Goddess grants the fruit of liberation to all. Therefore, O fair-colored one, she is called Dakshina Kali. The male principle is called 'Dakshina' <note>right/skillful</note> and the 'Shakti' <note>power</note> is called 'Vama' <note>left</note>. When she, the Left, overcomes the Right, she grants Great Liberation. Thus she is sung of in the three worlds by the name Dakshina."

<vocab>Kalika (The Goddess of Time), Adyantarahita (Without beginning or end), Adbhuta Ramayana (A 14th-century Sanskrit version of the Ramayana emphasizing Sita's power), Sita-stotra (Hymn to Sita), Rajo-guna (The quality of activity/passion), Biraja (Stainless/Pure), Tripura Sundari (The Beautiful Goddess of the Three Cities), Vaishvanara (The Universal Fire), Dakshina (South/Favorable/Sacrificial fee), Brihannila Tantra (A major Tantric text), Dakshina Kali (A popular form of Kali), Nirvana Tantra (A scripture on liberation), Kamakhya Tantra (A text focusing on the Goddess of desire), Mundaka Upanishad (Ancient Vedic text), Devi Gita (The Song of the Goddess)</vocab>

<keywords>Dakshina Kali, Hrīṁ, Bija Mantra, Tripura Sundari, Rajas, Sattva, Liberation, Tantra, Brahman</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Karpūradi Stotra, specifically moving from the general attributes of the Goddess to the specific rewards of her mantra and her iconographic form. It follows the previous discussion on her nature as the Absolute (Brahman).</meta>

# Hymn to the Camphor-like One (Karpūradi Stotra)

And so, it is said in the *Kramastotra* <note>The Hymn of the Sequence</note>:

->Through the recitation of Your name, O Great Goddess, the fourth attainment <note>Liberation or Moksha</note> is achieved.<-
->By the supreme grace of the Ocean of Compassion, a man attains sovereignty over the realm of worldly existence. || 3 ||<-

->In the upper left lotus-hand is a sword, and in the lower hand a severed head;<-
->On the right are the gestures of fearlessness and granting boons, O Kali, O Destroyer of the sins of the three worlds.<-
->Those who recite this name or meditate upon the power of Your mantra, O Mother,<-
->In their hands lie the eight perfections of the Three-Eyed One <note>Shiva</note>, O You of revealed teeth. || 4 ||<-

### Commentary

**O Destroyer of the sins of the three worlds** <note>original: "Trijagad-agha-hare"</note> means the one who destroys the sins of the people in the three realms. **O You of revealed teeth** <gloss>1</gloss> means one whose teeth are visible. **O Mother** <note>original: "Amba"</note>. **O Giver of Liberation** <note>original: "Dakshine"</note>, those practitioners who recite the power of your mantra—consisting of the previously mentioned three "Kali" seed-syllables, the two "Hum" syllables <term>Kurcha</term> <gloss>2</gloss>, the two "Hrim" syllables <term>Maya</term>, the word "Dakshine" consisting of three syllables, making a ten-syllable mantra, as well as the three-syllable name "Kalike"—and who meditate upon the following form:

In the upper left lotus-hand, a **sword**; and below it, in the lower hand, a **severed head**. In the upper right lotus-hand, the **gesture of fearlessness** <note>Abhaya-mudra</note>, and in the lower right lotus-hand, the **boon-granting gesture** <note>Varada-mudra</note>. Those who contemplate You in this specific way, with the sword in the upper left and so on, find that the **eight perfections** <gloss>3</gloss> <note>Siddhis</note> of the Three-Eyed One <note>Shiva</note>—the eight types of power such as "Minuteness" <note>Anima</note> and others—are held in their very hands, meaning they are obtained without effort. || 4 ||

### Explanation of the Form

**O Destroyer of the sins of the three worlds**: You who destroy the various physical, vocal, and mental sins of the three worlds. **O You of revealed teeth**: This indicates the white row of teeth representing the **Quality of Purity** <term>Sattva-guna</term> biting the lolling tongue, which is red to represent the **Quality of Passion** <term>Rajas-guna</term>. This signifies that She destroys passion and ignorance through the power of purity.

---

<margin>1. "One with projecting teeth, supreme, eternal, her body adorned with blood." — *Yogini Tantra*.
Also in the *Tara Kalpa*: "Dark like a fresh rain cloud, fearsome with revealed teeth, with large breasts."</margin>
<margin>2. "Maya" and "Lajja" refer to the syllable *Hrīṁ*; *Hūṁ* is called "Kurcha."</margin>
<margin>3. The eight perfections are: Minuteness (Anima), Greatness (Mahima), Lightness (Laghima), Reach (Prapti), Irresistible Will (Prakamya), Lordship (Ishitva), Control (Vashitva), and the power to suppress desires.
"A man obtains these eight lordly qualities, which indicate the path to Supreme Liberation." — *Markandeya Purana*.</margin>

<summary>This page explains the fourth verse of the Karpūradi Stotra, detailing the iconography of Kali (her four hands and revealed teeth) and the spiritual rewards of her ten-syllable mantra. It identifies the eight Siddhis (supernatural powers) granted to the devotee and provides symbolic interpretations of her physical features through the lens of the three Gunas (qualities of nature).</summary>
<keywords>Kali, Mantra, Siddhi, Anima, Sattva, Rajas, Yogini Tantra, Markandeya Purana, Iconography, Sword, Severed Head</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the line-by-line commentary on the fourth verse of the Karpūradi Stotra, specifically explaining the symbolic meaning of the Goddess’s physical attributes and the construction of her specific mantra. It then introduces the fifth verse, which details the "Great Mantra" (Mahāmantra) of Kali.</meta>

## With Commentary

In the vocative case: **O Mother** <note>original: "Amba"</note>. **O Giver of Liberation** <note>original: "Dakshine"</note>. **Your mantra-glory** <note>original: "manu-vibhavam"</note> refers to your form as the power of consciousness consisting of the three "seed-syllables" <term>Bija: the fundamental sound-units of a power or deity</term>, your form as the quality of purity <term>Sattva</term> consisting of the two "Kurcha" seeds <note>the sound "Hūm"</note>, and your form as purity dominated by activity <term>Rajas</term> consisting of the two "Maya" seeds <note>the sound "Hrīm"</note>. 

Likewise, **O Kalika, O Kalika**, by reciting and meditating upon this name, one contemplates the form of Truth, Consciousness, and Bliss <term>Sat-Cit-Ananda</term>, which has neither beginning nor end. You, **holding a female form** <gloss>1</gloss>, with your upper-left hand hold the **sword of knowledge** <gloss>2</gloss> to cut through the "noose of delusion" that binds practitioners who seek no worldly rewards. Below that hand, you hold a **severed head**, which represents the foundation of the knowledge of reality, free from the stains of passion. Similarly, with your upper-right hand, you grant **fearlessness** to those practitioners who still seek worldly goals, and with the hand below that, you grant **desired boons**. 

The inner meaning is that for those practitioners who meditate upon You in this form, the **eight perfections, beginning with the power to become infinitesimal** <term>Anima-siddhi: the yogic power to shrink one's body</term>, are obtained without effort. || 4 ||

->The first letter of the classes, joined with Fire, adorned by the Moon and the beautiful Beloved;<-
->Three of those, then a pair of Kurchas, followed by a pair of the Bashful ones, O Smiling-faced one;<-
->Then adding the two "Tha"s at the end; O Mother, those who recite this mantra,<-
->Meditating on Your own form, O Beloved of the Destroyer of Love,<-
->They become like the God of Love himself, watched by the playful, lotus-petal eyes of the Goddess of Fortune. || 5 ||<-

## Commentary

**O Smiling-faced one** means one with a face showing a gentle smile. **O Beloved of the Destroyer of Love** <note>referring to Shiva, who burned Kama, the god of love, to ashes</note> means the enchantress of the one who ended Desire. **O Mother**, those people who [recite the mantra]: **Joined with Fire** <gloss>3</gloss> means the letter "Ra" placed above. **Adorned by the Moon and the beautiful Beloved** <gloss>4</gloss>—the "Moon" is the nasal dot <note>Anusvara/Bindu</note> and the "Beloved" is the long vowel "ī"—joined with these. **The first of the classes** refers to the letter "Ka," which is the first of the five groups of consonants. Combined, this forms the seed-syllable **"Krīm."**

**Three of those** means a triad of that syllable. **Then** means following that. **A pair of Kurchas** means the two "Hūm" seeds. **A pair of the Bashful ones** means the two "Hrīm" seeds. **Below that** means following those. **The two "Tha"s** <gloss>5</gloss> refers to the word **"Svāhā."** By **adding** or joining these together [the full mantra is formed]. **Your own form** refers to the description beginning with "Beautiful as a bank of dark clouds," and so forth.

---

<note>1. "One should meditate on the Goddess in female form, or one should meditate on Her in male form, O Dear One; or one should meditate on the formless Brahman, whose body is Truth, Consciousness, and Bliss."</note>
<note>2. "Therefore, with the sword of knowledge, one quickly cuts through the entirety of karmic bonds—those made with desire and those without—and remains in the pure Self." (From the *Shivadharmottara*). Likewise in the *Yogini Tantra*: "O Goddess, one stays the 'animal' of sin and merit with the sword of knowledge."</note>
<note>3. "Ra" is red, the "fire-seed" (vowel-less R), and is considered luminous. (From the *Dictionary of Sounds*).</note>
<note>4. "Ī" is the form of the Goddess, the Great Illusion... and "Rati" (the Beloved) is the nourisher. (From the *Dictionary of Sounds*).</note>
<note>5. "Thi-Tha" (the two Thas) is Svaha, the beloved of Fire. (From the *Dictionary of Sounds*).</note>

<sig>2</sig>

<summary>This page explains the symbolic iconography of the Goddess Kali, where her sword represents the destruction of delusion and her four arms balance the needs of both worldly and spiritual seekers. It also provides a coded "recipe" for constructing her fifteen-syllable Mahāmantra using esoteric associations for Sanskrit letters.</summary>
<keywords>Kali, mantra construction, Krim, Hum, Hrim, Svaha, sword of knowledge, eight siddhis, Sat-Cit-Ananda, Karpuradi Stotra</keywords>

--- Page 106 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical breakdown of the Mahāmantra (Great Mantra) of Kali from the Karpūradistotra. While the previous page focused on the initial syllables and the Goddess’s physical form, this page explains the spiritual results of the 22-syllable mantra and the internal meditative identity between the practitioner and the deity.</meta>

Your form is composed of the **twenty-two syllable mantra**. Meditating <gloss>1</gloss>—that is, contemplating—upon this, they chant the "Queen of Mantras" <term>Vidyārājñī: a supreme feminine mantra</term> again and again, beginning with *Krīm Krīm Krīm* and ending with *Svāhā*. Those practitioners <term>Sadhakas</term> then possess eyes that are like the petals of the lotus used in the playful, dancing sport of Lakshmi <note>the Goddess of Fortune and Beauty</note>. They become **as lovely in form as Kamadeva** <note>the God of Love</note>. || 5 ||

### Explanation of the Essential Form || 5 ||

**O Smiling-faced One, whose form is Eternal Bliss.** O Consort of the Destroyer of Smara <note>Smara is a name for Kamadeva (Desire); his destroyer is Lord Shiva</note>. The implication is that She is the power of Shiva who destroys lust, anger, and other passions within her own practitioners. **O Mother**, whoever <gloss>2</gloss>—even the lowest of men—if they chant and meditate while contemplating your **Light-filled Essential Form**, which resides in the seed-syllables. That form is **as lovely as the moon**, endowed with coolness and beauty. It is the **primordial element of creation**, the first of the consonant classes <note>referring to the letter 'Ka' in the syllable Krīm</note>, consisting of pure consciousness. 

That form is a **triad** representing the three qualities <term>Gunas</term> of Purity, Passion, and Inertia <term>Sattva, Rajas, and Tamas</term>. Afterwards, it is joined by the **pair of Kurcha seeds** <note>the syllable "Hūm"</note>, which are the form of the Absolute Sound <term>Shabda-Brahman</term>. Then comes the **pair of Lajja seeds** <note>the syllable "Hrīm"</note>, representing the Absolute dappled with the power of Illusion <term>Maya</term>. Below those, by adding the **two "Tha" syllables** <note>the phonetic components of "Svāhā"</note>, which represent the illuminating power of Fire, one contemplates your **Essential Form** <gloss>3</gloss>. This is the form of one's own Self, recognizing no difference between the individual soul and the Divine. Those practitioners attain the **playful dancing sport of Lakshmi** <gloss>4</gloss>, meaning they achieve the beauty, wealth, and enjoyment associated with the Goddess of Fortune.

---

1. <note>original: "krīm-kārī mastakaṃ devi..."</note> "The syllable *Krīm* is the head, O Goddess; the syllable *Krīm* is the forehead..." and so on. This mapping of mantra sounds to the body can be found in the ninth chapter of the *Shāktānanda-taranginī*.
2. <note>original: "mahā-mahānnavidye-yaṃ..."</note> "This is the great, great knowledge known as Kalika. By attaining it, even the lowest of men reaches the liberation of extinction <term>Nirvana</term>." Furthermore, the *Kālīkula-sarvasva* states: "Whether with faith or without faith, whatever man remembers Her, having overcome difficulties and misfortune, he goes to the highest state."
3. <note>original: "sarvadā kālī-rūpam-ātmānaṃ..."</note> "One should always meditate upon the Self as being the form of Kali," according to the *Kālikopanishad*. Also, in the *Thousand Names Hymn* <term>Sahasranama-stotra</term>: "He who praises the Auspicious One <note>Shiva</note> while contemplating the Self as the soul of Kali, and meditates on the Guru as equal to Shiva, he is indeed the holy Eternal Shiva." Furthermore, in the *Yoginī-tantra*: "To him who meditates on the Self for even a moment, thinking 'I am Brahman,' the Goddess grants a fruit that is beyond counting. One should always meditate on one's own body as the form of one's Chosen Deity, and meditate on the entire universe as that same form."
4. <note>original: "nirvāṇa-tantre—asya grahaṇa-mātreṇa..."</note> The *Nirvāna-tantra* says: "By merely receiving this mantra, a man becomes Narayana <note>God</note>." In the *Shyāmā-kavacha* from the *Bhairava-tantra*: "Even Narayana, by holding this, became a woman and led Shiva, the Lord of Yoga, to desire." And the *Nirvāna-tantra* adds: "Through meditation one gains everything; through meditation one takes the form of Vishnu."

<summary>This page explains the construction and spiritual efficacy of the 22-syllable Kali mantra, emphasizing that meditation on these sounds leads to beauty, worldly success, and the realization of non-duality (the identity of the soul with the Goddess).</summary>
<keywords>Kali, Mahamantra, Krīm, Hūm, Hrīm, Svāhā, Shaktism, Advaita, Lakshmi, Mantra Yoga, Tantra</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical and spiritual explanation of the Karpūradistotra, specifically focusing on the fruits (results) of chanting the mantra and the mystical identity between the practitioner and the divine. It transitions from describing the syllables to the physical and spiritual transformations undergone by the devotee.</meta>

# With Commentary

[The practitioner’s eyes are] like the lotus used in the playful, dancing sport of Lakshmi <note>The Goddess of Fortune and Beauty</note>. This means they become the foundation for her beauty. They are like Vishnu, who is "Lotus-eyed" <term>Kamaladṛk</term>. As stated in the *Glory of the Goddess* <note>original: "Devī Māhātmya"</note>: "She whose sole dwelling is the heart of the enemy of Kaitabha" <note>Referring to Vishnu, the slayer of the demon Kaitabha</note>. They become **capable of assuming any form at will** <term>Kāmarūpa</term>. Just as the "Lotus-eyed" Vishnu assumed ten different forms such as the Fish, Tortoise, and Boar, as well as the form of the enchantress Mohini, the practitioners of Kali's mantra are able to take on any form they desire. This is the underlying meaning. ||

It is stated in the *Hymn of the Sequence* <note>original: "Kramastotra"</note>:

> Mother, listen to the fruit of your own name, which the wise consider the four-fold goals of life <note>Virtue, Wealth, Pleasure, and Liberation</note>. By worshiping the **three seed-syllables** <note>Krīm Krīm Krīm</note>, the king of sages attains sovereignty over the gods.
> 
> Chanting the **pair of Kurcha seeds** <note>Hūm Hūm</note>, they become perfected beings <term>Siddhas</term> in the form of the "Man-Lion" <gloss>1</gloss> <note>the Narasimha avatar of Vishnu</note>. Through the chanting of the **pair of Lajja seeds** <note>Hrīm Hrīm</note>, people attain success in their hearts' desires. 
> 
> At the end, they speak the word of the "Eater of Oblations" <note>Fire, referring to the word 'Svāhā'</note>; this they call the extraction of the mantra. || 5 ||

> Whether one, or two, or three of the most secret seed-syllables, 
> Joined with Her name, the wise always meditate and chant. 
> In their lotus-eyes Lakshmi <note>Goddess of Beauty</note> plays, and on their moon-like faces 
> Dwells the Goddess of Speech <note>Saraswati</note>, while contemplating the Mother who wears a garland of skulls, her throat swaying, and her full breasts heavy. || 6 ||

->**Commentary**<-

**O Goddess** <note>original: "Devi"</note>, you who are "Shining by Nature." **O Mother who wears a garland of skulls**, whose throat sways with the movement. **O You with full, heavy breasts.** **Extremely secret** means it is to be kept very hidden from those who are not practitioners. **Each one** <gloss>2</gloss> refers to the previously mentioned seed-syllables individually. **Two** refers to two syllables, and **three** refers to three syllables. **Joined with Her name** means ending with the name "Dakshina Kalika" in the vocative case. This refers to creating a mantra of one, two, or three syllables. Or, it refers to the **Queen of Knowledge** <term>Vidyārājñī</term> consisting of all twenty-two syllables. 

**Meditating** <gloss>3</gloss> means contemplating the meaning of those mantras, as described in your meditation manual <term>Dhyana</term>: your form which is dark like a fresh rain cloud, naked <note>original: "Digambari," meaning "clad in space"</note>, with full breasts, wearing a garland of skulls, and having disheveled hair. While they meditate and chant one of the previously mentioned mantras, for those practitioners, **Lakshmi** <note>the Goddess of Fortune</note> **plays within the lotus of their eyes**.

---

1. Having the form of Vishnu.
2. In the *Kalika Scripture* <note>original: "Kālikā-śruti"</note>, it says: "First, one may chant the whole Knowledge [mantra], or one, or two, or the name joined with the three seeds; thus one finds their path."
3. In the *Tantra of Elemental Purification* <note>original: "Bhūta-śuddhi-tantra"</note>: "Whatever deity is intended for a mantra, one should perform the mental chant while contemplating that specific form."
