<meta>This page continues the commentary on the sixth verse of the Karpūradistotra. It explains the spiritual rewards (phala) of chanting the mantra, specifically focusing on how the practitioner attains the grace of both Lakshmi (wealth/beauty) and Saraswati (wisdom/speech). It also transitions into a "Nature/Esoteric Explanation" (Svarūpa-vyākhyā) which links the syllables of the mantra to the levels of cosmic and individual consciousness.</meta>

# Karpūradistotram

[Fortune] always resides there. Similarly, on the moon-like disk of their beautiful faces, the Goddess of Speech, Saraswati, plays and moves about. The meaning is that they become both wealthy and highly learned.

From the perspective of the Goddess Tripura Sundari: O Goddess Tripura Sundari! Those practitioners who take the three-syllable seed-mantra <note>original: "bīja"</note> "Sakala" and join it with your name "Hrim," chanting either just "Sakala-Hrim" (known as the Power Peak <term>Shakti-kūṭa</term> mantra), or two peaks together with the King of Desire <term>Kāmarāja-kūṭa</term>, or the supreme fifteen-syllable mantra <term>Pañcadaśākṣara-mantrarāja</term> composed of the three peaks of Wisdom, Desire, and Power—meditating on you as they chant—for them, the description of the "lotus eyes" and everything else follows as previously described. || 6 ||

---

### Esoteric Explanation of the Nature of the Verse || 6 ||

O Goddess, you who are the very form of self-illumination! O you whose throat shines with a charming and tender luster! O you who consist of the fifty letters <gloss>1</gloss> and are the embodiment of the Sound-Absolute <term>Śabda-brahman</term>. O you who are full with swelling breasts, protecting the three worlds by providing joy through the gift of your form, and granting liberation to practitioners through the nectar of your milk. 

Mother, there are those who meditate on your seeds—either individually, in pairs, or all three together—which are supreme, deeply secret, and "Sakala" <note>meaning "with attributes" or "manifested"</note>. They contemplate these as the individual forms of consciousness known as the Sleeper <term>Prājña</term>, the Dreamer <term>Taijasa</term>, and the Waker <term>Viśva</term>, as well as the collective cosmic forms known as the Lord <term>Īśa</term>, the Thread <term>Sūtra</term>, and the Universal <term>Virāṭ</term> <gloss>2</gloss>. They chant and constantly reflect on these through hearing, contemplation, and deep meditation <note>original: "śravaṇa-manana-nididhyāsana"</note>. 

For those practitioners of the Yoga of Knowledge <term>Jñāna-yogins</term>, the Goddess of Fortune <note>Lakshmi</note> plays in the lotus of their eyes <gloss>3</gloss>. By the mere fall of their compassionate glance, even others attain wealth and prosperity—what then need be said of the practitioners themselves? This is the underlying meaning. 

In the moon-like disk of their beautiful faces, the Goddess of Speech <note>Saraswati</note> plays, meaning their speech becomes filled with beautiful prose and poetry. Or, O Mother, those people who meditate on your "Sakala" form (the Sound-Absolute) as being non-different from their own soul, and chant these mantras—for those practitioners, the "lotus eyes" and other blessings follow as described. || 6 ||

---

1. "She who wears a garland of heads made of the fifty letters, smeared with dripping blood." — *Niruttara Tantra*. 
"The seed-syllable situated in my throat is beautified by the fifty letters." — *Kamadhenu Tantra*.
And so it is said in the *Vishvasara Tantra*: 
> "The Absolute, consisting of supreme bliss, is adorned as the Sound-Absolute. O Beloved, it resides within one's own body and is the essence of all mantras." 
In the *Radha Tantra*: "The fifty letters of the Mother-alphabet, from 'A' to 'Ksha,' are imperishable and indivisible, residing in the throat of Tripura. They appear in the colors of white, red, yellow, and black."

2. "The Sleeper <term>Prājña</term> is the causal self, the Dreamer <term>Taijasa</term> is the subtle body, and the Waker <term>Viśva</term> is the gross body—thus the threefold nature is proclaimed. Likewise, the Lord is described by the terms Lord <term>Īśa</term>, Thread <term>Sūtra</term>, and Universal <term>Virāṭ</term>. The first set represents the individual forms, while the latter is known as the collective soul." — *Devi Gita*.

3. "Having cast aside their natural rivalry, the Goddess of Fortune <note>Lakshmi</note> resides with stability in the mouth, which is the temple of the Goddess of Speech <note>Saraswati</note>." — *Bhairava Tantra*.

<summary>This page explains the spiritual and material rewards of meditating on the "Sakala" mantra, equating the practitioner's consciousness with the cosmic levels of waking, dreaming, and deep sleep. It emphasizes the rare union of wealth (Lakshmi) and wisdom (Saraswati) granted to the devotee.</summary>

<keywords>Tripura Sundari, Sakala Mantra, Lakshmi, Saraswati, Shabda-Brahman, Prajna, Taijasa, Vishva, 50 letters, Tantric commentary</keywords>

--- Page 109 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the spiritual rewards of the mantra in Verse 6 to a vivid meditative description of the Goddess Kali's physical and esoteric form in Verse 7. It continues the pattern of providing both a literal commentary and a deeper symbolic "Nature Explanation."</meta>

 सान्वय-सटीकम् ।
<note>With commentary and word-for-word interpretation.</note>

## Even a dull-witted man becomes a poet by meditating on You, O Mother—
## You, whose hips shine with a girdle made from the many arms of the dead;
## You, who are clothed by the directions <note>naked</note>, the Creatress of the three worlds, and the three-eyed one;
## You, who are immersed in the act of love with Mahakala upon the heart of a corpse on a couch in the cremation ground. || 7 ||

### Commentary

O Mother, Creatress of the three worlds! "Of the dead" <note>original: gatāsūnāṃ</note> refers to those who have passed away. "Hips shining with a waist-ornament fashioned from a multitude of arms" <note>original: bāhu-prakara-kṛta-kāñcī-parilasannitambāṃ</note>—this describes her waist-belt made of severed hands. "Clothed by the directions" <note>original: digvastrāṃ</note> means she is naked, having the sky as her garment. "Creatress of the three worlds" means she who ordains both the worldly enjoyment and the final liberation for the three realms. "Three-eyed" <gloss>1</gloss> means her eyes are the Moon, the Sun, and Fire. "On a couch in the cremation ground, on the heart of a corpse" means she is on the bed formed by the corpse-like body of Sadashiva <note>the auspicious one in a state of stillness</note> within the cremation ground. "Immersed in union with Mahakala" <note>Great Time/the Destroyer</note> refers to being absorbed in amorous play with the Supreme Shiva. A person who is "dull-witted" <note>of slow intellect</note>, by "meditating" or contemplating "on You," becomes "a poet," meaning one who is skilled in the art of composing poetry. || 7 ||

### Esoteric Explanation of the Nature || 7 ||

O Mother, the Creatress of all things moving and unmoving! Regarding "the hips shining with a girdle made from the arms of the dead" <gloss>2</gloss>: At the end of a cosmic cycle <term>kalpa</term>, all living beings leave their physical bodies and, carrying their respective karmas, take refuge in their subtle bodies. They reside within the "causal body" of the Goddess—who is the personification of the conditioned Absolute <term>saguṇa-brahman</term>—in the form of primal ignorance <term>avidyā</term>, staying there until the beginning of the next cycle or until they reach liberation. Therefore, the "multitude of arms," which are the primary instruments for performing action <term>karma</term>, are envisioned as a "girdle" around the hips/womb area of the Great Goddess (the Universal Form), signifying the place where the seeds of future creation are held. "Clothed by the directions" <note>naked</note>: Here, "garment" refers to the veil of illusion <term>māyā</term>. Being without it means she transcends all illusion. "Creatress of the three worlds" means that at the start of a new creation, she ordains the rebirths and experiences of all beings according to their own accumulated past actions. "Three-eyed" means she is the knower of the three periods of time <note>past, present, and future</note>. "In the cremation ground" <gloss>3</gloss>: During the Great Dissolution <term>mahāpralaya</term>, all the elements and beings lie down in the form of corpses...

---

1. The Goddess Kalika beholds the entire universe through her eyes of the Moon, Sun, and Fire; for this reason, the three eyes are envisioned. — *Mahanirvana Tantra*.

2. A creature is born through karma and is dissolved through karma alone. When the body is destroyed, that karma is regained in a new body. — *Mahanirvana Tantra*.

And in the Holy *Devi Gita*:

> In Her, the karmas of beings, the beings themselves, and Time become indistinguishably merged during the transition, just as they do in the state of deep sleep.
> Having created the entire world, I enter within it, along with illusion, karma, and the vital breaths.

3. When the Great Dissolution arrives, even the great elements lie here as corpses; the place where they lie is therefore called the "cremation ground" <note>original: śmaśāna</note>.

<summary>This page translates Verse 7 of the hymn, describing Kali's iconographic form—including her girdle of arms and her presence in the cremation ground—and provides a philosophical interpretation where these features symbolize the storage of karma and the state of the universe during cosmic dissolution.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the esoteric explanation of Verse 7, transitioning from the physical imagery of the cremation ground to its symbolic representation as the "Great Space" of consciousness. It then introduces Verse 8, which focuses on the Goddess as the eternally youthful consort of Shiva amidst the dissolution of the universe.</meta>

...[things] dissolve; that place is the Great Space <note>original: mahākāśe</note>. On the bed of the heart of a corpse <gloss>1</gloss>; here, the "corpse" refers to the Quality-less Brahman <term>Nirguna Brahman: the ultimate reality without attributes or form</term>. "On the bed" refers to the support; meaning, She is situated upon the support that is the form of the Quality-less Brahman—Her own true station. She is engaged in union with the Great Time <note>original: mahākāla-surata</note>. Because at the end of an eon <note>original: kalpāvasāne</note> there is an absence of activities like creation, and because of Her state of inactivity, She experiences unbroken bliss in non-difference from the Supreme Shiva. One who meditates thus, making You—the embodiment of Being, Consciousness, and Bliss <note>original: sac-cid-ānanda-rūpiṇīṃ</note>—manifest through contemplation, even if they are a dull-witted person whose heart is afflicted by attachment to worldly objects, they become a "poet," meaning a person of great wisdom. || 7 ||

> Those who always meditate on You—the eternally youthful consort of Shiva—who has entered the manifest funeral pyre crowded with fearsome jackals and heaps of skulls and bones of corpses, and who is satisfied by the superior union <note>referring to the goddess in the active role in amorous play</note>; they shall never suffer defeat anywhere. || 8 ||

## Commentary

By "fearsome," it means terrifying! By "jackals" <note>original: śivābhiḥ</note>, it refers to the scavengers of the cremation ground. With "heaps of skulls and bones of corpses" refers to the skeletons and craniums of the dead. "Intensely" means extremely. "Crowded" means packed. "Entered the manifest funeral pyre" means she has taken her place; the meaning is that she stands in the midst of the fires of the cremation ground. "Satisfied by the superior union" means she is delighted by the "reverse" amorous play <term>viparita-vihara: a tantric yogic posture where the Goddess is active atop Shiva</term> with the Great Time <note>Mahakala</note>. "Eternally youthful" <gloss>2</gloss> means she is untouched by stages such as childhood or old age. "Consort of Shiva" <note>original: hara-vadhūṃ</note> refers to the Enchantress of Time. "They always meditate" means they contemplate without rest. For them, your practitioners <note>original: sādhakānāṃ</note>, "nowhere"—meaning neither in this world nor the next—shall there be "defeat" or disrespect.

## Nature Explanation || 8 ||

"Fearsome" refers to those who are terrifying and extremely difficult to overcome. "By the jackals" <note>original: śivābhiḥ</note> refers to "auspicious natures," meaning the un-compounded great elements <term>apañcīkṛta-mahābhūta: the subtle elements before they combine to form the physical world</term>. Regarding "heaps of skulls and bones of corpses"—here, the white color of the bones and skeletons indicates the Quality of Purity <note>original: sattva-guṇa</note>. Therefore, at the time of the Great Dissolution <note>original: mahāpralaye</note>, this refers to the collection of the Qualities <note>Sattva, etc.</note> of the deceased living beings. "Intensely crowded" means joined with or extremely full. In the "manifest funeral pyre" <gloss>3</gloss>, which is the pyre of self-illumination...

---

<gloss>1</gloss> The letters in the word "corpse" <note>original: śava</note> are indicators of Brahman, according to the *Preta-nirnaya*. Also in the *Gayatri Tantra* and the *Gandharva Tantra*: "The bed, O Supreme Goddess, is indeed the holy Sadashiva. Upon him, O Goddess, sits the subtle Tripura Sundari."
<gloss>2</gloss> "Though the beginning of all worlds, You are in the bloom of new youth." — *Saradatilaka*.
<gloss>3</gloss> "She who exists pervading this entire universe in the form of Consciousness [Chiti]." — *Sri Sri Chandi*.

# Page 15
### With Commentary

...in the form of the Power of Consciousness <note>original: cic-chaktī</note>. She has entered and presides over [the pyre]. She is satisfied by the superior union <gloss>1</gloss> <note>This refers to the *viparīta-rata* or "inverse union" where the Goddess takes the active role, symbolizing the dynamic Power (Shakti) acting upon the passive Absolute (Shiva)</note>. Having reached the Supreme Shiva, She delights in the works of creation, preservation, and dissolution according to Her own will. She is ever-youthful, meaning She always remains in the same unchanging state; She is indestructible. She is the Bride of Hara. He is called "Hara" <note>literally "The Remover"</note> because He removes the three-fold miseries <note>misery caused by oneself, by other beings, and by fate or nature</note> of living beings by granting the knowledge of the Truth; She is His Bride, His Power. This means She is the giver of liberation to all living beings. Those practitioners <term>sādhakas: spiritual aspirants following a specific discipline</term> who always meditate on You, who are composed of Consciousness and Bliss <note>original: cid-ānanda-mayī</note>, and visualize You every day with a steady mind—for them, there is no "defeat" in any womb. This means they do not experience rebirth; they attain the final liberation of Nirvana. || 8 ||

What can we, who are of such dull intellect, say to You, O Mother?
Neither the Creator <note>Brahma</note>, nor the Lord <note>Shiva/Rudra</note>, nor even Hari <note>Vishnu</note> knows Your supreme nature.
And yet, devotion to You makes us loquacious, O Immeasurable One;
Therefore, this must be forgiven by You, for anger toward a beast is surely not fitting. || 9 ||

->**Commentary**<-

O Immeasurable One <gloss>2</gloss>, meaning one who is without limits. O Mother, the Proformatress of the three worlds. The Creator (Brahma) does not know Your supreme, true essence; neither does the Lord (Rudra) know it, nor does Hari (Vishnu) know it. What can we—who are exceedingly dull-witted and of slow intelligence—say regarding You? Nevertheless, even in our ignorance, devotion to You—the inner impulse to offer praise—makes us loquacious; it prompts us to speak words of prayer. For that reason, this unworthy act of ours <note>attempting to describe the indescribable</note> should be forgiven or tolerated.

---

<gloss>1</gloss> She is the "Left-handed" <note>original: vāmā</note> who, having conquered the "Right," grants Great Liberation. (From the *Nirvana Tantra*).
Likewise, in the *Gandharva Tantra*:
"Having placed the Male Principle below, She who embodies the half-sound of 'Ha' <note>the 'h' sound in the seed-syllable 'Hrim'</note> delights in the inverse union, embodying the Fire, Moon, and Sun."
And in the *Niruttara Tantra*: "When Kali is engaged in the inverse union, She is both without attributes <term>nirguṇa</term> and with attributes <term>saguṇa</term>."
And in the *Vasishtha Ramayana*: "The state of non-self-nature is the creation; the state of true self-nature is Shiva."

<gloss>2</gloss> "Ama" refers to the one without attributes; she is also the Goddess of Wisdom, Aniruddha-Sarasvati. (From the *Niruttara Tantra*).
In the *Mahakala Samhita*: "You whose form is the Power of inconceivable and immeasurable shape, the single Reality that is the ground of every individual being. You are reachable only through non-dual awareness that transcends the qualities of nature. You alone are established in the form of the Supreme Brahman <term>Parabrahman: the ultimate, formless reality</term>."

# Hymn to Camphor
<note>original: Karpūrādi-stotram</note>

Indeed, it is certain. "Anger at the animal" <note>original: pashu-roṣaḥ</note> means anger toward the ignorant. It is not appropriate; it is not fitting. || 9 ||

## Explanation of the Supreme Nature || 9 ||
<note>The OCR reads "6," but the context confirms this is the commentary for Verse 9.</note>

<margin>
1. The unique, subtle, and unchanging one who gives birth to tens of millions of universes. Who am I, Vishnu? Who is he, known as Shiva? Or the other gods? How could we be capable of praising You? — *Hymn to the Goddess* <note>Devī-sūktam</note>.
Also, in the *Glory of the Goddess* <note>Devī-māhātmye</note>: Since Vishnu, I [Shiva], and also the Lord [Brahma] were made to take on bodies by You, who then would have the power to praise You?
And in the *Union of Vishnu* <note>Viṣṇu-yāmale</note>: O Mother, no one knows Your supreme form. The inhabitants of heaven worship only Your gross forms, such as Kali and others.
Again, in the *Hymn to Great Kali* <note>Mahākālī-stotre</note>: When there was neither the Creator, nor Vishnu, nor Rudra, nor Time, nor the five elements—then You alone, the single embodiment of the causal essence, were perfected in the form of the Supreme Brahman.
</margin>

O Immeasurable One <note>original: amite</note>—meaning You are without the limitation of attributes, You are beyond all qualities. O Mother! O Producer of us—Brahma, Vishnu, and Rudra! Therefore, the Creator <term>Dhātā: Brahma, who ordains the births and lives of beings according to their past actions</term> does not know Your supreme essence. Even the Lord <term>Īśa: Rudra/Shiva, endowed with divine power</term> does not know. Even Hari <term>Vishnu, the remover of the three-fold miseries of beings</term> does not know. Since You are the mother of even them, how is it possible for them to have knowledge of the true nature of the Mother of the World? The meaning is that it is quite impossible for children to know the circumstances of their own mother’s birth and deeds. 

What can we, who are dull-witted <note>original: jaḍa-dhiyaḥ</note>, say to You? Since the Lords of the gods like Brahma do not know Your nature, how can we, who possess only a little worldly knowledge, be capable of describing Your nature? This is the sentiment. Nevertheless, **devotion to You makes us loquacious**—even though we are unworthy to describe Your qualities, devotion regarding You impels us to speak a little of those qualities. Therefore, this presumption of ours should be forgiven by You; **anger at the animal**—that is, anger toward the ignorant—is not appropriate or fitting. || 9 ||

-> If a devotee, deeply absorbed in the erotic sentiment, recites Your mantra at night, visualizing You as a youthful woman with full breasts and heavy hips; and if, while meditating on You, he is naked and his hair is disheveled—then all the hosts of supernatural powers come under his control, and he lives on this earth for a very long time as a great poet. || 10 || <-

[With Commentary.]

## Commentary

O Mother! A devotee <note>original: bhaktaḥ</note> who is devoted to the Heroic Path <term>Vīrācāra: a Tantric path for advanced practitioners who confront their fears and desires directly</term> and is Your practitioner <note>original: sādhakaḥ</note>. If at night <note>original: naktam</note>, at the midnight hour, he is everywhere attached to a young woman who possesses full breasts and hips—meaning he is engaged in ritual union with a youth who carries the signs of maturity in her breasts and hips—being full of bliss. If he is naked <note>original: vivāsaḥ</note> with his hair loose and flowing, and while meditating on You as being eternally engaged in the bliss of union with Great Time <note>original: Mahākāla</note>—visualizing this within his own heart—he chants Your mantra, the king of mantras previously mentioned. Then all the hosts of perfections <term>Siddhis: supernatural powers or attainments</term> and all worldly successes come under the control of that Heroic practitioner. Furthermore, becoming a poet, he lives on this earth for a very long time. || 10 ||

## Esoteric Explanation || 10 ||

In this section, the Yoga of Dissolution <note>original: Laya-yoga</note> is described. O Mother! O Creatress! The devotee who is a yogic practitioner devoted to the Divine Path <note>original: divyācāra-rataḥ</note>. If at night <note>original: naktam</note>—meaning in the Night of Supreme Knowledge <term>Brahma-vidyā: the "night" where the mundane world is asleep but the spiritual reality is awake</term>, having become awakened. This means being attached to the youthful form of the Coiled Power <term>Kula-kuṇḍalinī: the primal energy residing at the base of the spine</term>, who is the producer and protector of all beings residing in all directions and sub-directions. By merging the individual soul <note>original: jīvātman</note> into the Supreme Soul <note>original: paramātman</note>. Here, the reference to "possessing breasts and hips" <note>original: stana-jaghana-dhṛk</note> indicates that the Kundalini energy is the one who performs the creation and maintenance of the universes. Naked...

---

1. An initiated practitioner should perform the *Kula* worship. O Goddess, without the *Kula* conduct, the Kali mantra is not perfected. — *Niruttara Tantra*
2. For one lying naked at night, well-settled in ritual union, or for one with loose hair, all successes are attained. — *Phetkāriṇī Tantra*
And in the *Order of Kali*: A practitioner of the "Animal" path <note>original: pashu</note>, devoted to his own daily duties, should chant one hundred thousand times during the day. A "Divine" or "Heroic" practitioner should perform three hundred thousand chants at night.
And in the *Kubjikā Tantra*: Those who are devoted to the Animal-disposition and are merely animal-like in form should never touch the mantra or the rosary at night.
3. That which is night for all beings, in that the self-controlled one wakes. — *Bhagavad Gita*
4. Having attained the Womb Gesture <note>original: Yoni-mudrā</note>, one becomes the embodiment of Power <note>original: Shakti</note> oneself. With the sentiment of beautiful eroticism, one should sport within the Supreme Soul. Having become full of bliss, union with the Absolute occurs. — *Gheranda Samhita*
Similarly, in the *Goraksha Samhita*: Having raised the Power <note>original: Shakti</note> together with the individual soul to the Lotus of the Head, becoming the embodiment of Power oneself, one attains union with Supreme Shiva. One should contemplate the highest state as a play of various joys.
5. One should meditate on the Goddess Kundalini as the embodiment of one's chosen deity. She is eternally sixteen years old, with full and high breasts, dark-complexioned, subtle, the form of creation, and the essence of creation, maintenance, and dissolution. — *Tantra-kalpa-druma*

Page 18

# Hymn to the Camphor-Clad One (Karpūrādi-stotram)

One who is "without clothes" <note>original: vigata-vāsaḥ</note> is one whose "veil of illusion" <note>original: māyā-āvaraṇaḥ</note> has been removed; this signifies one who is fully awakened. "With loose hair" <note>original: galita-cikuraḥ</note> means one whose "fickleness" <note>original: cāpalya</note> or mental agitation has been destroyed or lost. Such a person possesses a steady, unwavering mind. Meditating on You—who are devoted to the bliss of "perfected union" <term>Sāmarasyā: the state of aesthetic and spiritual equilibrium where Shiva and Shakti are one</term> with the Supreme Shiva—while mentally chanting Your Great Mantra, that person becomes a Knower of Truth <note>original: tattva-jñānī</note>. The result is that they live for a very long time, equal to the lineages of the divine, the perfected masters, and the primordial humans. "All the hosts of perfections" <note>original: siddhaughāḥ</note>: the use of the plural here indicates that one gains the favor of the three specific lineages of masters: the Divine, the Perfected, and the Human. || 10 ||

->If a person, becoming well-established, always chants for years,<-
->Meditating on You while contemplating Your intense union with Great Time;<-
->Then, for that wise scholar who wanders happily upon the earth,<-
->The hosts of perfections are held in the lotus of his hand, O Spouse of the Destroyer of Cities!<- || 11 ||

### Commentary and Esoteric Explanation || 11 ||

O Spouse of the Destroyer of Cities! <note>original: Purahara-vadhū, a title for the Goddess as the wife of Shiva/Tripurāri</note> O Beautiful One of the Three Cities! Being "well-established" <gloss>1</gloss> means possessing a steady and unwavering mind. If one meditates on You in your "reversed" state <note>original: viparītām; referring to the iconographic posture where the Goddess is atop Shiva, symbolizing the dynamic power (Shakti) acting upon the consciousness (Shiva)</note>—that is, contemplating You as being intensely immersed in the bliss of union with Great Time, the Lord of Desire <note>original: Kāmeśvara-Mahākāla</note>—one sees You as being ready to project the universe outward. While contemplating the inner meaning of Your mantra for "years" (meaning a full year) and meditating on You, the practitioner performs the chanting <gloss>3</gloss> of Your mantra.

Then, for that wise scholar <note>original: viduṣaḥ</note> who lives happily upon the surface of the earth, the "hosts of perfections" <term>Siddhis: supernatural powers or spiritual accomplishments</term> come to reside in the "lotus of his hand." These include the groups of powers belonging to the Sacred Knowledge <note>original: Śrī Vidyā</note>, such as the power to realize any desire. In essence, they become submissive to his will. If the text is read as "Great Hosts of Perfections" <note>original: mahā-siddhi-nibhah</note>, it implies that the four stages of liberation—residing in the same realm as the Divine, taking the same form, achieving union, and final extinction—all come under his command. || 11 || This concludes the esoteric explanation.

---

1. One who is peaceful, pure, moderate in diet, sleeps on the ground, is devoted, self-controlled, free from the dualities of life, steady-minded, silent, and restrained in soul—such a one should chant, O Beloved. — *Kularnava Tantra*.
2. At the beginning of chanting, one should meditate on the Auspicious One; at the end of meditation, one should chant again. The practitioner who combines chanting and meditation attains success quickly. — *Kaulavali Tantra*.
3. The mental contemplation of the syllables of the mantra is called "mental chanting" <note>original: mānasa-japa</note>. Chanting aloud is the basic level; whispering is a hundred times better; but mental chanting is said to be a hundred times better than that—it is the supreme method. — *Kaulavali Tantra*.

<summary>This page concludes the symbolic analysis of the practitioner's state (nakedness as freedom from illusion) and explains Verse 11, which promises the attainment of supernatural powers (Siddhis) through a year of disciplined mental chanting and meditation on the Goddess in union with Mahakala.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Karpūrādi-stotram, moving from the benefits of long-term meditation on the Goddess to her identification with the triple functions of the universe—creation, preservation, and destruction. It concludes by distinguishing the worship of the Supreme Mother from the worship of lesser deities.</meta>

Page १८

> O Mother! You give birth to the world, and You protect it all,
> From the earth onwards; at the time of dissolution, You also withdraw it.
> Therefore, You are the Creator, the Lord of the Three Worlds, the Lord of Fortune,
> And even the Great Lord. How can I possibly praise You, who are everything? || 12 ||

---

### Commentary and Explanation of the Nature of the Goddess || 12 ||

O Mother <note>original: Janani</note>, Producer of the worlds! You give birth to <note>original: prasūte</note> the world-process <note>original: jagat-prapañcam</note>. Therefore, You are the Creator <note>original: Dhātā/Brahmā</note>; the meaning is that You are His power of creation <term>Sṛṣṭi-śakti: the energy required to project the universe into being</term>. You also protect and support the world; for this reason, You are the Lord of the Three Worlds and the Lord of Fortune <note>original: Śrīpati/Viṣṇu</note>. You are the power of the All-Pervasive One who maintains the world <term>Vaiṣṇavī-śakti: the sustaining energy of the universe</term>. At the time of dissolution—the time of the world’s destruction—You withdraw the entire world, which consists of the five great elements <note>original: pañca-mahā-bhūta</note> beginning with the earth, and merge them into Your own causal body. Thus, You are also the Great Lord <note>original: Maheśa/Rudra</note>; the sense is that You are the power of destruction <note>original: saṃhāra-śakti</note>. The meaning is that You are both the efficient cause <note>the intelligent designer</note> and the material cause <note>the substance itself</note> of the entire world, composed of both the moving and the unmoving. Therefore, how can I praise You, who are the unique cause of the world, the one without a second? How can I explain Your true nature? The sense is that You are beyond the reach of speech.

It is also said in the *Hymn to Triputa* <note>original: Tripuṭā-stotra</note>:—

> You are the earth, the Creator, the one who performs the world’s creation;
> You are the waters, You are the All-Pervasive Vishnu, the protectress of the world.
> You are the fire, You are Rudra, the one who agitates the world;
> You are the form of sovereignty, taking the forms of space and air.

And so in another hymn:—

> To bring about creation, preservation, and destruction, You take on a diverse form.
> Through the three qualities of the world, Your transformations are known as the Creator, the Unchanging, and the Wielder of the Trident <note>original: Brahmā, Achyuta (Viṣṇu), and Śūlabhṛt (Śiva)</note>. || 12 ||

---

> Many serve the hosts of gods who are lower than You;
> Those fools, O Mother, know nothing of the Supreme.
> I have surrendered myself spontaneously to the Primordial One,
> Who is worshipped by Vishnu, Shiva, and Brahma,
> And who is immersed in the great bliss of the flavor of divine union. || 13 ||

<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Karpūrādi-stotram, specifically focusing on the 13th and 14th verses. The previous page established the Goddess as the source of all creative and destructive powers. This section distinguishes her as the ultimate reality beyond the lesser gods and as the physical and spiritual essence of the universe.</meta>

# Hymn to the Camphor-Clad One (Karpūrādi-stotram)

## Commentary and Explanation of the Nature of the Goddess || 13 ||

O Mother! Many people <note>original: aneke bahavo janāḥ</note>, being foolish and lacking discernment <note>original: vicāra-śūnyāḥ</note>, serve and worship various other groups of gods besides You. Those people do not know anything of the Supreme Truth <note>original: parama-tattvaṃ</note>. You are the one who is always to be worshipped and meditated upon by the gods—the Preserver, the Destroyer, and the Creator <note>original: hari-hara-viriñca-ādi-vibudhaiḥ; referring to Vishnu, Shiva, and Brahma</note>. You are the Primal One <note>original: ādyāṃ</note>, the original cause of the universe. You are absorbed in the great bliss arising from the flavor of love <note>original: rati-rasa-mahānanda-niratām</note>; this refers to the great bliss produced by the union with the Great Lord of Time <note>original: mahākālena saha</note> in the "inverse" position <note>original: viparīta-ramaṇaṃ; a specific tantric yogic posture</note>, as well as the enjoyment of the sacred wine <note>original: rasam madyapānaṃ</note>. I have completely surrendered to You <note>original: prapanno’smi</note>, who are the embodiment of consciousness and bliss <term>Cidānanda-rūpiṇīm: a form composed of the light of awareness and pure joy</term>. || 13 ||

->Earth, water, fire, and even the air and the sky—<-
->You alone, O Kali, the auspicious consort of the Lord of the Mountains, are everything.<-
->O Mother, what praise can be offered to You? Through Your own mercy,<-
->may You be pleased with me, who am helpless; let there be no more birth for me in this world. || 14 ||<-

---

> 1. In the *Kūrma Purāṇa*, the Divine Tortoise said:
> That which is known through all the Vedantas, determined by the knowers of the Absolute <note>original: brahma-vādibhiḥ</note> to be the One, all-pervading, subtle, unchanging, immovable, and eternal. It is the infinite, imperishable Absolute <note>original: brahma</note>, alone, without parts, and supreme. The great divine Yogis behold that supreme state. It is the truth higher than the highest, eternal, auspicious, and infallible. It is infinite, and though merged in Nature <note>original: prakṛtau līnaṃ</note>, it is the supreme state of the Goddess. It is white, stainless, pure, devoid of qualities <note>original: nirguṇaṃ</note>, and free from duality. It is the object of self-realization; that is the supreme state of the Goddess.
> Likewise, the words of the Goddess herself:
> "My formless nature <note>original: niṣkalaṃ rūpaṃ</note>, which is pure consciousness, alone and supreme, is free from all limiting adjuncts, infinite, and the state of immortality. That supreme state is obtained through knowledge alone, without hardship. Those who see through knowledge enter into Me."
> So it is also said in the *Yoginī Tantra*:
> "Her form is supreme bliss, the great cause of the worlds. That form of the Goddess is non-dual and free from all distinctions."
> 2. This knowledge <note>original: vidyā</note> is considered the Great Mantra-Soul Knowledge of Kalika. By attaining this, even the lowest of men reaches the liberation of Nirvana.
> So it is said in the *Argalā Stotra*:
> "O Supreme Goddess with four arms, praised by the Four-Faced One (Brahma)... O Goddess praised by Krishna with constant devotion, O Mother... O Supreme Goddess praised by the Lord of the Daughter of the Himalayas (Shiva)..."
> 3. Having drunk the essence of the lineage <note>original: kula-rasa; often referring to consecrated wine in a ritual context</note>, the Hero <term>Vīra: an advanced practitioner who has overcome worldly passions</term> should take refuge in meditation on the Absolute. O Queen of the Gods, meditation on the Absolute is the cause of liberation into the Absolute <note>original: brahma-nirvāṇa</note>.

<meta>catchword: ॥ Yoginī Tantra ॥</meta>

<summary>This page explains that the Goddess Kali is the supreme reality above all other deities and is identical with the five elements. The commentary and footnotes emphasize that she is both the formless Absolute (Brahman) and the personal Mother who grants liberation through knowledge and devotion.</summary>
<keywords>Kali, Brahman, Mahakala, Five Elements, Liberation, Kurma Purana, Yogini Tantra, Argala Stotra, Vira, Consciousness, Bliss</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Karpūrādi-stotram, specifically finishing the explanation of the 14th verse and beginning the 15th. The previous page ended with the start of a prayer acknowledging the Goddess as the physical elements of the universe (earth, water, etc.). This section concludes that prayer by asking for liberation from the cycle of rebirth and introduces a specific tantric ritual involving meditation in a cremation ground.</meta>

With Commentary.

## Commentary and Explanation of the Essence || 14 ||

O Kali! O remover of the fear of time and death! <note>original: kāla-bhaya-nivāriṇi</note> You are the Earth <gloss>1</gloss>, the Water <note>original: kīlālaṃ</note>, the pure Fire, the Air, and the Sky. You are the One, the non-dual, the Auspicious One <note>original: kalyāṇī</note> who provides for the welfare of all living beings by granting them the gift of Nirvana and liberation. You are the delight of the Lord of the Mountains <note>original: giriśa-ramaṇī</note>, the consort of Shiva. He who rests on the mountain peak <note>original: giri-kūṭe</note> is called Girisha, the immutable, supreme Brahman; You are His delight-giving power <note>original: hlādinī-śakti</note>. Although You are beyond all change <gloss>2</gloss>, You are extremely skilled in making the impossible happen through Your power of illusion <term>Māyā: the creative power that makes the singular Divine appear as the diverse world</term>. This means You take the form of the twenty-four cosmic principles, beginning with the Earth. All things, moving and unmoving, are Your form; as the scripture says, "All this is truly Brahman," and the *Chandi* <note>The Devi Mahatmya</note> states, "You are the soul of everything, the ruler of all, endowed with all power." O Mother, what praise can be offered to You? The sense is that it is impossible to properly praise You, as You are the very essence of everything. Through Your own mercy—Your extraordinary and natural compassion—be pleased with me, who am helpless <note>original: agatikam</note> and without any means of spiritual practice. In this world <note>original: bhavam anu</note>, may my birth not occur again; the meaning is: let me be merged into Your own state of fearlessness. || 14 ||

->**He who stays in the cremation ground, composed, with disheveled hair and naked,**<-
->**offering a thousand flowers of the Sun-plant, each moistened with his own seed,**<-
->**while reciting each syllable of Your mantra and remaining absorbed in meditation on You—**<-
->**O Mahakali! He effortlessly becomes a master of the earth. || 15 ||**<-

### Commentary.

O Mahakali! O power of Great Time! Furthermore, the person who stays in the cremation ground <gloss>3</gloss>—meaning one who is established in the land of the dead—who is composed and steady of mind, with hair unbound and wearing the directions as his garment <note>original: digambara; meaning naked, symbolizing a return to a natural, unconditioned state</note>, and who is absorbed in meditation on You, reflecting on Your gross form as "having a body like a dark cloud, without clothing," and so forth—

---

<gloss>1</gloss> You are the earth, the sky, the flood of water, the fire, and the form of the world's air. You are the space, the mind, the primordial nature, the principle of cosmic intelligence, and the ego. You are the Self, O Mother; indeed, You are the Supreme, and there is nothing higher than You. Forgive my offenses, O You with the bared teeth, the one who takes any form at will, the Terrific One. — *Guptavatī-tantra*
<gloss>2</gloss> The One, the Unique, whose form is subtle and unchanging, brings forth millions upon millions of universes. — *Devī-sūkta*
<gloss>3</gloss> Let him stay in the cremation ground, composed, with disheveled hair. Naked, he should offer a thousand Sun-flowers <note>original: sūrya-puṣpam; the Arka flower</note>. Moisten them with his own seed, and recite the praise while repeating each syllable of the mantra. Having meditated upon and worshipped the Great Power, let him recite the hymn as spoken by the Goddess to the Potter.
<margin>Mahākāla-saṃhitā</margin>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Karpūrādi-stotram. The previous page discussed the Goddess as the elemental essence of the universe (Earth, Water, Fire, etc.) and her role as the power of Brahman. # The Camphor-Hymn

Reciting Your <note>original: manu</note> mantra-queen, and offering a thousand <term>Arka flowers: blossoms of the Calotropis gigantea, often called the "sun-flower" in Tantric texts</term>, known popularly as Akanda, which have been smeared with his own fallen semen <note>The text here describes a "Vamachara" or Left-Hand Path ritual practice</note>—one who gives these to You, one by one, with You as his aim, that practitioner easily becomes a king ruling over the earth. || 15 ||

## Explanation of the Essence || 15 ||

But, on the other hand, for those on the "Divine Path" <note>original: divya; the path of the spiritually advanced practitioner</note>: O Mahakali! O You whose nature is the Supreme Brahman! The "cremation ground" is the place where all beings dissolve into the state of corpses at the time of the Great Dissolution <note>original: mahāpralaya</note>; that cremation ground is the Supreme Brahman itself. The person who stays there is one whose heart is attached to that—meaning one who is devoted to Brahman. Such a person is "well-situated" (steady-minded) and "clothed by the directions" <note>original: dikpatadhara; "sky-clad" or naked, symbolizing the removal of the veil of illusion</note>, having a mind free from the distortions of worldly change. He is "intent on Your meditation," meaning he contemplates Your nature as Being, Consciousness, and Bliss. As it is said in the *Rudrayamala Tantra*:

> A man who takes refuge in the "Kula" path <note>The Tantric tradition of the Kaulas</note> should recite the mantra while seeking shelter in the One whose form is Consciousness, who is the abode of Knowledge, whose body is the Bliss of Awareness, who shines with the light of ten million lightning bolts, and whose essence is the truth of all principles.

Reciting Your mantra-queen and mentally uttering Your supreme mantra, he makes an offering with his "own fallen semen" <gloss>1</gloss>, which refers to the nectar that naturally flows from the Thousand-Petaled Lotus <note>original: sahasrāra-kamala; the crown chakra at the top of the head</note>. He offers a thousand Arka flowers <gloss>2</gloss> located in the aperture of Brahman <note>original: brahmarandhra</note>, which are actually the mental states—such as compassion, knowledge, and forgiveness—arising from the movements of the mind born from the Sun-Consciousness. He gives these to You one by one through the method of mental worship, offering them into the Fire of Consciousness. Such a divine practitioner freely wanders the earth as a master...

---

<note>
1. "One should offer the heart-lotus as the seat; one should offer the nectar dripping from the Thousand-Petaled Lotus as the water for washing the feet. One should offer the mind as the ritual oblation. One should imagine the consciousness to be the flower, and the life-breaths to be the incense." — *Mahanirvana Tantra*. 
"One should satisfy the supreme Savior Goddess with the nectar from the moon-center in the aperture of Brahman. One should offer a beautiful oblation from the sun-vessel located there. One should give the flower of compassion, the flower of knowledge, the flower of forgiveness, the flower of sensory restraint, the flower of charity, the flower of merit, and the supreme flower of non-violence. The flower of bliss must be given, and the flower of the practitioner's own effort. He who offers these ten flowers reaches the state of the Savior Goddess." 
-> *The Secret of Tara* <-
</note>

<note>
2. "The mind dwells in the Sun, and life dwells in the Moon, O Beloved." — *The Tantra of the Collection of Knowledge*. 
Also in the *Yajnavalkya Samhita*: "The Moon is known to be in the Ida channel, and the Sun is remembered to be in the Pingala channel." 
And it is said in the *Pranagnihotra*: "There, the fire named 'Sun' has the form of the solar orb; it stands in the head as a unique Sage, surrounded by a thousand rays."
</note>

<summary>This page explains verse 15 of the hymn, contrasting a literal ritual of offering Arka flowers and semen for worldly kingship with an esoteric yogic interpretation. In the "Divine Path," the cremation ground represents the Supreme Brahman, and the offerings are redefined as the internal nectar of the crown chakra and mental virtues like compassion.</summary>
<keywords>Mahakali, Arka flowers, Sahasrara, Brahman, cremation ground, Tantra, yoga, mental worship, nectar of immortality, Kaula path</keywords>

--- Page 119 ---
<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Karpūrādi-stotram. The previous page discussed the ritual offering of Arka flowers and semen to the Goddess to attain worldly kingship. This page concludes the commentary on Verse 15 by explaining the spiritual "innate state" and introduces Verse 16, which details further ritual offerings involving hair and bodily fluids, interpreting them through both a literal "Heroic" lens and a symbolic "Divine" lens.</meta>

...The lord of the earth and the world becomes the master of the activities of the senses and the like, and thus attains the **innate state** <gloss>1</gloss>. This is the underlying intention. || 15 ||

-> **In the house, with a broom, the semen that has oozed forth, and the hair** <-
-> **He offers with its roots in the cremation ground on a Tuesday at the middle of the body.** <-
-> **By reciting Your mantra even once with love, O Kali, constantly** <-
-> **He travels as a lord of the earth, mounted on an elephant, a most excellent poet. || 16 ||** <-

### Commentary

O Kali! O Destroyer of Time! This refers to the person who is a "Heroic Practitioner" <note>original: vīrasādhaka; a practitioner of the Tantric path who uses physical substances in ritual</note>. "In the house, with a broom, the semen that has oozed forth"—here, "house" refers to the "temple of passion" <note>the sexual organs</note>, and "broom" refers to the phallus. It signifies the semen that has emerged from the pleasure of union with his <term>Shakti: the female ritual partner representing the Goddess</term>. Likewise, "the hair" refers to hair pulled out along with its roots. "On a Tuesday" means on the day of Mars. "In the middle of the body" <gloss>2</gloss> means either during the middle of the day or at midnight. "With love," meaning with devotion, "reciting Your <note>original: manu</note> mantra," he "offers" or presents these to You "in the cremation ground" even "once" <gloss>3</gloss> or a single time. Truly, that person becomes a "most excellent poet" and a "lord of the earth" (a king), and "travels" or moves everywhere "mounted on an elephant" at all times. This is the meaning. || 16 ||

### Explanation of the Essence || 16 ||

O Kali! O You who removes the fear of Time! This refers to the practitioner who is devoted to the "Divine Path" <note>original: divyācāra; the path of internal meditation rather than external ritual</note>. "In the house" refers to the "Abode of Brahman" in the form of the thousand-petaled lotus <note>the Sahasrara chakra at the crown of the head</note>. "With the broom, the semen that has oozed forth" refers to the nectar produced by the union with the Supreme Shiva—

---

1. <note>original quote from Mahopanishad and Kularnava</note> "For the practitioner whose awareness of power has arisen and who has abandoned all worldly actions, the **innate state** <note>original: sahajavastha</note> arises of its own accord. This occurs because the Sun of Consciousness shines upon the form of Kali." — *Mahopanishad*.
"The innate state is the highest; meditation and concentration are the middle; recitation and hymns are the lowest; and external sacrifice and worship are the lowest of the low." — *Kularnava Tantra*.

2. <note>original quote from Kakarakuta-rahasya</note> "Nails, hair, and one's own semen—whatever is gathered by the 'broom'—with hair disheveled and 'clothed by the directions' <note>naked</note>, and preceding the act with the root-mantra, if one performs a fire-sacrifice at midnight on a Tuesday in the cremation ground and recites the Thousand Names, he shall attract the lords of the earth."
-> The Secret of the 'K' Syllable Group <-

3. <note>original quote from Gandharva-malika</note> "O Queen of the Gods, when one takes pleasure with the Eternal Shiva for even a moment, O Goddess, nectar is produced at that very instant. The nectar arising from that, which resembles the color of red lac-juice, is what the practitioner should use to satisfy the Supreme Deity." — *Gandharva-malika*.

<summary>This page explains Verse 16 of the hymn, detailing ritual offerings of hair and semen in a cremation ground to gain poetic and political power. It provides two interpretations: a literal ritual for "Heroic" practitioners and a symbolic yogic process (the union of Shiva and Shakti in the crown) for "Divine" practitioners.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on Verse 16 of the Karpūrādi-stotram, moving from the literal "Heroic" (vīra) ritual involving physical offerings to the "Divine" (divya) or yogic interpretation. It then introduces Verse 17, which focuses on the visualization of the "temple of the flower-bowed one" (the yoni) and the attainment of poetic mastery and ultimate liberation.</meta>

...es a master of enjoyment and liberation. <note>This completes the sentence from the previous page: "Truly, that person becomes..."</note>

<gloss>In the spiritual interpretation:</gloss> The "nectar of vitality" <note>original: vīryāmṛta; literally semen, but here refers to the nectar of the moon</note> is that which arises from the union with the **Coiled Serpent Power** <term>Kula-kundalinī: the dormant spiritual energy at the base of the spine</term>. The "hair" along with its "roots" refers to the **mind** <note>original: manas</note> along with its fluctuations such as restlessness. "On a Tuesday" refers to the day of Mars. "In the middle of the body," whether at midday, midnight, or during the day or night. Reciting Your mantra <note>original: manu</note> even "once" with love and supreme devotion. He "offers" or dissolves this mind into You, who are of the form of **Pure Consciousness** <note>original: cidrūpā</note> in the "cremation ground" <note>symbolizing the heart or the state where all dualities are burnt</note>. This means he achieves **Seedless Samadhi** <gloss>1</gloss>. The result is that he attains both worldly enjoyment and final liberation. || 16 ||

-> **Oh! If Your devotee, visualizing before him the temple of the god of the flower-bow** <-
-> **strewn with its own flowers, repeats Your mantra,** <-
-> **he becomes the lord of the hosts of celestial musicians; a veritable ocean to the rivers** <-
-> **of the nectar of poetry, he eventually dissolves into the Supreme Abode. || 17 ||** <-

# Commentary

O Mother! If a devotee, a "Heroic practitioner" <note>original: vīrasādhaka</note>, visualizing before him—thinking of repeatedly—the "temple of the flower-bowed one" <note>meaning the god of love, Kama</note>, which is the **womb** <note>original: yoni</note>, strewn with "its own flowers" <gloss>2</gloss>—meaning pervaded by **self-born flowers** <note>original: svayambhū-kusuma; a technical term for menstrual fluid in this ritual context</note>. If he recites Your mantra ten thousand times, then—oh, the wonder of it!—he becomes a "lord of the hosts of celestial musicians" <note>original: gandharva</note>, meaning the best of singers. Furthermore, he becomes an "ocean to the rivers of the nectar of poetry," meaning the ocean of the rivers of poetic nectar, the foundation of endless poetic skill. At the "end," meaning at the conclusion of his physical life, he "dissolves into the Supreme Abode," meaning he becomes absorbed into Your most excellent state and becomes capable of the works of creation and so on. This is the inner meaning. || 17 ||

# Interpretation of the Inner Form || 17 ||

In this verse, the practice of the **Womb Gesture** <term>Yoni-mudrā: a ritual or yogic hand posture/internal focus representing the source of creation</term> is described. As stated in the **Shakta Scriptures** <note>original: Śāktāgama</note>:
> He who knows not the meaning of the mantra, the consciousness of the mantra, and the Womb Gesture—his knowledge will not succeed even with tens of millions of recitations.

O Mother! If a devotee, a yogic practitioner intent on "Divine Conduct" <note>original: divyācāra</note>, visualizing the "temple of the flower-bowed one" strewn with "its own flowers"—the self-born—

---

1. "Seedless Samadhi" results from the cessation of all mental fluctuations. || *Yoga Sutras of Patanjali* ||
2. In the *Matrikabheda Tantra*: "The blood produced during the first menses of a married girl, O Great Goddess, is called the 'Self-born Flower' (svapuṣpa), which charms all. He who recites the mantra ten thousand times while meditating on the woman's womb strewn with menstrual fluid, truly becomes a lord of poets and charms the world with his voice." || *Tantrakalpadruma* ||

<summary>This page explains the yogic transition of Verse 16 from physical ritual to mental dissolution (Samadhi) and introduces Verse 17's focus on the Yoni-mudra. It describes how the practitioner attains poetic genius and spiritual liberation through the visualization of the Goddess's creative source.</summary>
<keywords>Kundalini, Seedless Samadhi, Yoni-mudra, Poetic Mastery, Menstrual Symbolism, Matrikabheda Tantra, Patanjali, Divine Conduct, Heroic Path</keywords>

--- Page 121 ---
<meta>This page continues the detailed commentary on Verse 17 of the Karpūrādi-stotram. The previous page provided the verse translation, describing the devotee who visualizes the "temple of the flower-bow" and chants the Goddess's mantra, thereby achieving poetic genius and ultimate liberation. This page interprets the symbolic "temple" as the triangular energy centers in the body.</meta>

### Commentary

The phrase "seated on the linga-shaped flower" refers to the "temple of the flower-bowed one" <note>the 'flower-bowed one' is Kamadeva, the god of love and desire</note>. This temple is the triangular mandala located either in the **root center** <term>Mūlādhāra: the energy center at the base of the spine</term> or in the **heart lotus** <term>Hṛd-padma: the heart energy center</term>.

It is said in the *Shiva Samhita*:
> In the pericarp of that root lotus, there is a beautiful,
> triangular **womb** <note>original: yoni; representing the source of creation and divine energy</note>,
> which is kept secret in all the Tantras.

Again, in the same work, in the fourth chapter:
> One should meditate on Desire <note>original: kāma</note> located in the **Womb of Brahma** <term>Brahma-yoni: a name for the triangular energy center</term>,
> resembling the *Bandhūka* flower,
> as bright as ten million suns and as cool as ten million moons.
> Above it is a subtle flame, the supreme part of **Pure Consciousness** <note>original: cidrūpā</note>.
> One should imagine the Self hidden by that flame, becoming one with it.

Likewise, in the *Tara-rahasya*:
> One should meditate in one's own heart on the excellent triangular mandala,
> accompanied by the vital fluids <note>original: rajobhiḥ; literally 'menses' or 'pollen,' symbolizing creative energy</note>,
> a triangle containing all colors.

"Visualizing before oneself" <note>original: puro nijāgre</note> means imagining and contemplating Your form, composed of the meaning of the mantra, within that triangle. It is said in the *Bhuta-shuddhi-tantra*:
> For whichever deity a mantra is prescribed,
> one should perform the mental repetition while contemplating
> the form of that specific deity.

And in the *Kularnava*:
> Devoted to That, with life-breath fixed on That, with heart set on That,
> and intent on That—one should chant the mantra while
> contemplating the meaning of its words, O Beloved.

"Your great mantra" <note>original: manuṃ/mantrarājaṃ</note> refers to your supreme mantra, which he chants "slowly, slowly" <note>original: śanaiḥ śanaiḥ</note>—meaning he chants it mentally. As it is said in that same text: "Slowly, slowly, clearly, neither too fast nor too slow." Such a person becomes the "lord of the hosts of celestial musicians" <note>original: gandharva-śreṇī-patiḥ</note>, meaning a supreme singer. This is because of the teaching: "There is nothing higher than song." Alternatively, he becomes like Chitraratha, the king of the **celestial musicians** <term>Gandharvas: divine beings associated with music and the arts</term>. Furthermore, he becomes a "mighty ocean for the rivers of the nectar of poetry," a great poet like **Kalidasa** <note>the most famous classical Sanskrit poet and dramatist</note>. Moreover, "eventually" <note>original: paryante</note> means at the end of the body’s life. He "attains the Supreme Abode" <gloss>1</gloss>, meaning that by becoming dissolved into You, who are of the form of **Truth, Consciousness, and Bliss** <term>Saccidānanda: the ultimate nature of reality</term>, he attains the state of **Nirvana** <term>Nirvāṇa: final liberation from the cycle of rebirth</term>. Regarding the fruit of practicing this **womb-gesture** <term>Yoni-mudrā: a specific yogic posture or internal seal</term>, the *Shiva Samhita* states:
> From practice comes success, and from success, one attains liberation. || 17 ||

---

1. In the *Devi Gita*, the Goddess says: "Then, his dissolution occurs into My form, which is Pure Consciousness alone." See also the commentary on the 13th verse.

It emphasizes that mental chanting and visualization lead to poetic genius, mastery of the arts, and ultimate union with the Goddess's supreme consciousness.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Karpūrādi-stotram, moving from the visualization of the internal "temple of desire" in Verse 17 to the specific meditation on the Goddess’s yantra (sacred geometry) and her position upon the body of Shiva in Verse 18. This section focuses on the practitioner becoming one with the Divine through late-night meditation.</meta>

# The Hymn of the Ingredients of Camphor

### Verse 18

Whoever meditates on You at night, O Mother—Your face smiling, seated upon the heart of Shiva-as-corpse on a fifteen-angled pedestal, steeped in the beauty of the essence of desire in the company of the Great Lord of Time—that person, though human, becomes the Destroyer of Desire <note>original: Smarahara; an epithet of the god Shiva, who burned the god of desire to ashes</note> himself.

->**Commentary**<-

**O Mother!** O Parent! **That person** refers to the practitioner <term>Sādhaka: a person following a spiritual discipline</term>. **At night** <note>original: naktam</note> signifies being **deeply devoted**, with a mind solely attached to You, while **himself absorbed in the joy of union** <note>original: ratānanda-nirataḥ; here referring to the bliss of spiritual or ritual connection</note>. **On a fifteen-angled pedestal** <gloss>1</gloss> refers to the sacred geometric diagram (yantra) composed of fifteen angles. **On the heart of Shiva-as-corpse** means upon the chest of Sadashiva, who takes the form of a lifeless body <note>This symbolizes that consciousness without its active power, the Goddess, is inert</note>. **Smiling-faced**... **With the Great Lord of Time** <note>original: Mahākāla; the aspect of Shiva representing eternal time</note>... **Steeped in the beauty of the essence of desire** refers to being intensely focused on the sweetness of the divine "reverse" union <note>original: viparita-rati; a Tantric concept where the Goddess is the active principle upon the passive Shiva</note>. If a practitioner **meditates** on You in this way, **that person becomes the Destroyer of Desire**—meaning they become Shiva himself. This is the intended meaning.

->**Essential Interpretation of the Form**<-

**O Mother!** Our Creatress! **That person at night**—meaning at midnight. It is said:
> At the great midnight or during the hour of Brahma <term>Brāhme-muhūrta: the auspicious period before sunrise</term>,
> if one is intent on meditation,
> they will certainly behold that form of Supreme Bliss.

**Deeply devoted** means having a one-pointed mind on You. **Himself absorbed in the joy of union** <gloss>2</gloss> means that by adopting the **Womb Gesture** <term>Yoni-mudrā: a specific hand position or internal state used to seal the senses and connect with the source of creation</term>, the practitioner becomes the embodiment of Divine Power <note>original: śakti-maya</note> and experiences the bliss of dissolution into the Supreme Soul.

**On a fifteen-angled pedestal** <gloss>3</gloss>: This describes the sacred diagram surrounded by the circle of **Ignorance** <note>original: avidyā</note>, possessing eight petals representing the **eight forms of Nature** <note>original: aṣṭa-prakṛti; including the five elements, mind, intellect, and ego</note>, characterized by a triangle representing the **five sensory organs, five motor organs, and five vital breaths**, and adorned with the "seed-point" <term>Bindu: the central dot of a yantra</term> which is the reflection of Pure Consciousness within **Illusion** <term>Māyā: the power that creates the appearance of the physical world</term>...

---

1. "First draw a triangle, then another triangle. Then the wise practitioner should draw three more excellent triangles... In the center, place the central point (Bindu) adorned with the seed-syllable of Illusion. Outside the six-angled star, draw a circle, and then eight petals... Surrounded by an outer circle and a square enclosure (Bhūpura). By knowing this King of Yantras, one attains liberation without a doubt." — *Kālī-tantra*
2. "By assuming the Womb Gesture (Yoni-mudrā), one becomes the embodiment of Divine Power. With the juice of beautiful love, one sports within the Supreme Soul. Becoming full of bliss, oneness with the Absolute (Brahman) occurs." — *Gheraṇḍa Saṃhitā*
3. "Likewise, the diagram (Chakra) composed of mantras is the supreme body of the Deity. The Yantra is the union of Shiva and Shakti; it is the form of the Absolute (Brahman). Yet, the Yantra known as one's own body is considered superior to all other Yantras." — *Gandharva-tantra*

<summary>This page describes Verse 18, which focuses on meditating on the Goddess seated upon the "corpse" of Shiva within a complex fifteen-angled yantra. The commentary explains how the practitioner, through specific rituals and midnight meditation, transcends human desire to achieve oneness with Shiva.</summary>

<keywords>Yantra, Mahakala, Sadashiva, Yonimudra, meditation, midnight, five elements, Kali Tantra, Gheranda Samhita, Gandharva Tantra, non-duality</keywords>

--- Page 123 ---
<meta>This page continues the commentary on Verse 18 of the Karpūrādi-stotram, specifically detailing the internal visualization of the Goddess within the practitioner's body and her manifestation from the Absolute. It then introduces Verse 19, which lists the various ritual offerings (bali) used in her worship to achieve spiritual powers.</meta>

**Composed of the elements** <gloss>1</gloss> [this means] within the "city of the earth" <note>the square outer part of a yantra</note> in the instrument of one's own body. **In the heart of Shiva-as-corpse** means in the heart of Shiva who is inactive like a corpse; while She is established in Her own state of the Absolute without qualities <term>Nirguṇa-Brahman: the Ultimate Reality without attributes</term>, She becomes two through the division of Shiva and Shakti <note>the male and female principles</note>, like a chickpea split into two halves, through Illusion <term>Māyā</term> which possesses the powers of Will, Action, and Knowledge. In my tradition—**intently devoted to the beauty of the essence of great passion** <gloss>2</gloss> [means] constantly joined in the "reverse" position <note>viparīta-bhāva; a Tantric concept where the Goddess is the active principle upon the passive Shiva</note> with the Supreme Shiva as the Absolute with qualities <term>Saguṇa-Brahman: the Divine with attributes</term>.

**Whoever meditates** <gloss>3</gloss> on **You**—the smiling-faced one, full of eternal bliss, the embodiment of the Absolute—should contemplate You as non-different from their own soul, because You reside in the instrument of their body as the reflection of Consciousness.

It is also said in the *Glory of the Goddess* <note>original: Devi Mahatmya</note>:

> That Goddess who resides in all beings in the form of a reflection...

And in the *Gandharva Tantra*:

> By contemplating one's own soul—which is unstained, without qualities, pure, and composed of the Goddess Tripura—as non-different from the self, a person becomes one with That.

**He becomes the Destroyer of Desire** <note>original: Smarahara; an epithet of Shiva</note>; the practitioner, endowed with the sense of non-duality <term>Advaita: the philosophy of oneness</term>, having merged into You—the Supreme Soul—attains the state of absolute liberation <term>Kaivalya: the final stage of spiritual freedom</term>. || 18 ||

->O Dark One! If mortals, during Your worship,<-
->Offer flesh along with hair and bone—be it from a cat,<-
->A camel, a sheep, a man, a buffalo, or even a goat—<-
->To those good men, every kind of supernatural power,<-
->Unprecedented at every step, comes into being. || 19 ||<-

## Commentary

**O Dark One!** O Kalika! In **Your worship**—the occasional ritual offering—**cat** <gloss>4</gloss> means relating to a cat; **camel** means relating to a camel; **sheep** means relating to a sheep; **of man and buffalo** means man and buffalo...

---

1. "Earth, water, fire, air, ether, mind, intellect, and ego—this is My nature divided eightfold." — From the *Bhagavad Gita*. 
Also in the *Gandharva Tantra*: "The subtle body, the means of experience, is composed of the five vital breaths, the mind, the intellect, the senses, and the non-individualized elements. Know the Soul to be different from these three coverings (bodies), which are caused by beginningless, indescribable ignorance."

2. "When that Supreme Power, who takes the form of the universe by Her own will, placed the Male Principle beneath Her, She became possessed by the desire for union. Then the Goddess herself, having mounted Bhairava [Shiva], increases Her own bliss through the collection of natural joys."

3. "Alternatively, one should remember the Self as composed of Her through the feeling of non-duality. Endowed with the sense of non-duality and with a steady body, one should worship the Goddess within one's own Self with the proper rituals and offerings." — From the *Gandharva Tantra*.

4. "Whoever offers a sacrifice <note>original: bali</note> of the meat, bone, hair, and skin of a cat, a sheep, a camel, or a buffalo—for him, the Goddess Dakshina [Kali] becomes a benefactor, just like a mother." — From the *Shyama Rahasya*.

<summary>This page explains the internal meditation on Kali as a manifestation of the Absolute split into Shiva and Shakti, emphasizing non-dualism. It also introduces Verse 19 regarding ritual animal offerings used to obtain spiritual success (siddhi).</summary>
<keywords>Nirguna Brahman, Saguna Brahman, Shiva-Shakti, chickpea metaphor, non-duality, Advaita, Kaivalya, animal sacrifice, bali, Kalika, Gandharva Tantra, Devi Mahatmya</keywords>

--- Page 124 ---
<meta>This page continues the commentary on the 18th verse of the Hymn to the Camphor-clad One (Karpūrādi-stotram), shifting from the literal ritual requirements of animal offerings to a deeper, symbolic "internal" interpretation where animals represent human vices. It then transitions into Verse 20, describing the ascetic and ritual discipline required of a practitioner.</meta>

# Hymn to the Camphor-clad One

<meta>catchword: The Wish-fulfilling Tree of Tantra (Tantra-kalpadruma)</meta>

**Connected with** or **either a goat** <note>original: "chāga"</note>, **connected to a goat, including hair and bones**, meaning including the hair and the skeletal structure—**great** [or] excellent **flesh** <term>palalam: specifically refers to meat used in a ritual context</term> [as] a **ritual offering** <term>bali: a worshipful gift or sacrifice</term>. For practitioners who are **bestowing** [these] **at will** [or] freely, at **every step**, meaning with every footfall, **unprecedented** [and] wondrous **all** [and] every kind of **attainment** <term>siddhi: spiritual success or the perfection of a ritual</term> [and] the successful fruit of the work **arises** [or] is produced in abundance. || 18 ||

## Esoteric Interpretation || 18 ||

**O Unfettered One** <gloss>1</gloss> [this means] "not white" or "not bound" <note>original: "asitā"</note>, one whose nature is eternally liberated. In **Your worship**, meaning in the "internal rite" <term>antaryāga: the practice of mental or meditative worship within one's own consciousness</term>, in the mental worship—**flesh of a cat and so on including hair and bone**—this means the **cat-like animals which are the six internal enemies** <gloss>2</gloss> [vices] equipped with all their limbs. Here, the animals are to be understood through their corresponding qualities: the **goat** is lust; the **buffalo** is anger; the **cat** is greed; the **human** is pride; the **sheep** is delusion; and the **camel** is envy. To those **bestowing the offering** <gloss>3</gloss> [means] those who, with the desire to destroy lust and the other vices, offer them as ritual gifts into You, who are the embodiment of Consciousness. To the **virtuous**, those people of saintly character who exist among mortals. **At every step**, meaning in successive order, **unprecedented** [and] most excellent **all attainment**—the **five-fold liberation** <gloss>4</gloss>—**arises** [and] is produced in abundance. This means such practitioners attain the liberations of "residing in the same divine realm" and so on. || 18 ||

->The self-controlled practitioner recites the mantra a hundred thousand times,<-
->devoted to eating pure offerings during the day, O Mother,<-
->expert in meditating on the pair of Your lotus feet.<-
->But at night, naked, through the joy of ritual union, he should recite<-
->the mantra a hundred thousand times; he becomes equal to Shiva on the surface of the earth. || 20 ||<-

## Commentary

**O Mother**, that **person** who is **self-controlled**, meaning one who has conquered their senses. **Devoted to eating pure offerings** <gloss>5</gloss> [means] being one who eats sacrificial food <term>haviṣya: pure, simple food suitable for an ascetic or ritualist</term> after completing the recitation. **During the day**, meaning in the daylight hours, being **expert in meditating on the pair of Your feet**, in the contemplation of Your two lotus feet...

---

1. <note>original: "ṣij bandhane"</note> The root *ṣij* refers to binding; with the suffix *kta*, it becomes *sita* (bound). The *Amara-kosha* dictionary states: "Bound and Arjuna are both called *sita*."
2. One should worship by giving the "goat-riding" lust and anger as a ritual offering. — From the *Annadā-kalpa*.
3. "I ever offer the functions of my senses as a sacrifice into the fire of the Self, which is ignited by the clarified butter of merit and demerit, using the mind as a sacrificial ladle through the path of the central channel (suṣumṇā). Hail! To the fire of the Absolute." — From the *Brahmānala*.
4. "Know, O Rama, that liberation is five-fold: residing in the same realm (sālokya), having the same form (sārūpya), having the same divine power (sārṣṭi), intimate union (sāyujya), and absolute oneness (kaivalya)." — From the *Shri Shiva Gita*.
5. "He who, devoted to eating pure offerings during the day, recites while remembering the Goddess, and at night is intent on ritual union—by reciting a hundred thousand times, he becomes a lord of the earth."

<summary>This page explains the symbolic sacrifice of "internal enemies" (vices like lust and greed) represented by various animals to attain five-fold liberation. It also introduces the specific daily and nightly disciplines (eating pure food and ritual meditation) required for a practitioner to attain Shiva-like status.</summary>
<keywords>internal sacrifice, six enemies, five types of liberation, self-control, pure food (havisya), internal worship, Asita, mantra recitation, ritual union</keywords>
